# Design decisions log (2026-10-29)

These came from a grilling session in which each question was asked with a recommended answer. The lasting, hard-to-reverse ones are also ADRs. Numbers are given as questions are asked; a skipped number was never used.

| # | Question | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| — | Is this worth building? | Yes as a team or personal tool. XPipe is the closest rival. The value is roster's model (team Inventory in git, per-service Logins, review gate, secrets kept from the UI), not "another SSH client". See [vision.md](vision.md). |
| Q1 | Who is it for? | The team eventually. It starts as a personal project and may be open-sourced or sold later. |
| Q2 | Platform | Cross-platform, anywhere with a display (Windows, X11, Wayland, WSLg). **Tauri.** |
| Q3 | v1 scope | Host finding, terminal tabs with automatic login, Host details, opening web services. |
| Q4 | Inventory writes | **Yes**, the app writes to the Inventory (it was read-only at first, then changed). |
| Q5 | Secrets | Never shown in the GUI. The app must really unlock the vault (Bitwarden or whatever provider the Inventory uses). |
| Q6 | Terms | **Host** is the saved entry. **Session** is one live terminal connection. |
| Q7 | Repo | A separate repo, **local only** while experimenting. *Superseded by Q56: the app now lives in the roster repo's `app/`.* |
| Q8 | "Native" | Native window chrome, shortcuts, fonts and light/dark. Our own design inside. SecureCRT is a skeleton, not a style guide. |
| Q9 | Frontend | React + Vite + **TypeScript (required)** + xterm.js (WebGL). Get it right on Windows first, then the Linux VM. |
| Q10 | API | `roster serve`, JSON-RPC over stdio, returning roster's **existing objects** (roster ADR 0001). |
| Q11 | Session transport | System `ssh` in a PTY, with passwords through SSH_ASKPASS (roster ADR 0002). |
| Q12 | App lock | Unlocking the vault is the app lock. Locks after 15 minutes idle (configurable). |
| Q13 | Write flow | Edit through roster, keeping comments, then diff, then **Publish** (commit and push to infra `main`, pulling first). *Ticket 12: Publish commits `roster.yml` alone (never `folders.yml` or any other file; other uncommitted files refuse it) with the infra repo's own git identity, and pushes to the branch's upstream, never forced. "Pulling first" is a fetch, then a fast-forward only over upstream commits that leave `roster.yml` alone; if `roster.yml` moved there, or the push is rejected, it stops with the Pending changes kept. `roster sync` runs after.* *Q88 (owner): forms no longer pull on opening; they pull in the background when the last pull is older than `pull_fresh_minutes`, and the save waits for it.* |
| Q14 | Backends | All three: native Windows, the Windows app with roster in WSL, and Linux. Auto-detected per machine. |
| Q15 | Name and location | `roster-desk` in `~/work/roster-desk`. *Superseded by Q56: it's now the roster app, in `app/` of the roster repo.* |
| — | Keyboard | Vim keys, mostly keyboard, mouse fully supported. |
| Q16 | Backend setting | Local or WSL: <distro>. The Windows default is auto-detected, and the Config can override it (`backend =`). Originally a Settings screen option, then replaced by Q24. |
| Q17 | Layout | Terminal first: the Picker overlay is the main way to find Hosts, side panels are closed by default, and there's one thin tab strip. |
| Q18 | Leader | Ctrl+Space, configurable. |
| Q19 | Vim scope | Vim keys in the app everywhere except the terminal. Can be turned off. Form fields stay ordinary inputs. |
| Q20 | Quick Connect | "Save to vault" saves only after the login succeeds. It creates the Vault item `<host> ssh` and the Host (reviewed, not enrolled) as a Pending change. The same checkbox appears for `auth: prompt` Hosts. |
| Q21 | Vault writes | Bitwarden only. Other providers come later, when needed. |
| Q23 | Config language | **Lua**: `init.lua` in the user config dir, run with mlua (Lua 5.4 built in). See [ADR 0004](../adr/0004-lua-config-file-only.md). |
| Q24 | Settings screen | **None. The config file is the only place to change settings**, as in WezTerm and Neovim. Remembered things like window size and recent Hosts are State, not Config. |
| Q25 | When | **Iteration 2**, with the design pass (ticket 21). In iteration 1 the keymap and options are built as data tables with defaults, ready for the Config to override. |
| Q26 | Window chrome | **A custom title bar like Slack's** (frameless window; our own bar with app icon, tabs, the Picker search, and minimize, maximize and close). It replaces Q8's "native title bar". The rest of Q8 stays: platform shortcuts, system font and OS light/dark. It lands in iteration 2's design pass (ticket 09). |
| Q27 | Visual direction | **Linear is the main reference**: lean, dense and quiet, with a muted palette and one accent, thin 1px borders, small type, fast and subtle motion, and keyboard-first with a command palette. Tabby and Warp are secondary references. |
| Q28 | Logo | Five concepts are prototyped on branch `prototype/logos` (`npm run proto:logos`). The user picks one or more to refine. |
| Q29 | Logo + accent | **D1 "Network R"** (seven nodes joined into an R, with the accent node at the end of the leg) and **jade `#1f9d68`** as the default accent. Prototype: branch `prototype/logos`. *Superseded by Q57: the logo is Gap orbit (plain) and the accent Apricot flare.* |
| Q30 | Fonts | **Inter** (Linear's UI font) for the UI. **Iosevka** for the terminal and the wordmark. Both are defaults the Config can change. |
| Q31 | Active item | The **raised** style: a slightly lighter fill plus a 1px outline. No underlines. |
| Q32 | Layout | **Title bar 3, extended:** the bar holds only the mark and minimize, maximize and close. **Sessions, personal Folders and the Inventory tree live in a left sidebar**, like SecureCRT's Session Manager or an editor's file tree, in place of a tab strip. The status bar can be hidden. A **Key bindings panel** (`␣ ?`) lists the resolved keymap and where each binding comes from. Prototype: `prototype-shell.html` (A tree / B explorer). This replaces Q17's tab strip, Q26's tabs in the bar, and "side panels closed by default". The Picker stays on `␣ f`. |
| Q33 | Modularity | **Everything above is only a default.** The Config (`init.lua`) can change every color token (themes are sets of tokens; several ship), the fonts, the keymap, and the layout pieces: sidebar on/off and side, Sessions as a sidebar or as tabs, status bar on/off, what the title bar shows. The app behaves like an editor you configure. |
| Q34 | Folders | **Personal:** kept in this Box's State (drag, or `␣ a`) and definable in `init.lua`. They're never written to `roster.yml`. Teammates see only the Inventory's groups. |
| Q35 | When | The **sidebar is built in iteration 1**: ticket 07 builds Sessions in the sidebar instead of a tab strip, and ticket 17 (Inventory tree and Folders) moves to iteration 1. Colors, fonts, the title bar and polish stay in iteration 2 (09). Themes and layout options land in 21. |
| Q36 | View modes | **Two modes** (simplified from three on 2026-10-29, so both can be done really well), toggled with `␣ m` or F11. `init.lua` picks the starting one.
1. **Standard**, the everyday layout: Sidebar tree A, title bar, optional status bar.
2. **Focus**: true OS fullscreen with no chrome at all. You get around like in Neovim: `␣ b` fuzzy-finds open Sessions (Telescope's buffer list), `␣ f` fuzzy-finds Hosts, and `␣ ]` / `␣ [` cycle Sessions.

**Every mode keeps all of roster:** the Picker, `:` command line, Quick Connect, Host details, the Key bindings panel, Lock, Inventory edits and Publish all work in every mode. Modes change how things are shown, never what you can do. What the Sidebar shows in Standard is reached through overlays and finders in Tmux and Focus. Prototype: `prototype-shell.html?mode=standard|focus`. Focus can optionally show the thin tmux-style session bar (`[box] 0:esxi-02* 1:…`): off by default, turned on with `focus.status_line = true` in `init.lua`. The Tmux mode, Compact rail and Mini window ideas were dropped. Split panes are still out of scope. *Q65 (issue #54): `␣ e` shows the Sidebar in Focus too, over the terminal; the tree finder is `␣ t`.* |
| Q37 | Your terminal config wins | **The terminal never fights the user's own setup.** Remote `.bashrc`, prompt, `LS_COLORS`, vim colour schemes and truecolor themes must render exactly as they do in any good terminal. The app sets `TERM=xterm-256color` and `COLORTERM=truecolor`, injects nothing else (no PS1, no sourced files, no output rewriting), and passes every escape sequence through. The 16-colour ANSI palette defaults to a neutral, standard one rather than the app accent. Themes and `init.lua` can change it, and a theme can say "leave the ANSI palette alone". |
| Q38 | Plugins and packages | **Neovim-style extensibility:**
- **Plugins** are Lua, run in the same runtime as the Config. They add commands, keys, themes, Sidebar sections, finder sources, status segments and event hooks.
- **Packages** are Plugins from git repos, listed in `init.lua` and pinned in `roster.lock` (lazy.nvim-style).
- The built-in features use the same registries.
- Plugins never see secrets, and their Inventory writes only become Pending changes.
- No plugin HTML/JS in v1.

See [ADR 0006](../adr/0006-lua-plugins-and-packages.md). Tickets 23–24, iteration 7. |
| Q39 | Per-mode layout | **Each view mode has its own layout table** in `init.lua`: which pieces show, where they go, and in what order. The pieces are the title bar, Sidebar (and its sections), session bar, status bar and its segments, and finders. For example, Focus can show the session bar at the bottom, and Standard can hide the status bar. Plugins can add pieces and segments to any mode. The defaults are the two modes as prototyped. |
| Q40 | No built-in vault | **The roster app will never have its own secret store.** Secrets stay in the user's password manager. The roster CLI and app only hold Login *references*, and never learn or keep a password beyond one lookup. Building a vault would mean owning encryption, recovery, sync, sharing and breach liability, and it would turn the product into the thing (Termius's vault) we set out to differ from. |
| Q41 | Local-only option | People without a team password manager get **local providers** instead of a vault: **KeePassXC** (`keepassxc-cli`, a local `.kdbx` file) is added, next to the existing `pass`. Both can read Logins and, like Bitwarden in ticket 13, create Vault items for "Save to vault". Ticket 25. |
| Q42 | Syncing your setup | `init.lua`, `roster.lock`, themes and personal Folders are plain files that sync through **git (dotfiles)**, the way the Inventory does. There's no vendor cloud. A `:config sync` helper that pulls and pushes that repo is a later proposal, not planned yet. |
| Q43 | Verified adds only | **A Host with a Login enters the Inventory, and a password enters the vault, only after a test login just succeeded with exactly those credentials.** That means the address was reachable, the host key was confirmed, and the user and password (or key) were accepted. This applies to the add-Host form (ticket 11), Quick Connect "Save to vault" (15), filling in `auth: prompt` Hosts (16), and changing a Host's Login in an edit (10). The order is always: verify, then create the Vault item, then add the Pending change. If the second step fails, the first is undone, so a failure leaves neither a stray Vault item nor a Pending change. Hosts *without* a Login (from `discover`) still enter as `reviewed: false` through the review gate. |
| Q44 | Clean errors everywhere | Every way a connection can fail, including entries made by hand in `roster.yml` or the vault, ends in a clean error with the Host, plain words, where the problem lives, the fix, and the raw message under Details. The catalog is `docs/app/errors.md`. `roster check HOST` / `:check HOST` run the diagnostics. A changed host key is never accepted automatically. Ticket 26, iteration 1. |
| Q45 | Live, two-way Inventory | Adding, removing and renaming **Hosts and groups** happens right in the Sidebar: `+` on a group heading, a row menu, `␣` keys and drag between groups. Each change writes `roster.yml` (comments kept) as a Pending change that you Publish (Q13). **Edits to `roster.yml` made outside the app show up in the Sidebar within a second**: `roster serve` watches the file, reloads it, and notifies the app. A file that doesn't load keeps the last good Inventory on screen with a clean error naming the line. Adding a Host with a Login still requires a successful test login first (Q43). Removing a Host asks for confirmation and never touches the vault. Tickets 28 and 10–12. *Reloading became Neovim-style (Q58, owner 2026-10-30): outside edits apply on `:source`, or within a second with `auto_reload = true`.* |
| Q46 | Folders in a file | Folders move from the State file into **`folders.yml`** next to `init.lua`: plain data that both you and the app edit. The app writes it on every Folder change, and edits made outside the app show up live. The app never rewrites `init.lua`, because that file is code. Folders that `init.lua` defines are shown marked "from init.lua" and can only be changed there. This supersedes the storage part of Q34; Folders remain personal and never go into the Inventory. |
| Q47 | Worst-case UI | Every screen is stress-tested with worst-case data using the `break-ui` skill: long and unbreakable names, 500+ Hosts, empty states, unicode, huge counts and narrow windows. The fixes ship with the design pass. Ticket 27, iteration 2. |
| Q48 | First Release | *Amended by Q70: `v0.1.0` after iteration 4.* **No Release until iteration 5 is done.** Iterations 1–5 build up on `dev`, each tested by hand before the next one starts. After iteration 5, `dev` → `main` is tagged `v0.1.0`. After iteration 7 (Platforms + Plugins), it's tagged `v0.2.0`. |
| Q49 | Terminal font weight | The terminal defaults to **Iosevka Medium (500)**, with bold at 700. It's configurable in `init.lua` (`terminal.weight`, `terminal.bold_weight`). The UI stays Inter 400/500/600. Delivered with ticket 21. |
| Q50 | Dark background | **Roster dark is a neutral near-black like Linear's, with no blue tint:** bg `#08090a`, chrome and Sidebar `#0f1011`, raised `#191a1b`, lines `#1c1d1f`. The terminal background matches the bg. These values come from third-party Linear design references, not an official source. *The values are superseded by Q57: Roster dark is now Ink `#0f1011` (bg and terminal) with chrome and Sidebar `#141617`; still neutral, still no blue tint.* |
| Q51 | Bundled themes | Besides Roster dark and light, ship complete **Gruvbox Dark, Nord, Catppuccin Mocha and Tokyo Night** themes from their official palettes, each with its own background, surfaces, accent and ANSI colors. A `:theme` finder previews them live. A choice made there is kept in State unless `init.lua` sets `theme`, which wins. Ticket 30, iteration 2. Accent text is dark on jade in Roster dark; Roster light uses `#17845a` for contrast. *The Roster colours are superseded by Q57 (apricot accent with Ink text in dark, `#9b411a` with white in light); every bundled Theme also gets a second tone.* *Which one wins is amended by Q69: a `:theme` choice applies and stays, over `init.lua`'s `theme` too, until that value changes.* |
| Q52 | Sign-in with 2FA | The lock screen also handles **full sign-in**: server, email, master password, every two-step method the provider's CLI supports (for Bitwarden: authenticator, email, YubiKey OTP, new-device code, API key, SSO), and a clean message for the methods it can't do. The flow runs through a step-by-step `vault.login` in `roster serve`. Secrets never go in argv or logs. Ticket 31, iteration 2, with a research step first. |
| Q53 | Interface review picks (ticket 29) | **All 26 findings are fixed.** On the flagged ones: **M1 option B**: `faint` is for icons and rules only; all real text (status bar, labels, addresses, hints, Sidebar headings) uses `muted`, hierarchy comes from size and weight, and tests hold every text token (`text`, `muted`, `warn`, `error`, `accent_ink`) at 4.5:1 on every surface and keep `--faint` out of text rules. **S11**: the raised fill stays (Q31), and the on-screen Session's row adds a **2px jade bar** at its start while the Session is live (3:1 against the Sidebar in every Theme); the App-mode cursor stays distinct (raised, accent outline, no bar). **M3**: state dots are nodes with **distinct shapes** (filled = key, ring = password, dashed ring = unreviewed, hollow grey = ended), and every row says its state to assistive tech ("esxi-02, password login, …"); the "unreviewed" badge keeps ticket 27's show-on-hover/cursor rule. **N6**: no text below the 11 px step (overrides Q27's 10–10.5 px keycaps and badges). **N7**: the tmux marks stay (Q36) with hidden words and a legend. See `docs/app/design/interface-review.md`. *Colours updated by Q57: the bar is the accent (apricot), and the dashed unreviewed ring is drawn in the second tone; the shapes are unchanged.* |
| Q54 | roster's own taste | `docs/app/design/taste.md` defines the identity: the network-map motif (nodes and edges, the D1 graph), jade only for what's live, Iosevka for data and Inter for talk, keycaps as onboarding, an honest plain voice, calm errors, and motion that confirms (an edge drawn on connect). **Configurable like Neovim or VS Code is part of the identity**: code not a settings screen, everything a registry entry, every source shown, changing it one step away (`:config`), Plugins that look native, and safety lines that don't move. Linear is the discipline we borrow, not the identity. The interface-review fixes and every later UI ticket follow it. *Q57 updates the motif and colour: the Gap orbit mark, and "the accent means live" (apricot, not jade).* |
| Q55 | Terminal like Windows Terminal | The terminal should look like the user's own terminal by default: prefer the installed fonts (Nerd Fonts) over the bundled Iosevka, accept sizes in points, a `dom` renderer option for crisper ClearType-like text, line height 1.2, and `:config import-terminal` to copy font, size and colors from Windows Terminal. Ticket 33, iteration 3. |
| Q56 | One repo, app in `app/` | **The desktop app joins the roster repo (2026-10-30) and is called the roster app.** The Python CLI stays at the root; the app goes into `app/` (frontend and `app/src-tauri/`), the Rust core into `crates/roster-core/` (was `desk-core`), with the Cargo workspace at the root. Docs move to `docs/app/`, the app's ADRs are renumbered after roster's (0003–0006), tickets go to `tickets/`. Both histories are kept. Names: product "roster"; window "Roster", binary `roster-app`, identifier `dev.roster.app`, config dir `roster`; the old Config and State are copied over once, and the `ROSTER_DESK_*` env vars still work as fallbacks for `ROSTER_APP_*`. App changes go through GitHub pull requests to `dev`, like the CLI, and CI moves to GitHub Actions. Supersedes Q7 and Q15. See [ADR 0007](../adr/0007-one-repo-app-in-app-dir.md). |
| Q57 | Final visual identity (round 5, issue #44) | **Palette "Apricot flare" on Ink and Paper, logo "Gap orbit" in the plain treatment.** Accent `#FF9A70` (dark) / `#9B411A` (light); **Ink `#0F1011`** is Roster dark's background and **Paper `#E8E8EA`** Roster light's chrome and Sidebar (Roster dark's text). Second tone **Sea glass** `#82CCD5` / `#296D82` (`tone` token) marks this Box and unreviewed Hosts: shapes only, never text or anything actionable, 3:1 on every surface; the other bundled Themes take a tone from their own palette. Semantics move off the accent: warn yellow `#E9C944` / `#5D580C`, error raspberry `#E25A98` / `#A11D6C`, danger `#B9205C` with white text (the old amber warn was ΔE 9.1 / 7.7 from the apricot, the old red error 13.3 / 8.5). The mark: the R inside its orbit, with the orbit broken where the Host sits; the leg reaches that Host, the one accent node, and the Box is the square at the foot of the stem. Plain: no live edge and no duotone in the mark. The orbit (radius 28 of 64) fills the tile and the R sits clear of it on every side; at 24 px and under the R is stem, bowl and leg, smaller, with a wider gap and the Host at 45° so it stays inside the tile, so it never reads as ®. **Why:** the round-4 coral `#F07A5A` was ΔE 4.3 from Claude's clay; Apricot flare is ΔE 11.0 (dark) / 9.4 (light) from Anthropic's clay and its other warm brand colours, 9.9 from GitLab and 8.7 from Firefox, and much further in light (22–23); Sea glass is 10.1 from Termius's teal and 9.2 (light) from XPipe's (OKLab ΔE ×100, round-5 prototype). With it, the UI text default rises from 13 to 14 px, and `fonts.ui_size` in `init.lua` sets it (the type scale, rows and Sidebar width follow; nothing under 11 px). Supersedes Q29 and the colour values of Q50, Q51 and Q53. See [themes.md](themes.md) and [design/taste.md](design/taste.md). *The app icon's tile and the radius-28 orbit are superseded by Q59: no tile, the R fills an orbit of radius 26. The 14 px default is superseded by Q61: 15 px.* |
| Q58 | Reloading the Config | **Neovim-style (owner, 2026-10-30):** saving `init.lua` doesn't apply it; `:source` (`:so`, `␣ S`, also `:config reload`) reloads it now, and `:source PATH` reloads only the file that owns PATH. `auto_reload = true` turns reload-on-save back on (off by default). A save that isn't applied shows a quiet "init.lua changed" hint in the status bar. Errors work as before: file and line, last good Config kept. What `:source` reloads is a registry, so ticket 28 adds `folders.yml` and `roster.yml` to it. Changes ticket 21's live reload; ticket 32. |
| Q59 | App icon without a tile; the R fills its orbit again | **The app icon is the Gap orbit on a transparent background (owner, 2026-10-30): no Ink tile and no outline, at every size, 16 px to the 256 px Start menu and installer icon.** The owner didn't want the dark box on the Windows taskbar. A transparent icon can't know which taskbar it sits on, so the R, its orbit and the Box are a mid-tone neutral `#808183` (4.18:1 on the dark taskbar `#202020`, 3.51:1 on the light `#f3f3f3`) and the Host a mid apricot `#cc5c2b` (3.98:1 / 3.69:1), solid with no fades; Paper, Ink and both Theme accents each fail one taskbar. The 256 px icon goes transparent too, for one consistent mark (it reads at that size). Masters `app/assets/logo-icon.svg` and `logo-icon-small.svg` (the small cut for 16–32 px); `icon.ico` holds 16, 20, 24, 32 (small cut), 40, 48, 64, 256. **The proportions go back toward the round-5 prototype:** Q57's later fix grew the orbit to radius 28 and shrank the R, leaving a big orbit round a small R; the orbit is back to radius 26 with the prototype's R and Host ring, the R moved up and right so it clears the orbit by about 1 px at 16 px (it touched there in the prototype), in the app's marks and the masters alike. The in-app marks keep the Theme's `text` and `accent`. Supersedes Q57's tile and its radius-28 orbit. See [themes.md](themes.md) and [design/taste.md](design/taste.md). *The small cut is redrawn (owner, 2026-10-30, issue #54): it was a plain R (stem, bowl, leg), so on the taskbar only the orbit and the Host showed and roster's network R was lost. It's now the network R with fewer, larger nodes (the stem's top and the bowl's tip), the Box square and the Host ring, drawn on each size's own pixel grid: `logo-icon-small-16.svg`, `-20.svg`, `-24.svg`, and `logo-icon-small.svg` for 32 px, each in `icon.ico` at its size. Same colours, transparent background.* |
| Q60 | Unknown host keys in Sessions (issue #44) | **Ask once, in the app (owner, 2026-10-30).** A Session to a Host whose host key roster hasn't recorded no longer trusts it silently (`accept-new`). The app first shows the Host, its address, and the key's type and SHA256 fingerprint, in plain words ("roster hasn't seen this Host's key before"), and asks whether to trust it. Trusting it records the key in roster's known hosts and connects; declining connects nothing and records nothing. A key that differs from the recorded one is still refused, never asked about (Q44). Verified adds and edits already record the key (Q43; review fix for address changes), so the question only comes up for Hosts that reached `roster.yml` another way (hand edits, a teammate's Publish) before `roster setup`/`sync`. Rejected: keep `accept-new` (trusts the first key without telling you) and strict refusal until `setup`/`sync` (safe but blocks a new Host a teammate just added). The CLI's own `roster ssh` is unchanged. Ticket 36, iteration 4. |
| Q61 | Bigger default text | **The defaults go up a step (owner, 2026-10-30): the UI's `fonts.ui_size` from 14 to 15 px and the terminal's `terminal.size` from `"12pt"` to `"13pt"` (17.33 px).** The owner found both a little small. The type scale, the row height (30 px) and the Sidebar's width (19 × the UI size) follow the UI size, and nothing goes under 11 px (Q53 N6). Both stay options in `init.lua`, so `ui_size = 14` and `size = "12pt"` bring back the old look; `:config import-terminal` still copies Windows Terminal's own size. Updates Q57's 14 px and ticket 33's 12 pt default. See [config.md](config.md). |
| Q62 | The Inventory is always private | **Every team's `roster.yml` stays private (owner, 2026-10-30): in the team's own private git repo or only on the Box, never in a public repo and never in roster's.** roster, the CLI and the app, is a generic project that knows nothing about any one team. Team-specific names stay in the Inventory and are mapped there, not in roster's code (for example, a team's own name for a web login type is mapped onto one of roster's built-in types by `web_login_names` in `roster.yml`). The app only reaches the Inventory through `roster serve` (ADR 0001), and nothing from it goes into fixtures, tests, docs or commits. |
| Q63 | Mesh: Host-to-Host access from one Box | **roster runs on one Box and sets up Host-to-Host access itself (owner, 2026-10-30): no roster, vault or password on any other machine, nothing to install or update there.** *Inventory:* a group's `mesh: true` lets its Hosts reach each other both ways; it cascades (`defaults` → group → Host), so `mesh: true` in `defaults` meshes every group, including new ones from `discover`; `mesh: false` opts out; unreviewed Hosts never join. Top-level `links:` add one-way reach across groups and networks, Host or group to Host or group (`{ from: subnet-1, to: subnet-42 }`, `both: true` for two-way). Each Host logs in to a peer as **that peer's** configured user. *How:* `roster mesh` (plan with `--dry-run`, then apply; `roster setup` runs it for meshed groups) makes a key **on** each source Host (the private key never leaves it), installs it on its peers tagged `roster-mesh:<host>` with `from="<source IP>"` and no agent or X11 forwarding, copies the peers' recorded host keys into the source's `known_hosts` (strict checking), and writes an ssh config so `ssh <peer>` works and a `ROSTER.md` listing only that Host's peers for Claude there (imported from `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` when Claude is installed). It never runs on its own: after a Publish, `sync` and the app say "mesh out of date" with a one-key apply. Apply goes one connection at a time (rate limiters). *Edges:* Hosts that can't take a key (`enroll: false`, appliances) stay in the mesh as password peers: ssh asks the person for the password, roster never copies a password to a Host, and `ROSTER.md` tells Claude to ask the user to log in. ESXi works both ways, with the key and config kept somewhere that survives a reboot, and `roster check` notices if they're lost. Links across networks are tested from the source ("orion-2 can't reach …:22") rather than assumed; a `via:` hop is later. Removing a Host, a link or `mesh: true` removes exactly its tagged entries on the next apply; down Hosts are kept as "pending" and finished when they're back; `roster check` reports drift; `roster mesh --rotate HOST` replaces a key. **Cut:** `roster mesh cut HOST` (one key in the app) removes HOST's key from every peer at once, in parallel, and every peer from HOST, without waiting for a Publish; the Box's own access stays so you can investigate (`roster revoke` is separate); it writes `mesh: false` for HOST as a Pending change so the cut survives the next Publish; it is quick but complete: every peer is confirmed, and any peer that's down or not answering gets a loud warning ("cut pending on 2 Hosts") until it's done when they're back. *Many Boxes:* any Box can apply; the mesh is derived from `roster.yml` only and keys are tagged per Host, so applying twice changes nothing and says "already in the mesh". *Visible risk:* the app shows each Host's reach ("orion-2 can reach 7 Hosts") and draws Links on the map; mesh and links changes show in Publish's diff. *Not a VPN:* Tailscale and other VPNs make the network path (and need an agent on every machine); the mesh manages who may log in where over the network you already have, and the two combine. T39 (#51), iteration 6. A **Topology** view (T40, #52) draws the Box, Hosts by group, Mesh reach and Links. |
| Q64 | Tickets live in GitHub issues | **Since the move to GitHub, tickets are GitHub issues only (owner, 2026-10-30):** one issue per ticket (`TNN: …`), one milestone per iteration, "Blocked by" naming issues, open or closed as the status. The `tickets/` folder is gone (its history stays in git, and every open ticket's full text moved into its issue), so filing or changing a ticket never needs a file or a pull request. Decisions stay in this file. |
| Q65 | Visible controls for common commands (issue #54) | **Every common command has a visible control by default, not only `:` or a key (owner, 2026-10-30).** A **toolbar** sits in the title bar: Find, Add Host, Changes (with the Pending changes count) and Publish (only while there are Pending changes), by the window's buttons. Each control is an entry in a toolbar registry (ADR 0006) that runs a command line from the command table, and the toolbar is a piece of each mode's layout table (Q39): `modes.<mode>.toolbar = { "add", "find" }` picks and orders the controls, `false` drops the toolbar; the ids are `sidebar`, `find`, `add`, `changes`, `publish`, and `cmdline`, `keys`, `lock`, `mode`, which are registered but not shown by default; an unknown id is a warning. Standard shows it; Focus doesn't (no chrome), and with `modes.focus.toolbar = true` it's a thin bar of its own at the top. Add Host is at the top now as well as the `+` on the Inventory and group headings, which stay. **The Sidebar** gets a visible toggle (the toolbar's `sidebar` control, at the left of the title bar, pressed while it shows) next to `␣ e` (`sidebar`, rebindable); it works in Focus too, where the Sidebar shows over the terminal, so the tree finder moves to `␣ t` (`finder tree`). What it chose is kept in the State per mode, unless `init.lua` sets `modes.<mode>.sidebar`, which wins (the core says which pieces the file set). The 16–32 px icon is the network R again (see Q59's note). Extends Q32's bare title bar and Q39's layout table. *Q88 (owner): a Sidebar row's buttons show on the selected row only, never on hover.* |
| Q66 | `:help` inside the app (ticket 37, issue #49) | **Neovim-style docs in a side panel (owner request, 2026-10-30).** `:help [TOPIC]` and the topic finder on **`␣ ?`**, as the owner asked; the Key bindings panel moves to **`␣ K`** (`:keys` is unchanged, and `keys = { leader = { ["?"] = "keys" } }` brings the old key back). Pages are a registry (ADR 0006) so Plugins add their own as data, never HTML. The pages listing commands, keys, options, Themes, finders and errors are generated from the live registries and the Config each time they show; each option says its value and where it came from (`default`, `init.lua:LINE`, or the State for `:theme` / `:hints`), from a list of what the file set that the core now sends (`Config.set`). The guides are `docs/app`'s Markdown bundled at build time (`?raw`), the only copy; a test fails when config.md's option tables and the core's schema disagree. Help's key tables always show their keys, whatever `ui.hints` says. Opened from a key, so it doesn't animate. See [help.md](help.md). |
| Q67 | Quick Connect's host keys and password (ticket 14, #14) | **A Quick Connect's host key is trusted for that one Session, never recorded in roster's known hosts; its password goes through askpass like a vault password (2026-10-30).** The tab shows the key `host.scan_key {address, port}` fetched, with a masked password field; `quick.prepare` fetches it again and goes on only for exactly that fingerprint (23 otherwise), and refuses a key that differs from one roster recorded for the address (43, Q44). serve writes the confirmed key to its own known-hosts file under a one-off `HostKeyAlias` (gone when serve exits) and ssh checks it strictly, so the key ssh meets is the one the user saw, and one Quick Connect's key is never trusted for another. **Why not roster's known hosts:** they hold the keys roster trusts for the Inventory; a one-off box would then be trusted for any Host added later at that address without a verified add (Q43). Ticket 15's "Save to vault" records it once the login succeeded. The password field may be left empty: the Session then logs in with this Box's own keys. Reached from the Picker (a `user@address[:port]` query is offered first), `:connect user@address` (a Host's name still wins), `:quick`, and a `quick` toolbar control (Q65), all only with a roster that has `quick.prepare` (API 12). No Inventory write. See [security.md](security.md) and [errors.md](errors.md) rows 64-67. |
| Q68 | "Save to vault" after a Session's own login (tickets 15, 16; #15, #16) | **serve holds a typed password only when "Save to vault" is ticked before connecting, and saves it only once that Session logged in with it (2026-10-30).** Q43 asks for a login that just succeeded with exactly those credentials; for a Quick Connect or an `auth: prompt` Host that's the Session's own login, so nothing logs in twice (rate-limited networks such as 10.19.x). ssh doesn't tell its terminal when it has logged in, so serve gives it a `LocalCommand` (its logged-in script), which OpenSSH runs only after authentication; it reports the Session's askpass token, and serve counts it only for the token that handed that password to ssh. The app gets the `session.logged_in` notification, offers the save in the pane's corner (never taking the terminal's focus), and the save names the id: the password never crosses back. Held in serve's memory only, until one save, `vault.forget_typed` (the Session ended, or Not now), `vault.lock`, or 10 minutes. The order is Q43's: the name and group checked, the Vault item, the Host (the item taken back out if the Host can't be written), then the host key confirmed for the Session recorded for it (Q67), never a fresh scan. The Host's name starts as what its shell calls itself (the window title or prompt after the login), which the user can change. **Rejected:** a second test login at save time (another connection to a rate-limited network, and it would prove a different login); the app judging the login from the terminal's output (the Host prints whatever it likes); keeping the password in the app until the save (secrets never reach the frontend). API 13. See [security.md](security.md) and [errors.md](errors.md) rows 68-70. |
| Q69 | `:theme` over `init.lua`'s `theme` (issue #56) | **Neovim `:colorscheme` style (owner, 2026-10-30):** choosing a Theme (`:theme NAME`, or Enter in the themes finder) always applies it at once and keeps it in the State, even when `init.lua` sets `theme`. The State then also keeps the value `init.lua` had (`themeOver`), and the choice wins at start-up and on `:source` for as long as `init.lua` still names that same Theme; once the file's `theme` changes to anything else, the file wins again. When a choice is made over a pinned value, a status message says so once (`init.lua:12 also sets theme = "nord": this choice stays until that line changes. Delete the line to unpin it.`), and the finder's note says the same instead of "This only previews". `:help options` says `State (:theme, over init.lua:12)`. `:config import-terminal` defines the Windows Terminal Theme in `init.lua` but no longer writes `roster.set("theme", …)`: it chooses that Theme as `:theme` does, so `:theme` can still change it; the preview is still exactly what's written. **Why not "the State always wins":** editing `init.lua`'s `theme` and running `:source` would then do nothing, which breaks Q58's promise that the file applies on `:source`. **Why not "init.lua wins on restart and `:source`":** the pick wouldn't stay, the bug the owner reported (an imported `theme` line undid every `:theme`). Tracking the value is one more State key and keeps both promises: the last thing you did, a pick or an edit to that line, is what shows. Amends Q51. |
| Q70 | First Release moves up | **`v0.1.0` comes after iteration 4, not 5 (owner, 2026-10-30).** Iteration 4 (Quick Connect, Save to vault, KeePassXC, the host-key question, `:help`, the resource audit) is tested by hand, then `dev` → `main` is tagged `v0.1.0` and the release workflow builds the draft Release, which the owner publishes ([releasing.md](../releasing.md)). `v0.2.0` still follows iteration 7. Amends Q48. |
| Q71 | Host details and web services (ticket 18, #18) | **Owner (2026-10-31): Host details is a side panel on the right, opposite the Sidebar, closed until asked for, so the tree and the details can show together;** with the Sidebar set to the right it goes to the left. In Focus it floats over the terminal on its side, as `␣ e` shows the Sidebar there. **Owner: the command is `:info HOST`;** `:info` alone still says what the app runs on (Backend, Box, Inventory, command, font), and Tab completes Host names after `:info `. The panel shows what `roster info HOST` shows, from `hosts.list`'s Host (it follows live Inventory changes; it closes when its Host is gone): title, description, type, product, group, `user@address:port`, access (key, password, or not set up), mac, the ssh Login, the default Login for services, the web UI, then each service's name, port, URL and Login (user and vault reference, e.g. `bw:esx-02 admin`: the Vault item's name, never a value), and the service entries roster left out with their fix. **Design calls (agent, for the owner's review):** (a) **visible controls (Q65):** an ⓘ on every Host row in the tree (and in Folders), an ⓘ on each Picker row (shown on the selected row only, never on hover: Q88) plus Ctrl+I in the Picker (`picker.info`; it closes the Picker), and a toolbar toggle `details` in the default set by the window's buttons, pressed while the panel shows; (b) **`:details`**, bound to `␣ i`, shows or hides the panel for the selected Host (the Sidebar's cursor, else the Session on screen), which is what the toolbar toggle runs; (c) **`:open` keeps `:open HOST`** and also takes `:open SERVICE` (a web service of the Host in the panel, else the selected Host) and `:open HOST SERVICE`; a Host's name wins over a service of the same name, so nothing that worked before changes. Tab completes the current Host's web services first, then Host names, then HOST's services; (d) **opening a URL:** only http and https, with a host, no spaces or control characters, and no user or password in it, checked in the frontend (`details.ts webUrl`) and again in the core before it's handed to the OS (`roster-core browser.rs`: ShellExecute's "open" on Windows, `xdg-open` or `open` elsewhere; no shell parses it). A password written into a URL in `roster.yml` is drawn as `•••` and never opened; (e) **no serve change:** `host.get` and `hosts.list` already return everything `roster info` prints (a test checks it), so `api_version` stays 13 and there's no feature gate; (f) **no motion:** like every side panel it opens from a key as often as a click (taste.md: motion confirms; a key confirms itself); (g) Esc in App mode and `:hidepanel` close it last, after Key bindings, the forms and `:check`. *Q88 (owner): (a)'s ⓘ on Sidebar rows shows on the selected row only, never on hover; T72 (owner, 2026-10-31): the Picker's ⓘ too.* |
| Q72 | Switching vault account (ticket 42, #68) | **Signing the vault CLI out is a vault action in roster, not a terminal chore (owner, 2026-10-31).** The lock screen names the account under the unlock form ("Signed in as you@example.com on bitwarden.com") with "Use a different account"; `:vault signout` does the same while unlocked. Both ask first ("Sign out of you@example.com on this Box? The CLI's local copy of the vault is removed; signing in again syncs it."; Enter signs out, Esc keeps it): on the lock screen the question takes the unlock form's place, unlocked it's the side panel `:host remove` uses. serve's `vault.logout` forgets everything `vault.lock` does **before** `bw logout` (API 14), and the app goes straight to the full sign-in form (Q52) with the email empty, since the point is another account. Already signed out (15) just shows the sign-in form. Open Sessions keep running behind the lock, as with any lock. Providers with no account (KeePassXC, `pass`, `env`, `cmd`) get no link, and the command says why (KeePassXC: change `vault.kp.database` in init.lua). One account at a time, as the CLI holds: switching, not several accounts at once. |
| Q73 | Finding your way in the docs (ticket 43, #69) | **Neovim's lexicon and flow, roster's own look (owner, 2026-10-31).** The look stays taste.md's (Inter for prose, Iosevka for anything you could type, keycaps for keys, the accent only for what's live); only the words and the flow follow Neovim's help. **The index** groups the pages as Neovim's `help.txt` does: Getting started, Make it yours, Reference, Extending (a page's `part`; a Plugin's page is listed under its source). Its first line says where to start (`start`, new: `docs/app/start.md`, which forks by where you come from: new, SecureCRT or PuTTY, Windows Terminal, Neovim or tmux) and offers a link to try (`help#links`, which teaches `Ctrl+o` back). **New pages:** `quickref` (generated: the commands you'll use most per area, with their keys now; the list is curated in `pages.ts`), `all` (generated: every page, command, option, error, Theme, finder, alias and key, A to Z, Neovim's `index.txt`), `recipes` (paste-able `init.lua` snippets) and `extending` ("How far roster bends": every registry, what `init.lua` does with it today, what Plugins will). **The CLI's docs are in `:help` too** (`cli`, `new-box`, `adding-hosts`, `serve-api`; `:help roster.yml`, `:help roster check`). **Navigation:** `Ctrl+]` follows the focused link (Enter still does); `g O` (and an Outline button) is the page's outline in a finder (Neovim's `gO`); `:helpgrep TEXT` searches the text of every page in a finder (words as typed, not fuzzy; headings first; the index and `all` left out since they only repeat summaries), and Enter opens the place with the page search set to the first word, so `n`/`N` go on; `:help TOPIC /TEXT` does the same by hand. An option named like a page is tagged `'keys'`, `'themes'`, as Neovim writes options. A code span in keymap notation (`Ctrl+o`, `␣`, `Esc`, `g O`) is drawn as keycaps, and `` `:help X` `` links to X. **Motion:** none. Help, its finders and link jumps all start from a key, many times a day, so nothing animates (emil-design-eng: never animate keyboard-initiated actions; Q66 already said so). **`:help` alone still opens the topic finder** (Q66), not the index. **Not now, filed:** the docs website and PDF (T46, #76: needs an ADR and the owner's approval), news (T47, #77), deprecations (T48, #78), a Box health check (T49, #79), `:tutor` (T55, #80), generated serve-api tables (T51, #81), help from the command line and App mode (T52, #82), a weekly external-link check (T53, #83), and three small accessibility follow-ups from the interface review (T54, #85). The man page is T45. |
| Q74 | The docs check themselves (ticket 43, #69) | **Like Neovim's `make lintdoc` and `linterrcodes`, on every PR (2026-10-31).** `app/src/help/lint.test.ts`: every link in every help page goes to a topic, or to a file (and heading) in the repo; no two topics share a name, ignoring case (they used to collide silently: the options `keys` and `themes` hid behind the pages); every alias goes to a topic. `tests/test_docs.py`: every relative link in `docs/`, the READMEs, CONTRIBUTING and GLOSSARY resolves to a file and GitHub heading; every `serve.METHODS` name and every error code in `errors.py` is in `docs/serve-api.md`; every `roster` subcommand (`cli.build_parser()`) has its row in `docs/cli-reference.md`. **Developer notes stay out of help without a second copy:** the guides keep their "(ticket 32)" and "(decision Q58)" for whoever reads them on GitHub, and help leaves out a parenthetical that only names tickets, decisions, ADRs or issues (and drops those from a mixed one); headings lose them in the Markdown itself, so in-app anchors stay GitHub's, and a test fails on one that would still show in help. A link to a file that isn't bundled shows as its text with its path on hover, rather than vanishing. `docs/README.md` is the index of every doc, grouped like `:help`'s, and the user docs end in "See also". Rejected: rewriting every page by hand to drop the notes (they're history the design docs need, and it would conflict with every open PR), and a `<!-- dev -->` block per section (a second convention to learn). |
| Q75 | The docs on the web and on paper (ticket 46, #76) | **Owner (2026-10-31): approved for iteration 6; ADR 0008 is for the owner's review.** A website on GitHub Pages and one PDF, built from the same Markdown, the same generated pages and **the help panel's own renderer** (`HelpDoc` through `react-dom/server`), never a static-site generator, so the site, the PDF and `:help` can't drift. The help page, keycap and mark styles live in their own sheets (`help/doc.css`, `ui/keys.css`, `chrome/logo.css`) that the app and the site share. `npm run docs` in `app/` (`app/scripts/gen-docs-site.ts`, run by Vite's own module runner) writes a page per help page, `tags.json` and `tag.html?t=TOPIC` (a stable address for every topic), with roster's look: Inter for prose, Iosevka for anything you could type, keycaps, the roster dark and light tokens following the OS, the Gap orbit mark on the start page only, and no motion. Each page's footer: generated from the commit, "Edit this page", and "Wrong or unclear? Report it" (a new issue labelled `needs-triage` and `documentation`, with the page and topic, no host data). The build fails on a dead link or a repeated id. `docs.yml` builds on every pull request (an artifact only) and deploys on a push to `main`; `--pdf` prints every page in index order with headless Chromium, attached to each Release as `roster-docs-X.Y.Z.pdf` ([releasing.md](../releasing.md)). Public repo: defaults and repo docs only, never a `roster.yml`. See [ADR 0008](../adr/0008-docs-website-and-pdf.md). |
| Q76 | `:tutor`, an interactive tutorial on a demo Inventory (ticket 55, #80) | **Owner (2026-10-31): approved as the ticket wrote it, with Neovim's `:Tutor` as the reference for flow and words (lessons, "try it" steps, checkmarks, a lesson index, resume where you left off); the look stays roster's own (taste.md).** As the ticket set it: `:tutor` opens a help page whose steps tick themselves from **app events**, not text edits; the Block model gains a step block (`{t: "step", id, text, done}`); it runs on a **demo Inventory**, the fake Backend the tests use, under an explicit "Demo Inventory" banner, so nobody practises on their real network; tutorials are a registry Plugins can add to. **Agent's calls, for the owner's review:** (a) **Switching to the demo and back:** `App` now holds two workspaces. Yours stays mounted, hidden (`hidden` and `inert`) and **suspended**: it keeps its Sessions running (output is held for hidden terminals as it always is), its vault state and idle lock, its Plugins and its State, but takes no keys and leaves the document's Theme and hints to the demo; leaving the demo unmounts the demo's workspace (its Sessions end, its State in memory goes) and yours puts its Theme back and takes the keys and focus again. The demo's workspace gets the fake Backend over six made-up Hosts (`demo-01` to `demo-06`, `10.0.0.11` up, every Login a key, no vault), loaded lazily when a tutorial starts, so it can't reach `roster serve`, a vault or a Host; a Platform whose State lives in memory (seeded with your Theme and hints so it looks like your app, never saved); and from the real Platform only the window controls, the clipboard, the font probe and `init.lua` (read, and opened in the editor by `:config`, since that's what the lesson teaches; `:config import-terminal` can't write from the demo). No browser (the Hosts' addresses are made up), no `folders.yml`, no fullscreen. The **only** thing the demo writes to the real State is the tutorial's progress (`tutor`: the step ids done, per tutorial). The release build now carries the fake Backend as a lazy chunk for this. (b) **Steps** are data, not code, so a Plugin can write them: `{event, field = value…}`, matched on the event's fields ignoring case (`"*"` present, `""` missing), any of a list. The events are the app's own: `command` (every command run, with `id` and `arg`), `session_open`, `session_exit`, `host_selected`, `help_open`, `help_back`, `help_forward`, `help_search`; they go only to the tutorial running, on the demo, never to Plugins' code. (c) **The ticket's "Split it"** can't be a step: split panes are out of scope (Q36). Lesson 2 is "More than one Session" instead: open `demo-02`, then go back with `␣ [` or `␣ 1`, with a NOTE that Sessions are tabs. (d) **The lessons:** 1 Find a Host (the Picker, a Session to `demo-01`), 2 More than one Session, 3 Find your way in help (follow a link to `quickref`, `Ctrl+o` back, find `terminal.size` in `:help options` by `/` search or `:help terminal.size`), 4 Make it yours (`:theme`, `:config`), then a Summary that points to `start`, `quickref` and `config`. The keys on the page are the ones bound now. Neovim's words: "Lesson N: …", "NOTE:", a summary at the end, and a lesson index at the top (each lesson's steps done); title case, not Neovim's capitals (taste: honest voice). (e) **Commands:** `:tutor [NAME]` (from the demo: the page again, or another tutorial on the same demo), `:tutor quit`, `:tutor reset [NAME]`; no default key. The page is `:help tutor` (`tutor-NAME` for a Plugin's), listed under Getting started; the index's first line and `start.md`'s "New to roster" point to it. (f) **The ✓:** a step is a node like the Sidebar's, a ring while it's to do that fills with the text colour and a ✓ cut out of it when done (not the accent: a done step isn't live), its words stepping back to muted. It's the one motion in help, because the cause is somewhere else (taste: motion that confirms): the disc grows from 0.6 and fades in over 150 ms with the strong ease-out, the ✓ and words fade over 120 ms; a transition, so it retargets; reduced motion drops the growth and keeps the fades; a page that opens on a done step shows it done without motion. The banner doesn't animate; it says "N of M steps done" to a screen reader as a step ticks. As static text (T46's website, a PDF) a step is a checklist line. (g) **Plugins:** `roster.tutor.set(id, {title, summary, intro, lessons, outro})` with Markdown text, checked like every op (known events only, unique step ids, at least one lesson). **Not yet, a follow-up:** a Plugin isn't loaded on the demo, so its tutorial can only teach the app's own commands for now. |
| Q77 | The Mesh's details that Q63 leaves open (ticket 39, issue #51) | **Settled while building the CLI half, fakes only (2026-10-31).** *Opting out:* the most specific `mesh:` wins, and `mesh: false` keeps a Host out of its group's Mesh **and** every Link (that's what makes a Cut's `mesh: false` stick). *Password Hosts* (`enroll: false`, which is what appliances are: Dropbear ignores a key line with `from=`) are peers only, never sources: roster never writes on a Host it reaches with a password, so a Link from one is listed as `skipped`. *Files:* everything roster writes on a Host is tagged or in a file it owns: on Linux `~/.ssh/roster-mesh/` (key, its own `known_hosts`, `config`), two marked lines at the top of `~/.ssh/config` (an `Include`), `~/ROSTER.md` (left alone, with a warning, if roster didn't write it) and an `@~/ROSTER.md` line in `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` when Claude is installed; on ESXi the files go on the first datastore (`/vmfs/volumes/<id>/.roster-mesh`), peer keys in `/etc/ssh/keys-USER/authorized_keys`, then `/sbin/auto-backup.sh`. Removing the last of them leaves a Host byte for byte as before. *Two passes, one connection at a time:* each source first (make its key there, read the public half, test each peer's port with `nc`/bash), then every Host visited. *Not written as working:* a Link the source can't reach, or whose peer has no host key recorded on this Box, is left out of both ends; one the source can't test (no `nc` or bash) is written and shown as untested. A source that doesn't answer keeps its keys on its peers as they were. *Bookkeeping on the Box:* `~/.config/roster/mesh.json` holds the Hosts roster wrote on (so ones that left the Mesh are visited and cleaned), what's pending, cuts pending, and a digest of the Mesh at the last complete apply (`mesh out of date`); it's never the source of truth. A Host taken out of `roster.yml` altogether can't be reached, so its own files stay, and `roster mesh` says so. *Cut* also removes HOST's own Mesh files, and when `roster.yml` isn't in a git repo it writes `mesh: false` straight to the file. `roster setup` applies the Mesh only without `--only`, and `roster check` checks it only without `--only`, since the Mesh covers every Host. The app's half is T50 (#87), per Q80. |
| Q78 | Native Windows backend (ticket 19, #19) | **roster runs on Windows itself, with Windows' OpenSSH; `backend` in init.lua picks it, and changing it restarts serve (2026-10-31).** `auto` on Windows looks for roster on Windows first (PATH with PATHEXT, plus `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin`; nothing is started) and takes it if it finds an Inventory (`ROSTER_CONFIG`, `~\roster.yml`, `%APPDATA%\roster\roster.yml`, `~\infra\roster.yml`); else the WSL distros as before; else whichever has roster. So a PC without WSL just works, and one where only WSL has the Inventory keeps WSL without being asked, and WSL isn't booted when Windows has it all. `local` is only Windows' own roster; `wsl` only WSL's. A `:source` that changes `backend` or `roster_command` starts serve again (the vault locks; open Sessions keep running). **Askpass:** a named pipe owned by the user's SID with a protected DACL naming only that SID (no Administrators or SYSTEM), `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`, a 128-bit random name claimed with `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, opened by the helper with identification-level impersonation; the helpers are roster's entry-point launchers (`roster-askpass.exe`, `roster-askpass-login.exe`), since Windows' ssh runs only a program as `SSH_ASKPASS` and `LocalCommand` goes through cmd.exe. Never a `.cmd` helper: a Host-chosen prompt would reach cmd.exe's parser. For the same reason roster refuses to pass a `.cmd`/`.bat` tool (npm's `bw.cmd`) an argument with `"%^&\|<>!`. A timeout kills a tool's whole process tree (`taskkill /T`), as a batch file's real program is its child. Paths in ssh's config use forward slashes, and no `ControlMaster` (Windows' OpenSSH has no Unix sockets). Windows paths for `vault.kp.*` are taken only with `backend = "local"`. **Not yet:** signing the vault in from the app with a password or SSO (it drives `bw login` on a PTY); the API key works, or `roster bw login` in a terminal. **Rejected:** a Unix socket on Windows (AF_UNIX exists, but its file's ACL is inherited and the folder has no 0700 to rely on); `.cmd` helpers (cmd.exe parses the prompt); preferring WSL in `auto` (it boots a distro for teammates who have everything on Windows). No API change. |
| Q79 | This Box's health: `:check` with no Host (ticket 49, #79) | **Neovim's `:checkhealth`, roster's look (2026-10-31; agent's calls, for the owner's review).** (a) **The words:** `:check` with no Host checks this Box; `:check HOST` is unchanged. `roster check --box` is the CLI's, as the ticket names it: plain `roster check` keeps checking every Host's reachability, since scripts rely on it and it connects to each Host, which a Box check must never do. (b) **Local only:** every check reads files, the `PATH`, each vault CLI's own status command (the one the lock screen already runs) and the infra repo's git with no fetch. Nothing logs in to a Host, scans one or fetches, so it's safe on a rate-limited network; nothing networked was added, so there's no opt-in. Logins are counted by provider, never looked up (so it isn't `auth-check`, which reads each secret). (c) **serve:** one method, `box.check` → `{checks: [{section, level, message, fix?, topic?}], ok}`, API 16; no new error codes. It reads `roster.yml` as it is now without applying it, so the watcher still reports an outside edit. Sections: `roster`, `inventory`, `ssh`, `vault`, `askpass`, `repo`; a section the app doesn't know is shown under its id. (d) **The app's own checks:** how it reaches serve (Backend, WSL distro, API version, and a roster too old for `box.check` still gets the app's sections), `init.lua` (error with file and line, each warning) and the terminal font (one of `terminal.font`'s families installed). (e) **A registry:** `registries.health`, sections with an order and a `checks(ctx)` hook, so a Plugin adds a section the way the built-ins do; one that throws is an error in its own section. (f) **Shown as a help page,** `health` (alias `checkhealth`), opened at once ("Checking this Box…") and filled in, reusing the help panel's links, `/`, outline and `:helpgrep`. Each check is a definition entry: the level word (`OK`, `WARNING`, `ERROR`, in Iosevka; OK muted, warning yellow, error raspberry, never the accent), the message and fix, and its topic as a link. The section headings are topics (`health#vault`) even before a run. (g) **No toolbar control:** `:check` isn't a daily command, so Q65 doesn't call for one by default; the help index lists the page, a Session's error panel offers Run `:check`, and the start page names it. (h) **No motion:** the page opens from a key (taste: motion confirms; a key confirms itself). |
| Q80 | Minimal core, first-party Plugins | **roster stays small and suckless (owner, 2026-10-31): features that only some people need ship as optional Plugins we provide, not in the core app.** The first three are the Topology view (T40 #52), the Discover UI (T44 #72) and the mesh controls (T50 #87), in iteration 8, after the Plugin API (T23 #23, T24 #24). The CLI keeps the engines: `roster discover`, `roster mesh` (T39 #51 is the core half) and their `roster serve` methods. Plugins stay Lua-only with no HTML or JS (Q38). Like Neovim's plugins, which fill the editor's own windows and text rather than drawing pixels, they feed native core primitives with data and actions: panels and floating windows, lists and tables, text with highlights and links, status and toolbar segments, a graph view, confirmations and progress. The core draws them in roster's style, and Plugins reach only the serve methods they're scoped to, never secrets. The bundled Themes stay in the core. Q73–Q79 are left for iteration 5's T43. |
| Q81 | Plugin trust: Neovim's model plus permissions | **Plugins work like Neovim's (Lua, native UI), but with permissions enforced by the core (owner, 2026-10-31).** A Plugin's Lua runs in the same sandbox as `init.lua`, and the core gives it only the functions for the permissions the user granted. Without a permission, that function doesn't exist in the Plugin's sandbox. Every call into the core is also checked against its grants. Permissions: none (UI, commands, keys), `inventory.read` / `inventory.write` (as Pending changes), `sessions.open`, `terminal.read`, `terminal.write`, `network` (listed domains), `serve:<method>`. **No permission ever reaches secrets**: vault passwords, askpass and the master password live only in `roster serve` and never pass through the app. A password the user types into a terminal is visible to `terminal.read`, and the install prompt says so. Warnings: at install (every permission, with the risky ones flagged and explained), on any update that asks for a new permission, and in `:plugins` (grants, revoke toggles, recent use). **UI:** no HTML, JS or iframes (Q38 stands; sandboxed web panels were considered and dropped). Plugins build UI only from core primitives, each one a registry the core itself uses: Sidebar sections, side panels, floating windows over the terminal, full views (Topology), finders and Picker sources, commands and keys, toolbar and status segments, badges on Host rows and sections in Host details, notifications, confirmations and progress, and placement in the Q39 layouts. The core draws them in roster's style, so Plugins look native without CSS. **Every Plugin is the same:** one API, the same permission prompts, the same install path (`:plugins install`), no back doors and no special treatment. The only difference is where a first-party Plugin lives and what it has been through: it lives **in the roster repo**, is reviewed like the core, tested in CI with every release, and security-reviewed. None ship inside the core app (Q80). A third-party Plugin comes from its author's git URL (T24), shows its author and source, and is pinned in `roster.lock`. Rejected: full trust like Neovim (a malicious or hacked Plugin could act on every server) and HTML panels (keystroke and IPC exposure). T23 #23, T24 #24. |
| Q82 | How ticket 23 builds the Plugin API (#23) | **Agent's calls within ADR 0006 and Q81, for the owner's review ([plugins.md](../plugins.md)).** (a) **Where it runs:** roster-core runs each Plugin in its own Lua state on its own thread (`crates/roster-core/src/plugin/`), with `init.lua`'s sandbox; the frontend never runs Lua. Each call into a Plugin answers with its result and **ops**: plain data (`{op = "section", id, spec}`), each Lua function sent as `{handler = N}`; the frontend checks every op (`app/src/plugins/ops.ts`) and puts it into the registries with the Plugin's name as its source, and runs a handler with `:plugins call NAME N`. So the Tauri side is five commands and no events. (b) **A Plugin is a folder:** `roster-plugin.lua` (the manifest: name, version, description, author, `api`, `permissions`; Lua, run in the sandbox with no `roster` module and no `require`, so reading it does nothing) and `init.lua`, with `require` from its own folder. Names are lower case with `-`; `builtin`, `init`, `roster` and a built-in Plugin's name are kept. (c) **Install, local only:** `:plugins install PATH` (`~/…`, absolute, or under the config folder) reads the manifest and prompts; the folder is used where it is, not copied. Grants live in the State (`plugins`: path, granted, asked), which neither `init.lua` nor a Plugin can write. Packages (T24) will clone and then load through the same `PluginHost::load(Source::Dir)`. (d) **`roster.api_version` is 2**, one number for `init.lua`'s module and Plugins'; a manifest's `api` above it asks for a newer app. (e) **Permissions:** each one's functions exist only when granted and check again when called. `inventory.write` is `host.update`, `host.rename`, `host.move`, `host.review` (Pending changes only). `serve:<method>` takes an allow-list (`inventory.info`, `hosts.list`, `host.get`, `host.check`, `changes.diff`, `mesh.*`, `discover.*`); anything else, every vault, session, quick and Publish method above all, makes the manifest invalid; one that can change something (`mesh.apply`, `mesh.cut`; everything but `inventory.info`, `hosts.list`, `host.get`, `changes.diff`, `mesh.status`, `mesh.plan`) is flagged in the prompt. **`network` is refused for now:** it needs an HTTP client in the core and a domain list in the manifest (filed). (f) **Isolation:** 32 MB per Plugin; 1 s of Lua for `init.lua`, 250 ms per call or event (time waiting for serve doesn't count), uncatchable like `init.lua`'s; a call not answered within 2 s more is abandoned and the Plugin disabled (one long C call). An error is a notice naming the Plugin, file and line; an error while loading disables it, and so does the third error after that ("repeat offenders"); calling an action the Plugin already replaced isn't its fault and doesn't count. A disabled Plugin's UI goes away; `:plugins reload NAME` tries again. (g) **What a Plugin can't take:** keys only after the Leader or in the Sidebar (`leader`, `list`, `list_vim`), never the terminal; never a command, key, Theme, section, segment, finder or help topic another source has, nor a key sequence that would hide a bound one (`a z` when `a` is bound). Ops are checked and cut (500 characters of text, 2,000 rows, 1,000 ops a call). (h) **Prompts and `:plugins`:** the install prompt shows author, version and the folder as "Unverified", every permission with the risky ones (`inventory.write`, `sessions.open`, `terminal.read` saying a password typed at an echoing prompt is visible, `terminal.write`) flagged and explained; Enter installs, Esc cancels and installs nothing. An installed Plugin whose manifest asks for a permission it wasn't asked about **waits** for the update prompt; "Not now" runs it without the new permission, recorded as asked, so it doesn't nag; any permission can be turned on or off in `:plugins` (switches), which reloads the Plugin at once. `:plugins remove`, `reload`, `revoke NAME PERM`, `grant NAME PERM`. (i) **The built-in proof:** the status bar's Host count is a Plugin on the public API (`crates/roster-core/plugins/hosts`), compiled into the app (`builtin:hosts`), granted what it asks until the user revokes it in `:plugins`, and not removable. **Folders isn't moved yet** (taste.md said ticket 23 would): it keeps its data in `folders.yml`, and Q81 names no permission for a Plugin's own files, so it waits for the owner's call on per-Plugin storage (filed). (j) **Primitives in v1:** commands, keys, Themes, Sidebar sections (rows with actions and a Host), status segments, finders (a list of items the Plugin sets; no lazy function), badges on Inventory rows, a floating window (a title and lines), notices, help pages (Markdown through the help registry, listed under the Plugin's name), sections of `:check` (the health registry, T49: checks the Plugin sets), and the events `lock`, `unlock`, `host_selected`, `session_open`, `session_exit`, `inventory_changed` (`inventory.read`; the Session ones also with `terminal.read`) and `terminal_output` (`terminal.read`: what Sessions print, batched every 200 ms, escape sequences stripped, at most 16 KB; never what's typed). Not yet, filed for the first-party Plugins (Q80): tables, the graph view, side panels and full views, Host-details sections, toolbar controls, confirmations, progress, and timers. (k) **No motion:** the prompt, `:plugins` and a float open from a key like every side panel; notices reuse the Config toast's entrance unchanged. **Iteration 8 review (2026-10-31):** a failed call is all-or-nothing in the core too, as its ops are dropped: the handlers it replaced are let go only once a run succeeds, and a failed one puts handlers, timers, the Plugin's commands and its quiet handlers back (before, its timers that never reached the app counted toward the cap of 100 and the buttons it replaced went stale). What its Lua changed (a variable, a listener) stays, as a failed `init.lua`'s would. |
| Q83 | How ticket 24 builds Packages (#24) | **Agent's calls within ADR 0006, Q81 and Q82, for the owner's review ([plugins.md](../plugins.md#packages)).** (a) **Where:** roster-core's `package` module (`crates/roster-core/src/package/`) runs the system's `git` as a subprocess with no shell; the clones live in the app's own data folder (`<local app data>/packages/NAME`, next to the State), never the config folder; `roster.lock` (JSON, name → `{ source, tag, branch, commit }`, sorted) sits next to `init.lua` for dotfiles. (b) **git's guard rails:** no credentials from roster and no way to ask for any (no terminal prompt, `GIT_ASKPASS`/`SSH_ASKPASS` removed, Git Credential Manager non-interactive, stdin closed); only the `https`, `ssh` and `file` transports (`protocol.allow=never` plus those three; no `ext::`, and plain `http://` and `git://` refused in init.lua); no submodules; hooks pointed at nowhere; a 120 s limit per git run. The user's own git setup (an ssh agent, a credential helper) still applies. (c) **Declaring:** `packages = { { "owner/repo", tag = …, config = { … } }, "owner/repo", … }`; `owner/repo` means GitHub over https; a full URL, `git@host:path` or a full local path works too. At most one of `tag`, `branch`, `commit`; `name =` when the repo's name won't do; names are unique ignoring case (Windows folders). **`config` is a table, not a function:** init.lua and each Package run in Lua states of their own (Q82a), and a function can't move between them without bytecode, which the sandbox refuses; the Package reads it as `roster.plugin.config`, and a change reloads it. A function there is an error that says so. (d) **Trust and order:** the core clones only what init.lua names and refuses any other name on every call; a new Package's clone is checked out (the lock's commit, else what init.lua asks for) and then gets the same install prompt as `:plugins install`, with its git URL and commit shown as unverified. Allow pins it in `roster.lock`; Cancel removes the clone and leaves the lock as it was, and it isn't asked again until the next start. A Package is the Plugin `package:NAME` in the State's `plugins`, so grants stay per Box while the lock roams. (e) **Offline:** with a clone and its lock line, a start runs no network; git fetches only to clone, or when the lock pins a commit the clone doesn't have. If init.lua later names another version than the lock, the lock wins until `:packages update` (shown in `:packages`). (f) **`:packages update`** fetches each one and shows, per Package, the commits (at most 50), `--stat`, the diff (cut at 256 KB) and the new manifest's permissions, in the install prompt's place; Enter applies and pins it and grants what it newly asks for; Esc keeps the version it has (unlike a local Plugin's update, which runs without the new permission, Q82h). (g) **Removing:** `:packages clean` removes clones and lock lines init.lua no longer names, and their State entries; `:plugins remove NAME` on a Package removes its clone and lock line too, and says to take it out of init.lua. A Package init.lua stops naming is unloaded at once, kept until `clean`. (h) **No serve change** (API stays 17). (i) **No motion:** the update prompt and `:packages` open from a command like the other side panels. |
| Q84 | What's new, deprecations, help from where you are, and help's accessibility (tickets 47, 48, 52, 54; #77, #78, #82, #85) | **Neovim's `news.txt`, `deprecated.txt`, `K` and `Ctrl-]`, in roster's own look (agent, 2026-10-31, for the owner's review).** **News:** `docs/news.md` (`:help news`) has `## Unreleased`, then one section per Release, each with Breaking, New, Changed, Removed and Deprecated (left out when empty), the bullets grouped by area in bold (CLI, App, serve API with its API version) rather than by more headings, so the topic finder isn't filled with `news#app-7`. On the first start after an upgrade the status line says once "roster 0.2.0: what's new: `:help news`" (no popup: taste.md's honest voice); the State keeps `newsSeen`. A first start says nothing; an older app's State (used, but no `newsSeen`) counts as an upgrade; an older build on a newer State says nothing. `scripts/bump-version.sh X.Y.Z` renames `## Unreleased` to `## vX.Y.Z`; the Release's notes start with that section (`scripts/news_section.py`, which points its links at the files at the tag and keeps help topics as text), then the install note, then GitHub's PR list; a tag with no section fails the Version job before anything builds. **The reminder:** `.github/workflows/news.yml`, one job with a sparse checkout and the PR read through the API, fails a PR whose title or commits start `feat`/`perf` or say `!:` when neither `docs/news.md` nor `docs/deprecated.md` changed; the `skip-news` label silences it. **Deprecated:** `docs/deprecated.md` (`:help deprecated`), `## Deprecated in 0.N`, then CLI, App, init.lua options, serve API and Keys, each a table old → new. Its rows are topics (`deprecated#roster_desk_roster`, a guide's `rowTopics`), and each old name is an alias in `guides.ts` that lands on its row (`:help ROSTER_DESK_ROSTER`). The old `␣ ?` for Key bindings has a row but no alias, since `␣ ?` is help's key now. A renamed `init.lua` option goes in the core's `DEPRECATED_OPTIONS`: it still applies under its new name (the new one wins when both are set) and the frontend warns "OLD is deprecated: use NEW (:help deprecated#…)" at its line; the list is empty today. **Removal policy (proposed, owner to confirm):** an old name stays for at least one minor Release after the one that deprecates it, and its removal is a Removed bullet in the news. **Help from where you are:** `Ctrl+]` and F1 in the command line (`cmdline.help`) open help on the command typed (`:conn` → `:connect`, an empty line → `commands`); Esc in help brings the line back with its text and cursor. `␣ K` is Key bindings (Q66), so App mode's lookup is **`␣ F1`** (`:lookup`): a Host's row opens `:connect`, any other row its command, no row the topic finder. F1 in the Picker and every finder (`picker.help`) opens the selected result's topic (a finder source may name its own: Themes give `themes#ID`), and F1 in the Key bindings filter the first row's option (`keys.cmdline` → `'keys'` searched for `cmdline`). F1 because it's every desktop app's help key and no shell or finder query needs it; it isn't a `global` key since TUI apps (htop, mc) use it in a terminal. **Accessibility (the T43 review's three LOWs):** each finder and the Picker has a visually hidden `role="status"`, always mounted, that says the count ("12 results", "No matches") once typing pauses for 500 ms; help's search count is the same kind of region, mounted empty while help is open and set 150 ms after it settles, so a search that arrives with the page is heard; the visible counts are `aria-hidden`. The outline's and `:helpgrep`'s path sits at the right. **Motion:** none; every one of these starts from a key. |
| Q85 | One source for serve-api.md's tables; the docs' external links weekly (tickets 51, 53; #81, #83) | **The method tables are generated from serve's docstrings, and external links are checked by a weekly workflow, never per PR (2026-10-31).** Each handler's docstring carries `Params:`, `Result:`, `Errors:` and `Since: API N` after its prose; a method that takes two shapes of params (`host.scan_key {host}` and `{address, port?}`, `vault.login`'s actions) has one `Params:` line, and so one row, per shape. `scripts/gen-serve-api.py` rewrites the regions between `<!-- BEGIN generated:methods NAME ... -->` and `<!-- END generated:methods -->`, so the sections and their prose stay hand-written and each marker says which methods its table holds; every method must be in exactly one table, and a new one fails `tests/test_serve_api_doc.py` with what to do. The tables gain an Errors column and a Since API column; the history paragraph under `api_version` stays. The script lives in `scripts/`, not the package, because nothing at run time needs it (unlike `manpage.py`). `CommandDef` gets an optional `doc`, a Markdown paragraph shown under the command's label in `:help commands`. External links: `scripts/check-links.py` (standard library only, no action from the Marketplace) runs Mondays and on demand, skips made-up and private addresses so it fetches and prints only the docs' public URLs, counts only 404, 410, 5xx and unreachable (twice) as dead (403 and 429 are robots turned away), and keeps one issue, "Dead links in the docs" (`documentation`, `needs-triage`), reopened with its body replaced, or closed when all work. **Rejected:** generating whole sections (the prose around each table explains it); a per-PR link check (slow, and someone else's outage would fail a PR); `lychee` (another binary to trust, for a few dozen links). |
| Q86 | The Linux check: what `:info` reports, and how Linux is measured (ticket 20, #20) | **Measure from inside, no automation (2026-10-31; agent's calls, for the owner's review).** (a) **The renderer:** `:info` names the renderer the terminal on screen really draws with, not the one `terminal.renderer` asks for: `WebGL`, `DOM`, `DOM (no WebGL here)` (no WebGL2 context: WebKitGTK without GPU, some VMs, WSLg on software GL; the terminal stops trying for its life) or `DOM (WebGL context lost)` (it tries again the next time it's shown). With no Session on screen it names the option. (b) **The Picker's open time** joins `:info` once the Picker has opened (`Picker opened in N ms`, key to first frame), because WebKitGTK has no DevTools protocol to drive or read like WebView2's: two numbers, no timers. (c) **Terminal latency is measured by a Session** (`app/scripts/perf/latency.py`, the stand-in serve's `latency` Host): it times cursor-position requests, which xterm.js answers only after parsing everything before them, so idle round trip, a 2 MB log, an htop-like full-screen redraw and a 1 MB paste are measured end to end without window automation. (d) **Linux runs with its own HOME and XDG dirs** (`app/scripts/linux-run.sh`), so no `roster.yml`, `init.lua`, State or vault of the user's is ever found. (e) **CI builds the Linux app on every pull request** (`app-linux`, Ubuntu 22.04 as the release build) so a Linux break shows before a release, and its binary is what `linux-run.sh fetch` takes. (f) **IME:** a key that's part of a composition names no chord, so no binding runs on it. See [dev/linux.md](dev/linux.md). |
| Q87 | The example third-party Plugin, and what it found (ticket 56, #90) | **Agent's calls within Q81–Q83, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **recent-alerts** (`examples/plugins/recent-alerts`) is built as an outside author would: only the public API, `terminal.read` only, its settings (`patterns`, `leader`, `max`, `notify`) from `roster.plugin.config` (Q83c), its own README as the template. It's the worked example of [plugins.md](../plugins.md#a-first-plugin); pinned stays as the small one. (b) **Tested end to end on both sides:** `crates/roster-core/tests/recent_alerts.rs` makes a git repo from the folder, installs it as a Package and drives the real Lua (every slot, a probe that finds nothing it wasn't granted, revoke, updates, removal, throw/loop/allocate); it records one scripted run as `app/src/plugins/recent-alerts.transcript.json`, which the frontend replays against the fake Backend's stand-in (`fakeRecentAlerts.ts`) so the UI tests can't drift from the Lua. (c) **One API addition, `roster.clipboard.copy(text)`** (op `copy`, and a `copy` row-action icon): the spec's "copy" action had no way to work.
