It needs no permission but works **only in a call the user started** (an action, command or finder item: the core's `Job::Call` is the one place that decides, and the frontend applies the `copy` ops the core sent, see (g4)), never at load or on an event, so a Plugin can't swap the clipboard behind the user's back. (d) **An update asking for `network`** can't be prompted yet: `network` is refused in any manifest (Q82e), so `:packages update` says the new version can't be used and the old one keeps running; the re-prompt itself is tested with `sessions.open`. (e) **Fixed on the way:** a Package's install prompt that came up behind the lock screen lost focus to the workspace on unlock, so Enter and Esc didn't answer it; it now keeps the keys. (f) **Left for iteration 8 (filed by the coordinator):** going to a Session by its id and to a line in its scrollback (Enter goes to the Host's Session); a Plugin can't run a command line from a function (so `:alerts` can't open the finder); no way to know which Leader keys are free before binding (a refused key is only a notice; `a` is Folders'); no timers (a "2 min ago" can't refresh); no section-level actions ("dismiss all" lives in `:alerts clear`); the float has no actions. (g) **After the end-of-iteration review (2026-10-31):** (g1) **The install and permission prompt opens on the safe choice** (Cancel, Keep, Not now), never on Install/Allow, and a repeating Enter (one held from before the prompt came up) answers nothing: a Package's prompt comes up behind the lock screen, so the Enter that unlocked, pressed twice, installed unverified code. Install is a deliberate move (`Shift+Tab` then `Enter`, or a click); the key hint says so. No arm delay: with the keys on Cancel, a stray Enter can only decline, and a declined Package is asked about again at the next start. (g2) **Focus stays in its own workspace:** the workspace gives the keys to its own prompt (a ref, not a document-wide query), so with `:tutor`'s demo in front (Q76) the demo's never reaches for the hidden, inert prompt of yours, and coming back from the demo gives the keys to your prompt if one is up. (g3) **Text that isn't UTF-8** from a Plugin (a label, notice, badge, float line, what it copies) is shown with U+FFFD where it breaks, not refused: refusing failed every redraw after one bad line, and three of those disabled the Plugin. Names (ids, table keys) are still refused. recent-alerts also cuts its carried line where a character starts. (g4) **The core alone decides what a Plugin may do:** the manager no longer second-guesses a `copy` op; the fake Backend mirrors the core (it must, for the UI tests), and its manifest checks read the core's own table (`crates/roster-core/tests/fixtures/plugin-permissions.json`, which permission.rs tests its constants and cases against), so `serve:host.remove` or `disk.read` is refused on both sides. (g5) **A failed copy says so:** if the clipboard can't be written, the app shows why and drops the notices that call gave after the copy, so the Plugin's "Copied" never shows for nothing. (g6) **A Quick Connect Session's `host` is `nil`** in Plugin events (`session_open`, `session_exit`, `terminal_output`): it isn't a Host of the Inventory, so a Plugin mustn't badge it or make a row go to it; recent-alerts labels those alerts "Quick Connect". (g7) **recent-alerts** gives at most one notice per Host every `quiet` seconds (a new setting, default 30; the next one counts those held back), skips a line that looks like a shell prompt and a command (its own echo; a heuristic, said in its README, since Plugins never see typed input and won't), matches the whole line before cutting it to show, and checks `notify` is true or false. (h) **After the owner's test in the Windows app (ticket 73, #126, agent's calls):** (h1) **Details duplicated an alert because the terminal was resized:** the float was a docked side panel, so opening or closing it narrowed or widened the terminal, and a resized Session prints its screen again (ConPTY repaints on Windows; full-screen programs redraw anywhere), which `terminal_output` passed on as new lines. Two fixes in the core, for every Plugin: the float now lies over the terminal in every layout (as `roster.float`'s docs always said), on the side away from the Sidebar, with no motion like the other panels; and for a second after the app resizes a Session, lines it already sent that Session's Plugins lately are left out of `terminal_output` (new lines still go), and a cursor move to a row is a line break, so rows drawn by position don't run together. A repeat of the very same line in that second is lost: the price of telling a repaint from output without a terminal emulator in the path. (h2) **A Plugin's badge is a quiet count chip**, not roster's own `unreviewed` badge (warn outline, shown only on a raised row, hiding the address): always shown right after the Host's name, so the address keeps its place; 16 px high, a pill at least as wide, a 22% muted fill and the text colour, never the accent (live) or warn; Iosevka tabular digits; cut after 12 characters; its tooltip and the row's accessible name say it with the Plugin's name (`recent-alerts: 2`); one chip per Plugin. The contract stays text (at most 40 characters); the docs ask for a count. recent-alerts sets `"2"`, not `"2 alerts"`. |
| Q88 | Row buttons on the selected row; no pull blocking a form (tickets 68, 69; #119, #120) | **Owner (2026-10-31), while testing before v0.2.0. Amends Q65 and Q71(a) (when a Sidebar row's controls show), and Q13 with ticket 10's pull on opening a form (spec story 56).** **(1) A Sidebar row's buttons show only on the selected row, never on hover.** The selected row is the one last left-clicked, and in App mode the cursor row, so j/k move the buttons. Hovering still highlights a row, and its address stays in view until the row is clicked. It's the same on touch and pen, which have no hover. The controls are still visible (Q65), just on one row at a time. The Inventory heading's "Add a Host" stays pinned. A raised (on-screen) row no longer shows them by itself. **The Picker's ⓘ follows the same rule (owner, 2026-10-31, T72 #124):** it shows on the selected row only, never revealed by a hover. The Picker's selection still follows the pointer, as Enter acts on it, so the ⓘ goes with the selection, and a row the keyboard left keeps none under a resting pointer. **(2) No form blocks on a pull.** `:edit`, `:host add` and Save to vault open at once from the Inventory the app has. If the last good pull of the infra repo is older than the freshness window, they pull in the background. Save waits for that pull, and pulls again if the form sat open past the window. A failed pull (diverged, offline, uncommitted: 62, 63, 71) blocks the save with the same errors and Try again, never the form. One pull at a time, shared by the forms and `:pull`. **The guarantee kept:** a write lands only on a repo pulled within the window, or one with Pending changes already here (those were pulled before the first one; Q13's Publish fetches and refuses a `roster.yml` that moved upstream). **Agent's calls, for the owner's review:** (a) the option is **`pull_fresh_minutes`**, default **5**, 0 to 1440, and `0` pulls every time, as before but without blocking; (b) **a mid-form change** is said in the form, not reset: in `:edit`, the fields you hadn't touched take the pulled values and the rest keep yours. A status line names both ("The pull changed esx-02: Address now shows the new value; Title changed there too, and yours is kept"). A Save whose own pull changed the Host stops there, so you save on the new values. In `:host add` and Save to vault, a name the pull brought is called out under the form, and roster refuses it (81) anyway; (c) App mode starts its cursor on the selected row when there is one, else on the raised row as before, so the buttons don't jump when you press the Leader; (d) the buttons appear **without motion** (animate: list navigation from keys is the no-animation tier; the click itself confirms). |
| Q89 | The Plugin API reference and its stubs, from one source (ticket 70, #121) | **Neovim's `:help api` and its LuaCATS meta files, generated from the core (agent's calls, 2026-10-31, for the owner's review).** (a) **The source is a declarative table in Rust**, `crates/roster-core/src/plugin/api.rs`, next to the runtime it describes: each function (parameters, returns, errors, what the app refuses with a notice instead, permission, `Since: api N`, an example), each value, type (fields with type, required, meaning; aliases) and event, with types written as LuaCATS. Rust rather than a JSON or TOML schema because the tests that hold it to the runtime are Rust, it needs no new dependency, and a typo in a struct doesn't compile. (b) **What it generates:** `docs/plugin-api.md` (a classic reference: an index of every function, a section per module with one entry per function, a field table per type, the events, `roster.serve.call`'s results linking serve-api.md), `examples/plugins/types/roster.lua` (`---@meta`, classes `roster.Host`…, `---@overload` per event so a listener's `e` is typed) and `crates/roster-core/tests/fixtures/plugin-api.json` (the same as data, with the help topics). `cargo run -p roster-core --example gen_plugin_api` writes them; whole files, not regions, since nothing in them is hand-written. (c) **Drift, from every side that owns a piece:** `tests/plugin_api.rs` loads a Plugin under eight sets of grants and compares every function and value its `roster` module really has with api.rs, and `roster.plugin`'s keys with the Plugin type, and the committed files with the generator's output; unit tests hold the events to `runtime::EVENTS`, the manifest fields to `MANIFEST_FIELDS` (now the one list `parse_manifest` reads) and the Theme fields to `ThemeDef`; `app/src/plugins/api.test.ts` records with a Proxy every field `ops.ts` reads of each table a Plugin hands over and compares it with the type's fields; `tests/test_plugin_api.py` compares a Host, service, group and `inventory.info` from a real `roster serve` with theirs. (d) **Help topics:** the page is `plugin-api` (the old `plugin-api` alias to plugins.md's section goes); each function is `roster.x.y()`, each value and module its path, each type `roster-Type`, all aliases from the JSON onto GitHub-slugged headings, so the docs site gets them too. plugins.md stays the narrative and keeps a short map to the reference. (e) **Shipping the stubs:** in the repo at `examples/plugins/types/roster.lua`, and in the app as the help page `plugin-types` (`:help roster.lua`), the copy the app was built with; no command writes the file to disk yet. (f) **CI:** a `plugin-types` job runs lua-language-server 3.19.2 (pinned, checked against its SHA-256) as `--check examples/plugins --checklevel=Hint`; the examples carry annotations and `examples/plugins/.luarc.json` points at the stubs. (g) **Lua 5.4, said up front:** the reference opens with "The Lua" for Neovim authors (integers, bitwise operators, no `bit`/`jit`/FFI, `roster.*` not `vim.*`, the sandbox, which also has no `coroutine`), checked by `plugin_api.rs` in a running Plugin; ADR 0006 gains "Why Lua 5.4, not LuaJIT" (mlua's memory limit needs its allocator, which LuaJIT may refuse; count hooks don't run in JIT-compiled code; FFI would go around Q81; 64-bit integers; one portable build). (h) **Found on the way:** `roster.inventory.host` raises on an unknown name (code 2), so pinned's `:pin NOSUCH` was an error that counted toward stopping it; pinned now asks through `pcall`, documented as such. `host_selected` sent a Quick Connect tab's target as `host`; it's now sent only for a Host's Session, as Q87 (g6) meant. |
| Q90 | Code's colours, and the docs' type (ticket 71, #123) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **The highlighter is hand-written** (`app/src/help/highlight.ts`): a few rules per language for Lua, shell, YAML, JSON and TOML, about 5 KB minified (2 KB gzipped). `:help` needs it in the app, not only on the site, so build-time highlighting alone wouldn't do; Prism's core with those grammars is about three times that, highlight.js's more, and Shiki's runtime with its grammars and WASM is far heavier. The examples are short and ours, so rules beat grammars: the text always comes back whole, an unclosed string or comment runs to the end, and an unknown language (`ts`, `rust`, `text`) stays plain. Measured with `vite build`: the app's JS grew 2.9 KB (0.7 KB gzipped) and its CSS 1 KB, and it runs only when a help page is drawn, so start-up is untouched ([performance.md](performance.md)'s budgets hold). Search marks wrap the coloured runs they cover, so a match is still one `<mark>`. (b) **Eight syntax tokens in every Theme**, flat beside the UI tokens so `colors`, `themes.*.colors`, a Plugin's Theme and the base's inheritance work as for the others: `syntax_keyword`, `syntax_string`, `syntax_number`, `syntax_comment`, `syntax_function`, `syntax_punctuation`, `syntax_key`, `syntax_command`. Each is text: 4.5:1 on every surface. None is the accent (taste.md): in Roster dark and light all eight are at least 12 from it (OKLab ΔE ×100), and in every bundled Theme the coloured five at least 8; Roster's palette also stays off its warn yellow, error raspberry and Sea glass. The ports use their sources' syntax colours, swapped where those are the source's own accent ([themes.md](themes.md#codes-colours)). A command is the one change of weight (500), so it reads without colour. (c) **The PDF's palette** is Roster light's hues taken darker, each at least 7:1 on white, with keywords and commands at 600 (Iosevka 600 joins the site's fonts) and comments the lightest grey, so greyscale keeps them apart; printing a site page uses the same. (d) **Every fenced block in `docs/` names its language**, `text` for output and trees, and `lint.test.ts` fails one that doesn't. (e) **Type** (`help/doc.css`): headings step down in em from the page's size, so each level is above the body in the panel and on the site (h3 was below it); prose keeps a 62ch measure while tables and code use the page's width; bold is Inter 600, which ships, not a synthesized 700; code in a sentence sits on a quiet raised chip; `text-wrap: balance` on headings and `pretty` on prose; tables use tabular figures. (f) **No motion:** nothing here animates. **T75 (#130), 2026-10-31, agent's calls under the owner's rule:** *Code colours change only if text fails contrast against its background*; being close to the error or accent colour isn't enough. Measured with the same WCAG 2 test as every Theme token (`contrast.test.ts`: each of the eight syntax colours on all six surfaces, code blocks sitting on `panel` and the inline chip on `raised`), the lowest ratio per Theme is: Roster dark 5.73 (comment on raised), Roster light 5.33 (string on chrome), Gruvbox 4.52 (number on raised), Nord 4.51 (number on raised), Catppuccin Mocha 4.52 (comment on raised), Tokyo Night 4.54 (comment on raised). All pass 4.5:1, so no colour changed: Roster's default Themes were checked first, and Gruvbox keeps gruvbox.vim's palette (its keyword red is its error red, ΔE 0.2, and its strings and functions are ΔE 10 from its accent, which the owner's rule accepts). *Bundle size:* a budget for the app's JS and CSS, minified and gzipped (every chunk, fonts left out, KB of 1,000 bytes as Vite prints them), about 10 % above the build of 2026-10-31 (JS 1,972 KB raw / 586 KB gzipped, CSS 75 / 13.8): JS ≤ 2,170 / 650 KB, CSS ≤ 83 / 15.5 KB. CI builds the frontend and `scripts/check-bundle-size.py` fails a pull request over it; going over means raising the budget in the script and [performance.md](performance.md#bundle-size) with a reason. Raw is what the webview parses; gzipped catches a large but repetitive dependency. *Zoom and reflow:* the docs site's pages with the most inline code (mesh, config, plugins) at 320 CSS px and at 200 % zoom of a 1280 px window (headless Chromium): no horizontal scroll, no chip past the viewport (long ones wrap, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`), and the chip (19.5 px) stays inside its 24.8 px line, so chips on adjacent lines don't touch. Nothing changed. |
| Q91 | The Mesh's `via:`, `mesh_dir:` and the ESXi sticky bit (T57 #98, T58 #99, T67 #117) | **Agent's calls, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** *`via: HOST` on a Link* (Q63's "later"): one Host, never a group or either end; the source's ssh config gets `ProxyJump HOST`, and the peer's `from=` is HOST's `ip:` from `roster.yml` (the address the peer sees, assuming the jump Host has one). The source must have its **own** Link to HOST (`ProxyJump` logs in there first): roster never adds that reach by itself, so a missing one is a Link status, `no-via`, left out and failing `roster check`, not a load error (eligibility is only known after `mesh:`/`reviewed:`). A `via:` Link wins over the same pair without a hop; two different hops for the same pair stop `roster.yml` from loading; `both: true` goes back through the same Host; a group Link that expands to the jump Host itself goes direct. *The test runs through the hop*: the source tests HOST's port as for any peer, and the Box logs in to HOST (one connection, in the probe pass) to test the peer's port from there; a down HOST leaves the Link pending with the source's key kept on the peer; a password HOST can't be logged in to, so the hop is untested. `api_version` stays 16: no params change, `via` and `no-via` are new values in the Mesh result. *`mesh_dir:`* cascades like `mesh:` and applies on Linux too (in place of `~/.ssh/roster-mesh`); an absolute path of `[A-Za-z0-9._-/]` only, since it goes into ssh's config unquoted. Checked on the Host before anything is written (on ESXi under `/vmfs/volumes/<datastore>/`, parent there, writable), and because a user's directory can hold an `id_ed25519` or `config` of its own, roster writes or removes files there only when the directory is its own (a `roster-mesh:` public key or a config roster wrote); otherwise the Host is pending. The old directory after a move is warned about, not removed. *Sticky bit:* a `keys-USER/authorized_keys` roster creates on ESXi gets mode `1600`, as ESXi's own file; existing files keep theirs (`cp -p`); to be confirmed on a real ESXi (mesh-pilot step 13). *Commit messages* name changed `links:` (`add link a -> b via j`, `change link a -> b (both)`), keyed by `from`/`to`. **T83 (#146), 2026-10-31, agent's calls, fakes only:** *A group move keeps `mesh_dir:`*: it's pinned on the Host like `mesh:`, and as with `mesh:` a Host with no `mesh_dir:` anywhere takes the new group's (Q77), since it had nothing to keep. *Removing or changing `mesh_dir:` warns* as a move to one did: the old directory is found from the `Include` line after roster's mark in `~/.ssh/config` (roster wrote it, so it names the directory in use until now), reported once, only when it holds roster's own Mesh key, and never removed; that `Include` is replaced by the new one rather than left behind. *A jump Host with several addresses:* `seen_as:` on a Host, not on the Link: an address belongs to the Host, and a Host-level list also covers a multi-homed or NATed source on a direct Link, group Links and `both: true`, where a per-Link address would be ambiguous. Its addresses (IP addresses or networks, one or a list) are added to `ip:` in `from=`, not put in its place, so the common case is one extra line; each one is one more address the key is accepted from, which the docs say. It's on the Host only (a group or `defaults` refuses to load), kept beside the Hosts like `mesh_dir:` so serve's Host and the Plugin API's are unchanged, in the Mesh's digest, and named in Publish's commit message. `api_version` stays 18. **T97 (#170), 2026-10-31, agent's calls, fakes only:** *A source that leaves the Mesh as its `mesh_dir:` changes* is cleaned up by the directory the `Include` after roster's mark names (the one in use until now), not by what `roster.yml` says now: an old `mesh_dir:` holding roster's key is warned about (the same warning) and never removed; roster's own default directory (`~/.ssh/roster-mesh`, a datastore's `.roster-mesh`) is removed, since roster picked it and leaving the Mesh takes roster's files away; the directory `roster.yml` names now is left as before (removed only if it's roster's). The mark and the `Include` after it go whatever directory it names. `api_version` stays 19. **Iteration 8 review, 2026-10-31, agent's call, fakes only:** a `via:` Link needs the source's own Link to the jump Host *written*, not only asked for: with no host key recorded for the jump Host that Link is `no-host-key`, and the `via:` Link is too (`no recorded host key for jump-1 (orion-3's hop to db-9)`), rather than `ok` with a `ProxyJump` naming a Host the source's ssh config doesn't have. `api_version` stays 19. |
| Q92 | Plugin API 3: the reference's follow-ups and Plugins' `:check` sections (tickets 74 and 60, #127 and #101) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **`cmd:` and `env:` Login references are cut to their kind for Plugins** (`cmd:`, `env:`): a reference that says how a secret is fetched is closer to the secret than a vault item's name, a secret pasted into a `cmd:` line would show, and no Plugin needs either (Q81: Plugins never see secrets). The core does it for every `auth` in every roster serve answer a Plugin gets (`roster.inventory.*` and `roster.serve.call`), not per method, so a new method can't leak one. `bw:`, `op:`, `kp:` and the words (`prompt`, `agent`, …) are untouched. **Owner, please confirm**; the other choice was cutting only a `cmd:` line's arguments. **Raw text too (ticket 86, #150):** `changes.diff`'s `diff` shows `roster.yml`'s lines as written and `host.check`'s step messages echo a Login (`cmd:pass show … resolves to one item`), so the core cuts every string of the answer, not just an `auth`: a `cmd:` reference to the end of its line (a closing quote put back), an `env:` one's variable name, wherever one starts the text or follows a space, a quote or `=:,({[`. A Plugin's diff stays a diff (`-    auth: "cmd:"`), so Pending-changes Plugins keep working; the app's own UI gets the whole text. Rejected: making `changes.diff` and `host.check` ungrantable (they're useful and nothing else in them is secret) or only flagged (a flag in the prompt doesn't stop the leak). The cost: any text that happens to hold `cmd:x` or `env:X` (a description, say) reaches a Plugin cut too. (b) **`roster.api_version` is 3**, one number with `init.lua`'s (Q89): `inventory.host` gives `nil` for an unknown name (it raised, code 2: the one breaking change), plus `inventory.groups()`, `keymap.del`, `themes.remove`, `coroutine` and a handler context. A manifest's `api = 2` still installs. (c) **`coroutine` for Plugins only**, inside the same budget: the instruction hook is set as mlua's global hook so a coroutine Lua makes inherits it, and `coroutine.resume`/`close` re-raise running out of time as `pcall` does. `init.lua` doesn't get it (nothing there runs long enough to need one). (d) **Removals:** `keymap.del(context, keys)` takes the Plugin's own binding off; `keymap.set(…, false)` is deprecated and now does the same, since claiming keys with no action had no use a Plugin could explain. Every `remove` checks its id as `set` does (an error, not silence). (e) **Handler context:** a row's, button's, finder item's and status segment's function gets `{ section, row, action }`, `{ finder, item }` or `{ segment }`, and the Session on screen (`session`, `session_host`, named apart from a table row's `host`) only with `inventory.read` or `terminal.read`, the permissions that hear `host_selected`. It travels in the command line as URI-encoded JSON (`plugins call NAME N CONTEXT`), so nothing is kept per handler. Return values are still ignored. (f) **Health sections like `vim.health`:** `roster.health.set(id, { check = function() … end })` is called on each `:check` with `{ health = id }` and returns `HealthCheck[]`; the registry's `checks` may now answer later, every section runs at once, and one that raises, returns bad lines or takes over three seconds (the core's quarter-second budget plus its two-second grace) is an `ERROR` line of its own with a fix pointing at `:plugins`. The page keeps saying "Checking this Box…" until all have answered. No `start`/`ok`/`warn` helper functions as in Neovim: returning the lines fits how every other `roster.*` spec works. (g) **`:check %`** is the on-screen Session's Host (a Quick Connect Session has none: it says so). (h) **`:plugins types PATH`** writes `types/roster.lua` into a Plugin folder (it must exist), over an older copy, through the core's generator, so it always matches the app. (i) **`session_exit` for a closed tab** names its Host (its `code` stays `nil`: there's no exit code). **Iteration 8 review (2026-10-31):** serve's error messages are cut too, on `roster.serve.call`, `roster.inventory` and `roster.serve.request` alike (a message that echoes a Login reached the Plugin whole); a trailing `(code N)` is kept even after a `cmd:` reference that runs to the end of the line, so a Plugin still tells errors apart. **Amended (T107, #187): one set of cases.** The cut lives in two languages (runtime.rs for Plugins, `jsonout.py` for the CLI's `--json`, Q110d), so its cases are one file, `tests/vectors/redaction.json` (input, expected output and a note each), which both test suites read: a change to one rule fails the other's tests until both match. Where they differed: the CLI had no error-message rule (it now keeps a trailing `(code N)` as the core does), and the core took `(code -)` and `(code --5)` for a code (now ` (code ` then one optional `-`, at least one digit and `)`, last in the message; anything else is cut as text). Settled with the file: a bare kind in another case (`CMD: is ready`) becomes the lower-case kind; only ASCII whitespace is a boundary and only `\n`/`\r` end a `cmd:` line (not a no-break space or U+2028); an `env:` name is ASCII letters, digits and `_`. **Amended after the iteration 9 review (2026-11-01):** (k) **Events from serve are cut too:** `job_progress` is serve's `job.progress` notification, and a failed job's `error.message` echoes the Login it was given (a check's `cmd:pass show …` failure), so the core cuts its payload as it cuts `roster.serve.call`'s answers (`heard_payload` in runtime.rs, in `PluginHost::event`), before any Plugin hears it. It's the only event from serve: `inventory_changed` carries nothing, and the others carry the app's Session ids, Host names (letters, digits, `. _ -`) and a Session's output (`terminal_output`), which is the terminal's, not serve's, and so stays whole as (a) says the UI's does. (l) **Keys too:** the cut reaches an object's keys as well as its values (an error's `data` can be keyed by what serve was given); `tests/vectors/redaction.json` gains whole-value cases (`values`) that both suites read. |
| Q93 | Per-Plugin storage: each Plugin's own data folder; Folders as a built-in Plugin (tickets 61 and 82, #104 and #143) | **Owner (2026-10-31): option 2, Plugin-owned files.** Each Plugin gets a data folder of its own under the app's data folder, "so it's nice and clean": it reads and writes only inside it (no paths out of it, no symlinks out), the folder goes with the Plugin (`:plugins remove`, `:packages clean`), and it's never the config folder and never on the network. Folders moves onto it, with `folders.yml` migrated. **Agent's calls within that, for the owner's review ([plugins.md](../plugins.md#a-plugins-own-files)):** (a) **Where:** `<app data>/plugin-data/NAME/`, next to the State and the Packages' clones (`ROSTER_APP_STATE_DIR` moves all three), named after the manifest's `name`. Nothing syncs it or sends it anywhere. (b) **The API is files by name, never paths:** `roster.storage.read`, `write`, `list`, `remove`, `read_json`, `write_json` (api 3, no new api number). A name is letters, digits, `-`, `_` and `.`, not starting with `.`, at most 100 characters, and not a Windows device name (`con`, `NUL.txt`), so there are no folders, no `..`, no absolute paths and no drive letters; Lua still has no `io` and no `os` beyond the clock. (c) **One rule for "stays inside its folder"** (`crates/roster-core/src/plugin/folder.rs`): a link in the data folder (a symlink, or on Windows any reparse point, junctions included) is never read, written or removed through, the data folder itself (and `plugin-data`) may not be a link, and removing a folder that is a link removes only the link. (d) **Limits:** 1 MB a file, 10 MB and 1,000 files a folder, each with an error that names the limit; a write is a temporary file, `fsync`, then a rename, so a crash leaves the old file or the new one; the time the disk takes doesn't count against the Plugin's budget. (e) **No permission:** the folder is the Plugin's own and reaches nothing else, so it isn't in the install prompt (the owner leaned that way); what a Plugin keeps there is as private as its memory. (f) **Lifetime:** kept across reloads, updates, revoked permissions and a Plugin being stopped; removed by `:plugins remove NAME` and by `:packages clean` (through the core's `plugin_forget`), never by anything a Plugin does. (g) **Folders is the built-in Plugin `folders`** (`crates/roster-core/plugins/folders`), on the public API like `hosts`, asking for `inventory.read` (to check and complete Host names and know the Host under the cursor); revoking it leaves Folders working without those checks. That fulfils T23's acceptance example. It needed, all api 3 and open to every Plugin: `roster.echo` (the message line, in a user's call), `roster.cmdline` (opens the command line with one of its own commands typed in, in a user's call), a command's `complete` function (asked as the user types, never a user's call), the Host and row under the Sidebar's cursor in a command's context, Sidebar rows with a glyph, `fold`, `kind = "host"` (drawn as the Inventory's Host), `drop` and `drag_out`, a section's `count` and `drop` (`dropped` in the context), and a finder over a Plugin's own section. A built-in Plugin's settings come from the app: Folders gets init.lua's Folders, which stay read-only. The keys (`␣ a`, `␣ A`), commands, messages, buttons and drag and drop are as before. (h) **Moving `folders.yml` in, once, without loss:** at each start the app reads `folders.yml` (never writes or watches it now) and hands its Folders to the Plugin, which moves them into `folders.json` only while it has none; with no `folders.yml`, the State's older `folders` move the same way. A `folders.yml` that doesn't parse moves nothing and the Folders can't be changed until it's fixed, so nothing is written over it; a Host that isn't in the Inventory moves too. `folders.yml` is left where it is: `:check` says it isn't read any more, for the user to delete. Comments in it don't move. This retires `folders.yml`'s part of Q46: Folders made in the app are no longer a file to hand-edit (hand-written Folders go in init.lua), and `:source` and `auto_reload` no longer touch it. A folded Folder's id is now `folders:NAME` (it was `folder:NAME`); T92 (#161) moves old ids in the State when it's read and lets `:fold folder:NAME` still fold it, so a Folder folded before stays folded. (i) **A Plugin's code stays inside its folder too (T82):** the manifest, `init.lua` and every `require`d module are read only if their real path, after symlinks and junctions, is inside the Plugin's folder, for local Plugins and Packages alike; `:plugins types` won't write through a link. (j) **No serve change, no motion:** serve's API stays as it is; nothing new moves on screen. **T92 (#161), 2026-10-31, agent's calls:** *CI runs roster-core's `cargo test` on Windows* (`Core tests (Windows)`): the core only, with its own Rust cache and the Python it installs for `tests/fake_serve.py` (named `python3` there), `--no-fail-fast` so one run shows every failure. Its first run found one test bug: the stand-in HTTP server in `tests/network.rs` didn't read a request's body, and Windows resets a socket closed with unread data. *Removed:* `config::watch_files`, `Changed` and `config::folders::write` (the app only watches `.lua` files now, through `config::watch`). *A finder's own `prompt`* (api 3, its title if left out; at most 64 characters): Folders' is `Folders`, matching the built-in finders' one-word prompts, and a finder with items no longer showed `›` twice. *Data-folder sizes in `:plugins`* (optional) were left for later: they need a core call and a Tauri command in the Plugin runtime, which another ticket was changing at the same time. **T98 (#171, PR #229) built them:** `:plugins` shows each Plugin's "Data: 2.1 MB in 34 files (of 10 MB, 1,000 files)" (or "none yet", or why it can't be counted), asked once when the panel opens, not refreshed while it stays open. It counts what (d)'s limits count, from the folder's listing and each entry's metadata alone, never opening a file; a link in the folder isn't counted (it can't be written through, (c)), and a folder that is a link can't be counted at all. |
| Q94 | First-party Plugins live in the repo's `plugins/`; Packages from a repo subfolder (ticket 81, #139) | **Owner (2026-10-31): first-party Plugins live in roster's repo under `plugins/NAME/`, and users install them as ordinary Packages named in `init.lua` and pinned in `roster.lock`: the same install path, prompts and permissions as any third-party Plugin (Q81), never bundled in the app (Q80). The agent's calls below are for the owner's review.** (a) **`dir`:** a Package line takes `dir = "plugins/NAME"` (`{ "https://github.com/2styx2/roster", dir = "plugins/topology" }`); the clone is the whole repo, and the Plugin loads from that folder. Without `name =`, the Package is named after the folder's last part, so two folders of one repo are two names. (b) **Safe paths only:** `dir` is relative with `/` between folders; a full path (`/x`, `C:/x`, `\x`, anything with `:`), a backslash, `..`, `.git` or an empty folder is refused when init.lua loads (`packages.N.dir`, errors.md rows 80–81). Once cloned, the folder must exist at that commit and, after following symlinks, still be inside the clone (and not in `.git`); otherwise the Package shows an error and doesn't load (rows 82–83). The check runs at sync, again every time the Tauri side resolves `package:NAME` to a folder (`Packages::loadable`), and before `:packages update` pins a new commit: an update whose folder fails stays on the old commit. (c) **`roster.lock`** keeps `dir` next to `source` (left out when there's none, so older locks read as they are). A lock line counts only for the same repo *and* folder: changing `dir` is other code, so the install prompt asks again. (d) **One clone per Package, not one per repo.** Two Packages from the same repo each get their own clone under `packages/NAME`. Each is pinned, updated, discarded and cleaned on its own, which is what the lock already means (name → commit); a shared clone has one checked-out commit, so pinning one Plugin would move the other, and sharing would need `git worktree` or reference counting for `clean` and `remove`, more moving parts in the code that guards what runs. The cost is disk and fetch time per extra Package, small for a repo of roster's size; `--reference` or a shared object store can come later if it matters. (e) **Prompts:** the install and update prompts read "Unverified: from URL, folder plugins/NAME, at 1a2b3c4", and `:packages` shows the folder with the URL. `:packages update` limits the log, `--stat` and diff to the folder and reads the new manifest from it; a commit that leaves the folder's tree as it was isn't an update (unless init.lua now asks for another tag, branch or commit than the lock pins), so roster's busy repo doesn't prompt for every core change. (f) **`plugins/`** at the repo root has a README (what first-party means, a copyable `init.lua` line, how they're tested, the security-review checklist) and `.luarc.json` for the generated stubs. **CI** runs them in the existing jobs: `crates/roster-core/tests/first_party.rs` (in `cargo test`) installs every folder there as a Package with `dir` from a local git repo and loads it with the permissions it asks for (passing with none yet), each Plugin's own tests go in `crates/roster-core/tests/plugin_NAME.rs` and `app/src/plugins/`, and the Plugin types job runs lua-language-server over `plugins/` as well. No first-party Plugin yet: T40, T44 and T50 add them. (g) **No serve or Plugin API change** (serve's API_VERSION and `roster.api_version` stay as they are). |
| Q95 | Plugin views, tables and Host-details sections (ticket 76, #134) | **Agent's calls within Q81, Q82 and Q87, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **Two modules, one content:** `roster.views` (`set`, `update`, `open`, `close`, `remove`) and `roster.details` (`set(id, host, section)`, `remove`). A view and a Host-details section hold the same three things, drawn top to bottom by the app: `text` (the app's voice), `facts` (label and value, as Host details draws its own) and one `table`. One table per surface, not a list of blocks: the first-party Plugins (Discover, Mesh controls, Topology's list) each need one, and a list can come later without breaking this. A view's `place` is `"panel"` (beside the terminal like `:help`, over it in Focus and narrow windows) or `"full"` (over the Sidebar and workspace, between the title and status bars). (b) **Rows are plain data; what a row does is the table's.** A row is `{ id, cells, host?, tone? }`; `open` (Enter) and `actions` (each a label, a one-letter `key`, an optional icon) are the table's, and a function there gets `{ row, host, view or section }`. So 5,000 rows cost no Lua functions, and `roster.views.update(id, { rows, remove, text, facts, title })` changes rows in place by id (new ones at the end, the rest untouched, the selection kept) as plain data only (a function there is an error). The core drops a view's functions when it's set again or removed, and a section's per Host (owner `details:ID@HOST`), so setting one Host's doesn't break another's. (c) **Opening is the user's call:** `:view ID` (built in, Tab completes), a key the Plugin binds to `view ID`, the `views` finder (`:view` alone; the Picker's sources are the finders) or `roster.views.open` in a call the user started (the core's `Job::Call`, as `roster.clipboard.copy`, Q87c); at load or on an event it's an error, so a Plugin never takes the screen. `roster.views.close` works any time. One view is open at a time; `Esc` closes it and focus goes back where it was; a removed or unloaded Plugin's view closes. (d) **The table** (`app/src/ui/DataTable.tsx` over the pure `ui/table.ts`): columns are `{ id, label, kind, width }` with `kind` `"text"` (Inter), `"data"` (Iosevka, numbers inside sorted as numbers, so `10.0.0.9` before `10.0.0.10`) or `"number"` (Iosevka, right-aligned, tabular). Keys: `j`/`k` and arrows, `g g`/`G`, Home/End, `Ctrl+D`/`U`, PageUp/Down, `s` sorts by the next column (then back to the Plugin's order), `S` reverses, Enter opens, an action's key runs it; a click selects, a double click opens, a heading's click sorts. The table keeps `j k g G s S / ? q` (the last three for a filter and help later); an action can't take one, and one used twice is refused. The selected row is raised like the Sidebar's cursor, never the accent (taste.md: live only); its actions show as buttons on it alone (Q88). A row's `tone` is `"muted"` (dimmed: ended, down) or a small warn or error node before it, never the accent. An `aria` grid with `aria-rowcount`, `aria-rowindex` and `aria-activedescendant`; headings carry `aria-sort`. (e) **500+ rows:** only the rows on screen and 8 each way are drawn, each placed by `transform` in a fixed-height row (measured from the Theme's `--ui-size`), plus the selected row wherever it is (the grid's active descendant); a set or update takes 2,000 rows, a table holds 10,000. (f) **No motion** (the `animate` gate): views open from keys hundreds of times, as every side panel (Q82k); rows that arrive from an update just appear, since a scan streams them and movement in data being read is noise. (g) **Not in this ticket** (T77–T80): the graph view, a table filter (`/`), toolbar controls, confirmations, progress, section actions, timers. |
| Q96 | The Plugin runtime's missing pieces (ticket 79, #137) | **Agent's calls within Q81, Q82 and Q87, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **Timers are ops, with the clock in the frontend:** `roster.defer(ms, fn)` and `roster.every(ms, fn)` return a Timer (the handler's number), `roster.cancel(timer)` takes one away; each sends `{op = "timer", id, ms, every, run}`, and the frontend's `setTimeout` calls the handler back through the same `plugin_call` as an action, so the Tauri side stays five commands with no events (Q82a) and the function runs on the Plugin's own thread and Lua state. The core knows which handlers are timers, so it alone decides a timer's run isn't a call the user started (no clipboard, Q87 g4), and a deferred one is gone once it runs. **Can't starve it:** at most 100 waiting; a deferred function waits at least 50 ms; a repeating one at least 1,000 ms (less is an error, since it would run far less often than asked), and its next wait starts when the last run has answered, so runs never queue behind each other; each run has a call's budget and its errors count toward the three strikes. **Cancelled with the Plugin:** a reload is a new Lua state and the frontend runs the old timers' disposers with the rest of its UI on unload, reload, a revoked permission or a disable. Rejected: a clock in the core pushing ops to the frontend (needs Tauri events, and a second path for ops). **Handler ids are unique per load (ticket 85, #149):** a handler's number carries its load's generation above 32 bits (`HANDLER_BITS` in runtime.rs; ids stay whole numbers to JavaScript for 2^21 loads), so a late timer or a queued click from before a reload finds its function gone ("stale", not the Plugin's fault) instead of running a new, unrelated one that restarted at 1. (b) **`roster.has(permission)`** returns whether it's granted; a permission that can't be granted here (`network`, a disallowed serve method) is `false`, so a Plugin can ask about a newer roster's; a name that's no permission is an error (a typo). (c) **Ops in a fixed order:** a Plugin's `pairs` visits keys in order (numbers, then strings, then booleans, then the rest), with a table's `__pairs` still winning and `next` left as Lua's; it costs a sort per loop, which plain tables in a Plugin can afford. Sorting the ops instead was rejected: order between ops matters (a remove then a set, a notice after a copy), and rows built from `pairs` would still differ. **`{}` vs `[]`:** documented, no marker: an empty table is `{}`, and the app takes `{}` as an empty list wherever it wants one (ops.ts did already). (d) **Lazy finders:** `items` may be a function, run (as `plugin_call`) each time the finder opens (a new optional `open` on a finder source); "Loading…" until it first answers, then the last answer while it asks again. (e) **A taken key:** no free-key query (the core can't see the frontend's keymap without new plumbing); the refusal names what has the key and up to three free sequences like it. **Still no `roster.keymap.free` (ticket 85):** it would need the frontend to send its whole keymap to the core on every change (a sixth Tauri command or an event, and `init.lua`'s and every Plugin's bindings in each Plugin's state) for an answer the refusal already gives; not simple, so not built. (f) **init.lua's layout names** (`modes.*.sidebar.sections`, `statusbar.segments`, `toolbar.controls`) are judged once the Plugins and Packages the app starts with have loaded (or the backend failed), and again as they come and go; Plugins' status segments count. (g) **`?fixture=recent-alerts`**: the Package installed and granted, no vault, two Sessions whose logs have alerts. (h) **`terminal_output` from a terminal's buffer:** each Session's output also goes through an xterm `Terminal` that's never opened (no new dependency), sized as the PTY; a line is sent when a line feed moves the cursor past it, as the terminal shows it then, unless the same text was already sent from that row (a repaint); after a resize the rows above the cursor count as sent, and the alternate screen is never sent. **No opt-in for the alternate screen (ticket 85):** a full-screen program redraws its screen rather than committing lines, so there's nothing line-shaped to send (`top` would be a flood of repaints, `vim` a file's contents scrolling past), and it's where a pager shows what the user chose to look at (`less` on a config file). A Plugin that needs it can ask for a ticket with its case; it would be a permission of its own, flagged in the prompt. This replaces Q87 (h1)'s one-second repaint filter and its cursor-move-as-line-break rule; a line rewritten in place after its line feed isn't sent again (a price of reading the screen). (i) **Versions:** the new functions say `Since: api 3`; `roster.api_version` and the manifests stay 2 until the coordinator sets the number at merge. **Iteration 8 review (2026-10-31):** `pairs` keeps its order at less cost: a sequence (1..n, as the array part gives it) is in order already and isn't sorted, and keys all numbers or all strings sort with Lua's own comparison instead of a Lua comparator (release build, per walk: 10,000-item sequence 8.3 ms to 1.1 ms; 10,000 string keys 12.1 ms to 3.5 ms; 5 keys 1.7 µs to 1.3 µs). Sorting only where ops are serialized stays rejected for (c)'s reasons. |
| Q97 | The `network` permission: an HTTP client in the core, one site per grant (ticket 80, #138) | **Agent's calls within Q81 and Q82(e), for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **One permission per site:** `network:SITE` in the manifest, as `serve:METHOD` names one method, so the install prompt lists each site, `:plugins` turns each off on its own, and an update that adds a site asks again with no new machinery (Q82h, Q83f; T56's gap 1). A bare `network` doesn't install. (b) **Sites match exactly:** `api.example.com` is https on 443 to that name only; `*.example.com` (the one wildcard, `*.` at the start before two names or more) is the names under it, not the domain itself; `http://` before a site allows plain http to it and only that; `:PORT` after it allows that port instead. An IP address is a site of its own, never matched by a name. Lower case, no path, user or password, punycode for other scripts; the URL parser normalises a request's host (`2130706433` is `127.0.0.1`) before matching. (c) **Nothing inside by name:** the core resolves every name itself and refuses it if any address is loopback, private, link-local, shared (CGNAT), multicast, documentation or reserved, or an IPv6 address carrying one (mapped, NAT64, 6to4); the connection then goes to exactly the addresses checked (reqwest's resolver hook), so DNS rebinding can't re-point a site after the check. Such an address is reachable only when the manifest lists the address itself (`network:http://10.0.0.5:8080`), and the prompt says it's on a private network. A name that is meant to resolve inside (a homelab service) isn't supported yet (Q116 adds `private:` sites, pins and the user's proxy). (d) **Redirects** (at most 5) only to a site the Plugin may reach, by the same rule; reqwest drops `Authorization` and `Cookie` when one goes to another site. (e) **Nothing of roster's goes along:** no cookie jar, no proxy (the environment's and the system's are ignored, so no proxy credentials leak, at the price of not working behind a proxy-only network), no `Referer`, no retries (a POST isn't sent twice), no decompression; the Plugin's own headers go, minus those that steer the connection (`Host`, `Content-Length`, `Transfer-Encoding`, `Connection`, `Upgrade`, `TE`, `Trailer`, `Expect`, `Keep-Alive`, `Proxy-*`). Caps: 256 KB out, 1 MB back (checked while reading), 10 s to connect, 30 s in all, 4 in flight per Plugin, `GET HEAD POST PUT PATCH DELETE`. (f) **Async:** `roster.http.request(req, done)` and `roster.http.get(url, done)` return at once; the request runs on two network threads the core starts the first time a Plugin needs them, and the answer runs `done(response)` or `done(nil, why)` on the Plugin's own thread as a call of its own (a call's budget; no clipboard, it isn't the user's call). The host hands what that call did to a listener: the Tauri shell emits it as `plugin-reply`, which the frontend applies like a call's reply. An answer for a Plugin unloaded or reloaded since is dropped, and its requests are aborted with it. The Plugin's slot now exists while `init.lua` runs, so an answer that comes during the load isn't lost. (g) **The client is reqwest (0.13, no default features) with rustls on ring and the OS trust store (rustls-platform-verifier).** reqwest was already in the lock (Tauri's, for mobile) and the desktop app already builds tokio, `http` and `url`, so it adds hyper, tower and the TLS stack; ureq would add about as much (it needs rustls too) and its resolver hook, which the pinning in (c) needs, is in its unversioned API. ring rather than aws-lc-rs: no CMake or NASM on the Windows build. No TLS existed in the tree before. (h) **The frontend never sees a request:** it gets only the ops `done` makes; the webview's CSP is unchanged. The fake Backend mirrors the site rules and risk texts from the core's table (`plugin-permissions.json`). (i) No serve change; `roster.api_version` stays 2 (a Plugin checks `roster.http ~= nil`). (j) **No motion:** no new UI; the prompt shows each site as any other permission. (k) **`roster.has` (Q96):** `roster.has("network:SITE")` answers for that site, and a bare `roster.has("network")` whether any site was granted (whether `roster.http` is there); a malformed site is `false`. |
| Q98 | Plugin interactions: questions, progress, the toolbar, `roster.cmd`, going to a Session (ticket 78, #136) | **Agent's calls within Q81, Q82, Q87 and Q95, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **The API, small and in the existing shapes:** `roster.confirm(question)`, `roster.cmd(line)`, `roster.progress.set/remove`, `roster.toolbar.set/remove`, `roster.sessions.show(session, line?)`; `actions` on a Sidebar section; `actions` and `links` on a float; `when` on a table action; `columns`, `sort`, `desc` and `actions` in `roster.views.update`. All `since: 3`. (b) **Only the user starts them:** `roster.confirm`, `roster.cmd` and `roster.sessions.show` raise an error unless the run is a call the user started (the core's `Job::Call`, as `roster.clipboard.copy` and `roster.views.open`, Q87c, Q95c); at load, in a listener or a timer, never. A question's answer is itself a user call. (c) **The core draws the question; a Plugin can't fake or answer it.** It's an op (`confirm`) whose `run` must be a function: the app calls it only on the user's yes, `cancel` on Esc or Cancel (or another Plugin's question taking its place; one question at a time, the latest). A Plugin's own new question replacing its earlier one doesn't call the earlier `cancel` (ticket 85: documented, not called; the Plugin asked the new one, so it knows, and the core has dropped the earlier functions by then). It's the Remove Host panel's shape with "Asked by PLUGIN" under it, and nothing else a Plugin puts up looks like it (floats and views carry the Plugin's name). No command answers it, so `roster.cmd` can't. **The stronger form** (`danger`): the danger-coloured button and the keys starting on Cancel (as the install prompt, Q87g1), so a stray Enter declines; `typed` adds a word to type first (a Host's name, GitHub's pattern for deleting a repo), for a Mesh Cut. A held Enter answers nothing. (d) **`roster.cmd` reaches only what the Plugin may do:** its own commands (the core tracks `roster.commands.add`/`remove`) and the commands of roster's in api.rs's `COMMANDS` table, which only show something (`finder`, `view`, `help`, `find`, `info`, `changes`, `session`, `next`, `prev`), plus `open` and `connect` with `sessions.open`. Never `:plugins`, `:packages`, `:publish`, `:source`, the editor or the vault, nor another Plugin's commands (they'd run with that Plugin's grants). The table is generated into plugin-api.md and plugin-api.json; the app checks the same rule again (`plugins/cmd.ts`), including that the command named is still roster's own and not one another source took. **The same rule now covers every command line a Plugin hands over** (a row's, a button's, a finder item's, a status segment's, a toolbar control's): they run as `:plugins run NAME LINE`, refused with a notice otherwise, because a button labelled "Open" could run `:plugins grant NAME terminal.write`. **Key bindings too (ticket 88, #154):** a Plugin's `roster.keymap.set` line is bound as `plugins run NAME LINE`, so a key is checked when pressed, as a button is, and `:keys` shows the wrapper and the line (it was bound as written, with `:keys` the only check). (e) **Progress** is data, set any time (from an event too, since it changes nothing but itself): a title, `done`/`total` or neither, and a `cancel`. It shows in the status bar as an edge, never a spinner (taste.md): a 1 px track in the text colour, not the accent (live Sessions own that), filled as far as it's done with a 120 ms linear catch-up between updates, or with a 12 px edge travelling along it (1.4 s, linear, a CSS animation off the main thread) when nothing's known. Reduced motion: the fill steps and the travelling edge becomes a still dashed track. `:cancel [ID]` and the stop button call `cancel` once and show "stopping…" until the Plugin sets or removes it. Hidden with the status bar in Focus; `:cancel` still works. **Amended (ticket 85, #149):** without the status bar (Focus, or the bar hidden) the same items sit in the top-right corner under the title bar, in a quiet panel (no motion of its own), where a terminal's lines rarely reach: the prompt is bottom-left and the notices bottom-right. Several at once give way in order: the latest keeps its title longest (the others shrink first), and under 900 px the older ones show only their track and share (the title stays in the tooltip and the progressbar's name). (f) **Toolbar controls** are toolbar registry entries (Q65) with the Plugin as source, after roster's own by the window's buttons, shown by default unless `modes.<mode>.toolbar` lists ids. An icon from a short list (`find`, `add`, `details`, `help` and a new `map`: three nodes joined), or text alone; never `publish`, `lock` or another of roster's own meanings. (g) **Floats and sections:** a float's buttons (a key each while it has focus; `q` closes) and links (`https`/`http` only, no user info, shown with their host in the data font so where one goes is seen first, opened through the platform's checked `openUrl`); a section's heading buttons are row actions, at most 4, shown on the selected heading (Q88). (h) **Going to a Session** is by the id the events carry, so two Sessions to one Host are told apart; the line is found by its text, from the bottom, in the terminal's own search bar (the user's n/N, Esc), since Plugins never see line numbers and xterm's change as the scrollback trims. A Session that's gone is a notice. It needs no permission: it shows the user what's there and tells the Plugin nothing. So `roster.sessions` is a table for every Plugin now, and only `open` needs `sessions.open`. (i) **Tables (T76's follow-ups):** `/` filters the rows by every word typed across the columns' cells (Enter keeps it, Esc clears it, then Esc closes the view), the foot says "12 of 600 rows"; an action's `when` (cell = value or a list) offers it only on matching rows, from the row's data, so a 5,000-row table still has no Lua per row (a cell needn't be a column); `roster.views.update` changes `columns`, `sort` and `actions`, the new actions' functions owned by `view:ID:actions` so the next update or a removal drops them while `open` stays the set's. The set's own actions are owned by `view:ID:actions` too (ticket 85), so the first update that replaces them drops their functions at once, not when the view is set again or removed. (j) **No motion** but the progress edge: questions, floats and toolbar controls open from the user's key or click like every side panel (Q82k, Q95f). **Iteration 8 review (2026-10-31):** (b) **The user's call is what the user chose:** a command, a key, a row's or button's action, a finder item, a toolbar control, a question's answer. A finder's `items` (run as the finder opens), a `:check` section's `check` and a command's `complete` are the app's, marked so by the core when they're set; a timer is the core's; a question's `cancel` run because another Plugin's question took its place is the app's, which says so (`plugin_call`'s `user: false`, `PluginHost::call_by_app`). So Plugin A's `roster.cmd("finder b")` opening Plugin B's finder gives B nothing: B's `items` isn't B's user call (A chose to open it, not the user), and only the user's pick of one of its items is. A's own command run by A's `roster.cmd` stays A's user call (Folders' drop runs `folder add` that way and answers with `roster.echo`); bounding that chain to one hop needs the core to mark a `cmd` op made in a carried run, a follow-up. **Amended (T105, #183): one hop, as requests (Q103d).** A Plugin's own command that its `roster.cmd` runs from the user's own call is that call carried (`PluginHost::call_carried`, `plugin_call`'s `carried`): it may copy, ask, echo, run a line and act, as a request's answer may. A `roster.cmd` made in a carried run (that command, or a request's answer carrying the call) comes out as `{ "op": "cmd", "line", "user": false }`, and the app runs the Plugin's own command it names as the app's call (`user: false`), which can't; a request made in a carried run doesn't carry the call either. So a chain of a Plugin's own commands, or of requests and command lines in turn, ends one hop from what the user chose. Folders is unchanged: its drop is the user's call, `folder add` is the one hop, and its `roster.echo` still answers. (A first attempt made every command `roster.cmd` ran the app's, which broke that drop; calling the function directly would have fixed Folders but not another Plugin doing the same, and one hop keeps both rules one rule.) The app knows the hop only while the line runs (the command table runs it at once), and only for the Plugin that made the line. (d) **A command named after roster's isn't the Plugin's own:** the core held only the Plugin's added names to `roster.cmd`'s rule, so one that added `open`, `check` or `plugins` (which the app refuses) skipped the core's check; `COMMANDS`' names now keep their rule and `plugins`, `packages`, `publish`, `source`, `config` and `vault` are never a Plugin's own (`cmd.ts` still checks every line against the app's registry). **Amended (T106, #184): no built-in is a Plugin's own.** The app tells the core its built-in commands (`plugin_builtins`, `PluginHost::set_builtins`) before any Plugin loads and again whenever one registers later (App's effects add `:plugins`, `:panel`, `:tutor`, … after the start); the list only grows, so a built-in an effect takes out and puts back stays refused in between. A word is a Plugin's own to run only if the Plugin added it and no built-in has it, and until the app has said them once none of the Plugin's commands runs that way (`:NAME can't run yet`), so a send that fails leaves nothing open. A failed send is said once as a quiet message, retried 1 s, 2 s, … up to 30 s apart, and tried again before each Plugin loads; the list counts as sent only once the core has taken it. The six above (api.rs's `ROSTER_ONLY`) stay refused as ever. Rejected: a hand-kept list of every built-in in the core, which drifts whenever a command is added. A command whose line runs itself, or two that run each other, stop with a notice instead of recursing. **Amended (T135, #241): the user's call waits for the list, it isn't let through.** While no send has got through, a Folders drop (its `roster.cmd("folder add …")`) failed visibly until a retry did. Now a call the user makes (`call` with `user` true; not a carried hop, which comes from a call that waited already, nor the app's) waits first, in the app, until the core has taken the list: every held call waits on one promise, settled when any send gets through (the app's retry, a Plugin's load or the held calls' own try), so they run in the order they were made. While any waits, one loop sends at once and then every second, one send at a time, and its failures don't count toward the app's backing-off retries. Each call waits at most 5 s (`BUILTINS_WAIT_MS`, a timer, so a send that never answers or a clock change can't hold it longer), with at most 8 calls waiting (`BUILTINS_HELD_MAX`; another runs at once). A held call keeps the context the user acted on (the Session on screen then), not the one when it runs. An empty list is a real send. If the core refuses a line with `:NAME can't run yet` after it took a list (a core host built anew), the app sends the list again and waits for it as at the start; the refused call isn't run again. Past either bound the call runs as before and the core refuses the line (`:NAME can't run yet`, errors.md row 157). The core's rule is unchanged, so this widens no trust. Rejected: letting built-in Plugins' own commands through before the list, which needs the core to tell a built-in Plugin from an installed one by name or path alone and would let a built-in's command named after one of roster's later built-ins run as its own; queueing the `cmd` op in the core, which would hold the Plugin's run (and its slot) or replay it after the handler had already done the rest. |
| Q99 | Plugin graph view (ticket 77, #135) | **Agent's calls within Q81, Q82 and Q95, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **A graph is another content of a view,** next to text, facts and table (`roster.views.set(id, { graph = … })`; a view holds a table or a graph, not both), so it opens, closes and is found as every view is (Q95c). As with a table, the data is plain and what a node does is the graph's: `open` (Enter) and up to 8 `actions` with one-letter keys, a function there getting `{ node, host, view }` plus the handler context (the Session on screen, Q92) and a command line going through `:plugins run` (Q98), so 2,000 nodes cost no Lua functions; `roster.views.update` adds or replaces `nodes`, `edges`, `clusters` by id, `remove` takes nodes (with their edges) and clusters (their nodes stay, loose), `remove_edges` edges. Limits: 5,000 nodes, 10,000 edges, 500 clusters, after updates too. (b) **The vocabulary is generic but says what Topology needs (T40 #52):** a node's `shape` is roster's own (`dot`, `ring`, `dashed`, `hollow`, `square`: key, password, unreviewed in Sea glass, ended, this Box in Sea glass; Q53, Q57), its `state` `live` (the accent, the only accent in the graph besides a `live` edge, taste.md) or `down` (faint and crossed with a slash, so not colour alone), a `mark` (warn, error) and a `note` ("out of date", "cut pending"); an edge goes one way (an arrow) or `both`, `solid`/`dashed`/`dotted` (key and password peers), with a `tone` (`live`, `muted`, `warn`, `error`) and `cut` (broken in the middle by an error-coloured cross). An edge's end may be a **cluster**, which reaches every node in it, so a group-to-group Link is one edge, and `mesh = true` on a cluster means every node reaches every other without n² edges (Q63's `mesh: true`). Nodes and clusters share one id space. (c) **Reach** is transitive both ways (a key on a Host reaches what that Host's keys reach), computed on select by a walk (a cluster's nodes as one start for a collapsed cluster), and for the node list all at once from the strongly connected parts and a bitset per part (2,000 nodes: 2 s by walking each, 77 ms so). (d) **Layout: written here, no library.** Each cluster's nodes go on a sunflower spiral (golden angle) in the order given, so its first node is at the centre and each new one goes outside; clusters are packed in rows, loose nodes first. Deterministic and O(n) (2,000 nodes in 40 clusters well under 150 ms in tests), and every position is kept in the State (`graphs`: per `source/view`, the 8 most recent, node positions, cluster centres and the user's folds), so a node placed once never moves: a new node takes the first free spiral slot in its cluster, a new cluster goes in a row below. Considered: d3-force (ISC, ~20 KB with d3-quadtree; a simulation moves every node when one arrives, needs iterations at 2,000 nodes and isn't stable across openings without the same saved positions anyway), dagre (MIT, layered: for flows, not peers in groups) and ELK (EPL, ~1.4 MB, far over performance.md's budget); cytoscape.js would bring its own renderer and styling. Nothing new in `package.json`. (e) **Drawn on one canvas** (graph/draw.ts), not an SVG element per node: batched paths per look, only what's on screen, labels only where they don't overlap (the selected node's, then its reach's and search matches, collapsed clusters' titles, live nodes, the rest), Theme tokens read from CSS (a Theme change redraws). No animation loop at rest (performance.md: no frames at idle). Headless Chromium at 2,000 nodes all open: a pan, zoom or move redraws within one frame (about 17 ms between frames), the node list in 77 ms. Clusters the Plugin says nothing about start collapsed above 300 nodes; a collapsed cluster shows its count, a small accent node if a node in it is live and a mark if one is marked, and its edges join into one with their count (a joined edge keeps a tone only if all its edges share it, else a mark). (f) **Keys** (the graph keeps `h j k l H J K L g G n N o O t / ? q + - = 0`, and `s S`, which sort its node list): `h j k l` and the arrows go to the nearest node in a 90° cone that way, `/` searches for every word typed, as a table's `/` filter (Q98) keeps rows (in the name, id, kind, note, detail or cluster title), and dims the rest, `n`/`N` go through the matches, Esc clears a search before it closes the view, `o`/`O` fold, `+ - 0` zoom and fit, `H J K L` pan, `t` cycles graph, nodes table, edges table (the "equivalent list", DataTable; Enter on an edge goes to its start in the graph). The canvas is `role="application"` with `aria-roledescription="graph"`, and the selected node is said in words through a live region ("esx-02, ring, warning, out of date. Reaches 3 nodes; reached by 2"). Mouse: click selects, double click opens, drag on a node moves it (kept), elsewhere pans, the wheel zooms where the pointer is. (g) **Motion** (the animate skill): moving, selecting, highlighting, zooming and folding are keys, so they're instant. The one animation is an edge that turns `live` drawing in from its start over `--dur-draw` (120 ms, the app's strong ease-out), the connect edge of taste.md, at most 12 at once; under reduced motion (`--dur-draw` 0) it simply appears. (h) **Not here** (follow-ups): a filter that hides rather than dims (by group, type or state, as T40 asks), the graph in a Host-details section, keyboard moving of a node, and the Topology Plugin itself (T40). **Amended by T89 (#155), agent's calls for the owner's review (2026-10-31):** (i) **keyboard moving:** `m` on a node starts placing it: `h j k l` and the arrows move it 16 screen pixels (Shift, or `H J K L`, 64), its selection ring turns dashed, `+ - 0` still zoom and every other key waits; Enter or `m` keeps the place (in the State, as a drag), Esc puts it back, a click keeps it. The live region says "Moving esx-02: …", then "esx-02 placed" or "put back". `m` joins the keys an action can't take (graphs are unreleased, so nothing breaks). No motion: it's keys (the animate skill's gate). (j) **`when` on node actions,** as T78's on table actions, but naming a node's own fields (`id label kind shape state mark note cluster host detail`; any other is refused, so a typo fails loudly); a node without the field doesn't match. The key does nothing and its hint and its button in the node list don't show elsewhere; the node list asks each row the same question (the fields ride along in cells no column shows). (k) **No graph in a Host-details section.** The panel is 320 px (292 inside): with labels about 100 px wide, beyond five or six nodes the map draws unnamed dots (the label rule drops what overlaps), a Mesh Host's reach is often a whole group (hundreds), and the graph's own bar (modes, filter, search, zoom) needs about 760 px. Its keys (`h j k l / f t m`) would also fight the panel's scrolling and App mode's keys inside a side panel. A section keeps the numbers in words (Topology's "Reaches 5 Hosts directly"); the map is a full view one key away. Follow-up: a way for a section's action to open a graph view with a node selected. (l) **Several values per filter dimension** (Q102(i)): each dimension is a menu button (WAI-ARIA menu with `menuitemcheckbox` items) instead of a select: "Every cluster", "lab" or "3 clusters" on it; Enter, Space or ↓ open it on the first ticked item; ↑ ↓ (Ctrl+P/N), Home, End and a letter move; Space ticks and stays, Enter ticks and closes; Esc closes back to the button. A value the Plugin named that the graph doesn't have is listed too, so it can be taken off. No motion. (m) **Cluster titles:** an open cluster's title may be wider than its circle (up to 320 px, or the circle), whole with "· mesh" when that covers no other title, else without "· mesh", else a line higher, else cut to the circle as before; the selected node's cluster's title goes first, then bigger clusters'. Node labels now avoid the titles. A collapsed cluster's title gets 320 px too. (n) **An empty node or edge list** takes the focus itself (a group named "Topology: nodes"), so `t` goes on. |
| Q100 | Discover from the app: a first-party Plugin over serve's `discover.*` (ticket 44, #72) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **The scan runs in `roster serve`**, on a thread of its own, a batch of addresses at a time (32 with nmap, 64 with the built-in scanner): `discover.start` answers at once, `discover.status {since}` hands over what's new, `discover.stop` ends it between batches and kills nmap mid-batch, and `discover.add` writes the kept Hosts in a new group, `reviewed: false`, as a Pending change, as `roster discover --write` would. One scan per serve; ending serve stops it. Serve API 18, codes 120-123. (b) **Polled, not pushed**: a Plugin can't hear serve's notifications, so the Plugin polls once a second (`roster.every`) while a scan runs. (Superseded in part by Q103: a call into serve is no longer held to the Plugin's budget plus two seconds, since waiting for serve doesn't count, and the Plugin asks with `roster.serve.request`; polling stays, Q103f.) (c) **Limits**: 4,096 addresses as asked, as the CLI; up to 65,536 (a /16) with `force`, which the Plugin asks for with the stronger question, the range typed. (d) **Permissions**: five `serve:discover.*` methods; `discover.plan`, `.status` and `.stop` are reads, `discover.start` is flagged as a network action ("Can scan networks from this computer…") and `discover.add` as adding Hosts as Pending changes. (e) **The core holds `discover.start` and `discover.add` to calls the user started** (`SERVE_USER_ONLY` in permission.rs), like `roster.confirm`: a Plugin can't scan or add at load, on an event or from a timer, whatever it was granted. (f) A Plugin's float sits above a full view, so a table action's details show (`styles.css`). **Iteration 8 review, 2026-10-31, agent's calls, fakes only:** (g) **`quiet` is made true for nmap, and documented as 0 for the built-in scanner.** nmap runs without `--open`: that flag only filters nmap's output (host discovery, the port scan and `-sV` do the same work either way; it adds a line of XML per quiet Host), and it was what left those Hosts out. `roster discover` keeps `--open` with `--no-ping`, where nmap takes every address as up. The built-in scanner can't tell a quiet Host from no Host without a ping (root), so its `quiet` stays 0, and the docs say so; telling a refused connection from a timeout would do it, a follow-up if wanted. (h) **IPv6**: nmap takes IPv6 targets only with `-6`, and then no IPv4 ones, so a scan's batches are one family each (`-6` on IPv6 ones), and addresses sort IPv4 first. (i) **An address two targets hold is visited once**: a target another covers (inside it, and not an address it skips, its network or IPv4 broadcast address) is dropped from `targets` and the count, in `roster discover` too, where it was written to `roster.yml` twice. (j) The built-in scanner reads a batch's reverse DNS and SSH banners on 64 threads again, as `roster discover` does, and works out each Host's entry once, outside the scan's lock. `api_version` stays 19: no result's shape changed. **Iteration 8 review (2026-10-31):** (e) is **derived from the install prompt's flag**, not listed: every grantable serve method that isn't a read (`serve_user_only` in permission.rs) runs only in a call the user started, so `mesh.apply`, `mesh.cut` and `host.check` (it connects to the Host) joined `discover.start` and `discover.add`, and a new `mesh.`/`discover.` method waits for the user unless it's made a read. `inventory.write`'s methods (`host.update`, `.rename`, `.move`, `.review`) act on the Inventory and are held the same way. plugins/mesh and plugins/topology ask for an apply or a cut from a question's answer (a user's call), so they work as before. |
| Q101 | The Mesh controls as a first-party Plugin (ticket 50, #87) | **Agent's calls within Q63, Q80, Q81, Q94 and Q98, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **Every Mesh run is a job in serve (API 17):** a Plugin's call into serve may not wait more than its budget plus two seconds (the host disables a Plugin that doesn't answer; superseded by Q103: serve's wait no longer counts, and the Plugin uses `roster.serve.request`), and a plan or apply connects to each Host in turn for minutes. So `mesh.plan`, `mesh.apply` and `mesh.cut` take `background: true` and answer at once with a job number; `mesh.job` says how far it has got (`done` of `total` Host steps, `at`) and its result or error; `mesh.stop` stops it before its next Host, leaving the rest pending (`stopped: …`) as a deadline does. A job has no overall deadline (nothing waits for it). One Mesh run at a time: another is code 111. serve keeps the latest job only. The Plugin polls `mesh.job` once a second with `roster.every` while one runs. (b) **Brief answers:** a meshed group of 500 Hosts is 249,500 Links (12 MB of JSON, past a Plugin's 32 MB once in Lua), so `brief: true` on `mesh.status` and `mesh.job` lists only the Links worth a look (a `via`, or a status other than working or skipped; at most 1,000) and counts the rest (`link_count`, `link_statuses`, `reached`), and marks `no-via` without connecting. The Plugin requires API 17 and never calls the waiting forms. (c) **Permissions:** the six `serve:mesh.*` it calls and `inventory.read` (only for `inventory_changed`, to show "Mesh out of date" after a Publish). `mesh.job` and `mesh.stop` join the methods that aren't flagged (they change nothing on a Host); `mesh.apply` and `mesh.cut` stay flagged. (d) **The UI:** three full views (`mesh`: Hosts with reach, reached from, state; `mesh-links`: the Links worth a look, with `e` explaining a status; `mesh-plan`: each Host's changes), a Mesh section in each Host's details (reach, state, its Links, Cut), badges `pending` and `cut pending`, one status segment (Cut pending, else Mesh out of date, else N pending). Apply needs a plan first (`:mesh apply` without one plans) and asks with the plain question; Cut asks with `danger` and `typed` = the Host. A warning per Host that didn't confirm a cut (five, then a count). No graph: the Topology Plugin draws reach. (e) **A Plugin's question and float come over a full view** (z-index above `.view-full`): a Cut asked from a full view was drawn under it, typed into blind. **Iteration 8 review, 2026-10-31, agent's calls, fakes only:** (f) **serve closing mid-job stops the job at once and records it.** The app closes serve's stdin and kills it a second later (`rpc.rs`), less than one Host's step (up to 120 s), so waiting for the step isn't safe: serve sets the job's stop, ends the ssh under way (`mesh.close`, which also refuses new ones) and waits up to 0.8 s for the job to record what it did in `mesh.json`. The Host it was on is pending (`serve closed: stopped part way`), the rest `stopped`, and the next run finishes them. What a cut-short step leaves on a Host: each file whole, as before or after (`replace` is a temp file and a `mv`); a source whose new key is made but not yet on its peers keeps their old line until that next run (pending, so `mesh.status` says so). (g) A background `mesh.plan` or `mesh.apply` works out the Mesh (`visits_of`) once and hands it to the run, where it was three times, two under serve's lock. **Iteration 8 review, the core's side (2026-10-31):** for (f), the app now waits up to 3 s (`EXIT_GRACE` in `rpc.rs`, was 1 s) for serve to exit on its own after closing its stdin before killing it, so serve has room to stop the job and record it (about 0.5 s, up to 0.8 s) even on a slow Box; a test drops the client on a stand-in serve that takes 1.5 s to wind down and checks it exited on its own. The app takes at most that much longer to close when serve hangs. |
| Q102 | The Topology view as a first-party Plugin (ticket 40, #52) | **Agent's calls within Q63, Q80, Q81, Q94 and Q99, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **`plugins/topology`**, installed as a Package with `dir = "plugins/topology"`, built only on the public Plugin API (API 3): a full view (`:topology`, `:topology GROUP`, `␣ T`, the `views` finder) holding a graph, a Reach section in each Mesh Host's details, a `:check` section and `:help topology`. It asks for five permissions, each for one thing it does: `inventory.read` (the Hosts, their groups, and the Session events), `serve:mesh.status` (the Mesh; never connects), `serve:host.check` (only so `c` may run the app's `:check HOST`), `serve:mesh.cut` and `serve:mesh.job` (Cut, as a job). Without any of them it loads and says what's missing. (b) **Reach is every hop** (`reach = "all"` on the graph): a Mesh key on a Host goes on to whatever that Host's own keys reach, so what someone who got onto it could reach is the transitive set, and that's Q63's visible risk. The direct counts, the ones `roster mesh` reports, are in each Host's Reach section ("Reaches 5 Hosts directly"), so the two numbers are never confused. (c) **A group the Mesh touches is up to three clusters**: its Hosts that take a key and reach each other (the group's own cluster, `mesh = true`), its password peers ("· password peers": reached, never reaching, since roster doesn't write on a password Host) and the rest ("· not in the Mesh": unreviewed, `mesh: false`, not named), so a cluster's mesh and an edge to or from a cluster say exactly who reaches whom; a group the Mesh doesn't touch is one cluster.
