(d) **Few edges for a big Mesh**: this Box has one muted edge to each cluster; Links are grouped by the clusters at their ends (every pair between two clusters is one edge, a Host to a whole cluster one edge, a whole cluster to a Host one edge, the rest one each; both ways with the same look one two-way edge); a Link with `via` is its own edge, labelled, in the error tone when its source has no Link to the hop (no-via, worked out from the Links without connecting); a cut not finished is a dotted, broken two-way edge (it may still be reach). For that, **`mesh.status { grouped = true }` (serve API 19)** says each meshed group once (`meshed: {group: [hosts]}`) and leaves out of `links` the pairs those cover: 2,000 meshed Hosts are 2,000 names instead of 98,000 Links, which a Plugin's quarter of a second can't take in. T50's `brief` (API 17) counts Links instead of listing them, so it can't say who reaches whom across groups; the two don't go together. Before API 19 the Plugin draws from the pairs. (e) **Check is the app's**: a Plugin's call may last about two seconds and `host.check` takes longer, so `c` runs `:check HOST` (added to the command lines a Plugin may run, needing `serve:host.check`, the same capability), and the app's own panel shows the steps. The Plugin doesn't learn the result: a Host is drawn down only when its Session exits with ssh's 255. **Cut** asks the stronger question with `typed` (Q98) and runs as a Mesh job (`background`), polled once a second with progress until it's over. (f) **Data**: no new method for the map, only the grouped `mesh.status`, asked with `roster.serve.request` (Q103) so it never waits for serve, one at a time (and `mesh.cut` and `mesh.job` too); its `:check` section says what serve last answered instead of asking; Sessions from `session_open`/`session_exit` (none before the Plugin loaded), the Inventory read again only on `inventory_changed`, `mesh.status` every 60 s (`refresh`), the first look deferred (nothing calls serve while the app starts), only changed nodes, edges and clusters sent after the first draw (so a node that turns live keeps its place and its edge draws in), Reach sections 400 a call. (g) **Two additions to the graph, generic**: a **filter that hides** (`filter = { clusters, kinds, states }` on the graph and in `roster.views.update`; `f` goes to three selects, one per cluster, kind and state the graph has; hidden nodes and their edges aren't drawn or listed, positions don't move, and reach is still counted on the whole graph; Esc clears a search, then the filter, then closes; a Plugin's new filter replaces the user's; `f` joins the keys an action can't take), and **`reach = "direct"`** for one-hop counts and highlights (the default stays `"all"`). (h) **Motion**: nothing new. Filtering, selecting and moving are keys, so they're instant; the one animation is still Q99's live edge drawing in, off under reduced motion. The filter's selects are the text colour when set, never the accent. (i) **Not here** (follow-ups): Sessions open before the Plugin loaded, a `:check` result a Plugin can read (both done in T96: Q127), a graph in a Host-details section, the filter's several values chosen by the user, and an empty node or edge list keeping focus for `t` (the last two, and sub-cluster titles cut short, done in T89: Q99(l)–(n)). **Iteration 8 review, 2026-10-31:** `grouped: true` listing a meshed group's password peer (`enroll: false`) in `meshed` stands, as (a)'s API note and `docs/mesh.md` say: it's reached and never reaches, and the Plugin draws those as a cluster of their own from the Inventory, so `meshed` doesn't claim every pair is a key Link. |
| Q103 | A Plugin and a busy `roster serve`: `roster.serve.request`, and serve's wait never the Plugin's (ticket 90, #159) | **Agent's calls within Q81, Q92, Q97, Q100 and Q101, for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **The problem:** serve answers one request at a time, so a Plugin's call into serve queued behind the app's own (a Publish, up to 180 s) passed the host's wall clock (budget plus 2 s grace) and the Plugin was disabled, and a call into the Plugin queued behind that one too. (b) **Waiting for serve is serve's time:** each Plugin thread keeps how long it has waited for serve (`ServeWait` in `plugin/mod.rs`, around every `roster.serve.call` and `roster.inventory` read), and the host's wait for a call, an event, a load or an answer moves its deadline by however much of that came after it sent the work. So neither the call that waits nor one queued behind it is given up on; a long C call still is. serve's own deadline per method (`backend::timeout_for`, at most 300 s) bounds the wait. (c) **`roster.serve.request(method, params, done)`** (api 3, `since: 3`; `roster.api_version` stays 3) asks on a thread of its own and returns at once; `done(result)` or `done(nil, "roster.serve.request METHOD: MESSAGE (code N)")` runs on the Plugin's thread as a call of its own, through the same `plugin-reply` path as `roster.http` (Q97f). The same rules as the call, checked when it's made: the method granted, `SERVE_USER_ONLY` (Q100e), params as plain data, `cmd:`/`env:` cut (Q92). At most 8 waiting per Plugin (serve does one at a time anyway; 8 covers a poll, a stop and a start with room), error row 108. An answer for a Plugin unloaded or reloaded since is dropped (the load's generation, as Q97f). (d) **The user's call carries one hop:** the answer to a request made in a call the user started runs as part of that call (it may `roster.confirm`, open a view, copy, run a command line, and request `discover.start`/`.add`); a request made inside that answer doesn't carry it, and one made at load, on an event or from a timer never has it. **Amended (T105, #183):** the hop is one in all, whichever way it's taken: a `roster.cmd` made in that answer runs the Plugin's own command as the app's call, and a request made in a command a user call's `roster.cmd` ran doesn't carry the call to its answer (Q98b). Without this a Plugin couldn't ask a question that depends on serve's answer (Discover's question needs `discover.plan`'s numbers); one hop keeps a Plugin from holding the user's call open by chaining requests. (e) **`roster.serve.call` stays, not deprecated,** with the wait not counted (b): a `:check` section answers when it's run, so it needs a value there and then, and a short read at hand is simpler in a script. The reference says it blocks the Plugin and points to `roster.serve.request`. (f) **No serve notifications for Plugins yet:** serve sends none for a job's progress (only `inventory.changed` and `session.logged_in`), so hearing them would need serve to push `mesh.job`/`discover.status` progress (a serve change, API 20) and a subscription in the API. With the async request a poll costs a Plugin nothing while serve is busy, one question out at a time, so polling once a second stays; a follow-up can add job notifications. (g) **plugins/mesh and plugins/discover use `roster.serve.request`** throughout (Discover's `:check` keeps the call): one job-progress question out at a time, a refresh asked for while one is out runs again after, a Mesh run asked for while the last is still starting says so, and Discover asks `discover.plan` with `force` once (`addresses > limit` picks the stronger question) instead of retrying on code 121, since a retry from the answer wouldn't be the user's call any more. (h) **Tests:** the transcripts keep each step's own ops and then each answer's (`answers`), recorded once every request has answered (`PluginHost::requests`); the fake Backend's host has `serve.request` and `pluginSettled`. No serve change; serve's API stays 18. No UI change. **Iteration 8 review (2026-10-31):** (b) **serve's wait is bounded per call:** one call (a load, an event, an answer) may ask serve through `roster.serve.call` or `roster.inventory` at most 100 times and wait 300 s in all (serve's longest per-method deadline, so one call merely queued behind a Publish still gets its answer), then an error (row 112): without it a Plugin looping sync calls never ran out of time and kept serve, which answers one at a time, from the app. `roster.serve.request` isn't counted (8 waiting at most). |
| Q105 | OSC 133 shell-integration marks for `terminal_output`: a design, not built (ticket 85, #149) | **Agent's design within Q87 and Q96(h), for the owner's review (2026-10-31).** (a) **What they'd fix:** `terminal_output` reads committed lines from a hidden terminal (Q96h), so a line rewritten in place after its line feed isn't sent again, and recent-alerts guesses which line is the shell echoing what was typed (it skips one that looks like a prompt and a command). A shell with integration (fish 4, a prompt such as starship's, or an rc snippet) writes OSC 133 marks: `A` prompt starts, `B` command starts (the prompt ended), `C` output starts, `D;CODE` the command ended. (b) **How:** `committed.ts` registers an OSC 133 handler on each Session's hidden xterm (`parser.registerOscHandler`, no new dependency) and keeps per Session whether it has seen marks. With marks, rows from `A` to `C` (the prompt and the typed command) are never sent, the rows from `C` to `D` are read again at `D` as the screen shows them then and sent once (a progress bar arrives as it ended; a line rewritten in place is right), and `D` adds `{ code }` to a `command_done` event. Without marks, today's behaviour, unchanged. (c) **Typed input stays out, more than today:** the command line between `B` and `C` is what the user typed, echoed by the shell, so it's never sent; that's stricter than now, where the echo is in `terminal_output` like any output. No permission changes. (d) **Trust:** the marks come from the remote Host, as the output does, so a Host can only mislabel its own output; it can't make roster send what it typed. A Session that never sends `D` (a mark lost, a program that ate it) falls back to committed lines after a line limit. (e) **Size and split:** frontend-only (`committed.ts`, the event's fields in api.rs, recent-alerts dropping its guess), about a ticket of its own with transcripts re-recorded; roster can't turn integration on for a remote shell, so docs say how per shell. Not built here: filed as a follow-up. **Built in ticket 94 (#167):** Q121. |
| Q106 | Every panel and view docked, at any width, or full window; view follow-ups (tickets 95 and 91, #168, #160) | **Owner (2026-10-31): "allow the whole view of the screen to be what you want, like the docs could do that, or like Topology".** Agent's calls, for the owner's review. (a) **One frame for all:** `:help`, Host details, Edit, Add, Pending changes, Save to vault, `:check`, Key bindings, `:plugins`, `:packages` and every Plugin view sit in a `PanelFrame` (`app/src/panel/`), so each is docked beside the terminal or full window (over the Sidebar and terminal, under the title and status bars, as a full view was) with the same keys, button and edge. Questions, a Plugin's float and the import diff stay as they are (they're answers, not places). (b) **Keys:** `␣ z` toggles full window for the panel with focus, else the one used last (tmux's prefix z zooms a pane; free in the default keymap and not one of a table's or graph's own keys, which are typed without the Leader), `␣ <` / `␣ >` narrow and widen by 5% of the window (Vim's Ctrl+W < and >); `:panel [toggle\|full\|dock\|wider\|narrower\|width N\|reset]`, which stays in App mode so `>` again keeps widening. Esc in a panel made full window docks it first (a field's own Esc stays the field's). (c) **Width in percent of the window**, not px or columns: it survives window resizes and display scaling and needs no font; limits keep the terminal usable: at least 280 px, and at most what leaves the terminal 400 px (about 50 columns) beside the docked Sidebar; floating (Focus, under 1,040 px) up to all but a 48 px sliver. CSS clamps too, so a window made smaller later never squeezes the terminal. (d) **The terminal fits once, after the drag:** the frame's own width, which the terminal sits beside, changes only on release; while dragging, the panel is drawn at the dragged width over the terminal (ticket 73: a resized Session prints its screen again). Going full window keeps the docked width underneath, and a panel that opens full takes none, so the terminal doesn't change size for either. (e) **Remembered per panel in the State (`panels`), over init.lua's defaults:** `panel.width` and `views.ID = { place, width }` (ids `help`, `details`, …, a view's own id) are defaults, as the owner asked, so a choice the user makes wins over them until `:panel reset`. This differs from `modes.<mode>.sidebar` and `ui.hints`, where init.lua wins: there the file states a fixed preference, here the owner named them defaults, and a drag that a restart undid would read as broken. A Plugin's `place` is the last default. (f) **No motion** (the animate skill's verdict): toggling comes from a key or a click many times a day, and a drag follows the pointer; only the edge's hairline fades in on hover (colour, `--dur-fade`). The handle and the button are chrome: `--line-strong` and `--muted`, never the accent. The edge is a `role=separator` with `aria-valuenow`/`min`/`max` in percent, ← → by 2%, Home / End, a double click resets; the button is a toggle (`aria-pressed`) named "Full window". (g) **Layering is one rule** (`--z-float` 5, `--z-full` 6, `--z-full-top` 7, `--z-over-full` 8 in styles.css), replacing ticket 44's and 50's z-index 7 for floats and questions: a docked panel opened over a full one (Host details from Topology) comes over it, of two full ones the one used last is on top, and a question or a float comes over all of them. (h) **Ticket 91, view follow-ups:** `actions` on a view and on a Host-details section (heading buttons, at most 4, functions getting `{ view }` or `{ section, host }`); `roster.notify(text, level, { actions = { { label, run = "view ID" } } })`, a command line rather than a function so a notice holds none, run as `roster.cmd` would on the user's click, which opens a view when a background job finishes; `RowIcon` `plan`, `apply`, `cut`; `roster.views.update(id, { clear = true })`; a data column's long value cut in the middle (`splitMiddle`, its last up to 8 characters kept); columns keep a minimum width (`MIN_CH`) and a narrow table scrolls sideways; focus goes into a view's table when its rows arrive later, when focus lands on the view (after a question), and back to the table when a float opened from it closes. All `since: 3`; `roster.api_version` stays 3. The Mesh controls use them (Plan and Apply on their views' headings, Cut on the section's, icons, "Show the plan" on Plan ready); their transcript was recorded again. **Not built: a text field in a view.** It needs its own keys and focus rules inside a table's key handling (every letter is a key there), a value handed to Lua on each change or on Enter, and limits; the command line already takes a typed value with completion and history (`roster.cmdline("discover ")`), so it's a ticket of its own if a Plugin needs more. Item 7 of #160 (editing `links:` and `mesh:` from a Plugin) is the owner's call, later. |
| Q110 | `--json` on the CLI's read commands: the CLI is roster's interface for other apps (ticket 102, #180) | **Owner (2026-10-31): roster has no separate API for other apps; the CLI is that interface, and `roster serve` stays the app's internal backend.** Agent's calls within that, for the owner's review. (a) **Which commands:** `hosts`, `info`, `resources`, `check` (fleet, `HOST`, `--box`), `mesh status`, `providers`, `auth-check`; `--json` on `mesh` goes with `status` only (a usage error otherwise), and `reveal` has none. (b) **One shape:** the Host, group and resource objects serve answers with, and `check HOST`, `check --box` and `mesh status` exactly as `host.check`, `box.check` and `mesh.status`; only what serve has no method for (`hosts`' filters and `hidden`, the fleet `check`, `providers`, `auth-check`) has a shape of its own. Hosts come in `roster.yml` order, as `hosts.list`, not the table's group order. (c) **`version` is its own number, 1,** first in every document, not serve's `API_VERSION` (19, unchanged): serve's moves with the app's needs, and a script shouldn't break on a change the app needed. A breaking change is a removed or renamed field, a changed type or meaning, or a changed exit status; a new field isn't. (d) **The Q92 cut for the CLI too:** `cmd:` and `env:` references are cut to their kind in every string of the output, error messages included, by a port of runtime.rs's `redact_text` (`jsonout.py`), so a new command can't leak one. The person at the terminal can still read `roster.yml` and `info`'s table; JSON is what gets piped into other tools and logs. `auth-check --json` fetches each secret and reports only `ok`/`fail`/`ask`. (e) **Errors:** stderr `{version, error: {code, message, fix}}`, nothing on stdout, with serve's codes 1 (`roster.yml`, with its fix), 2 (unknown Host or resource) and 3 (anything else, wrong arguments too, which keep exit status 2). An unknown Host for `check HOST` stays a failed `inventory` step, as `host.check` has it. A filter that matches nothing is an empty list and exit 1, not an error. (f) **Format:** compact, one document per line (as serve's), no stderr warnings: each Host's `problems` carry them. (g) **Docs:** a Machine-readable output section in cli-reference.md, which the man page now includes (JSON OUTPUT), and `ROSTER.md` tells Claude to prefer `--json` when it reads roster's data. **Amended (T107, #187):** (h) **`discover --json`** is the preview only (with `--write`, a usage error, exit 2): `{version, targets, addresses, ports, scanner, count, quiet, found, group}`, `discover.plan`'s numbers and `discover.status`'s Found records after the scan (one function makes a Found for both, so they can't drift), and `group`, where `--write` would put the new Hosts. A run with no state of its own, so no `scan`, `state`, `done` or `total`. `addresses` counts what's visited (a /24 is 254), in the text mode's limit too. Too many addresses is serve's code 121, a Problem with its fix. (i) **`mesh --dry-run --json`** is `mesh.plan`'s Mesh result as it is (`applied: false`), exit 1 with a Host pending, as without `--json`; a refusal keeps serve's code (110). `--json` never goes with an apply or `cut`: a script reads, a person changes. **Amended after the iteration 9 review (2026-11-01):** (j) **The cut is over plain JSON:** `emit()` makes the payload plain JSON first (`json.loads(json.dumps(x, default=str))`: a set, a path or any other object becomes the text `json.dumps` makes of it, every key a string) and then cuts every string, a key as much as a value (Q92l). Before, it cut only strings inside dicts, lists and tuples and dumped the rest with `default=str` afterwards, so a set, a path, an object and every key reached stdout uncut. The no-secrets test now has a Host with a key and a set that hold a `cmd:` line. |
| Q112 | `roster serve` answers requests concurrently, with a job manager; pushed job progress and `:check` sections that answer later (ticket 101, #177, folding in T93 #164) | **Owner (2026-10-31): keep the Python backend (no rewrite); make serve multi-threaded with real jobs, for performance and so the app and Plugins stay responsive.** ADR 0001 stands (the app reaches roster only through serve); its amendment note says serve is no longer one request at a time. Agent's design within that, fakes only, for the owner's review. (a) **Workers:** a reader thread hands each request to a pool: 8 workers for everything, plus 2 of their own for the quick reads (`inventory.info`, `hosts.list`, `host.get`, `mesh.status`, `mesh.job`, `mesh.stop`, `discover.plan`, `.status`, `.stop`, `jobs.list`, `jobs.stop`, `inventory.watch`, `vault.forget_typed`), which touch only memory and take no lock that a long call holds, so they answer at once while 8 slow calls run. Answers can come back in another order than asked; JSON-RPC's `id` says which is which (the core's `rpc.rs` matched by id already; each Tauri command is one `invoke`). Each method's deadline is unchanged (`DEADLINES`, thread-local as before); the app's waits are unchanged. (b) **Locks, per resource, in one order** (`LOCKS` in serve.py, a row per method): **the Inventory** (`roster.yml`, its git repo, and the loaded Inventory) is one lock that every write holds for the whole method, so two writes never interleave and a Publish commits exactly what was written: `host.update`, `.remove`, `.rename`, `.move`, `.review`, `.add`, `quick.save`, `host.save_login`, `group.*`, `discover.add`, `changes.diff`, `.discard`, `.publish`, `inventory.pull`, `.reload`, and the watcher's reload of an outside edit; reloading for a read (`fresh`) takes it for the reload only, so `host.verify_login` and a Mesh plan don't hold it over the network. **The vault** is a readers-writer lock: unlocking, locking, signing in or out and `vault.configure` take it alone; everything that looks a secret up, makes an item or mints an askpass token (`session.prepare`, `quick.prepare`, `host.check`, `host.verify_login`, `host.add`, `quick.save`, `host.save_login`, `vault.status`, `.targets`, `.create_item`, `.undo_create`) shares it, so `vault.lock` waits for them and nothing holds a secret or a token once it has answered, as before. Order: a Mesh or Discover job's slot, then the Inventory, then the vault (`record_host_key` has a lock of its own). **A lock not free within the method's deadline** (or 50 s for a method without one, under the app's 60 s) is the new code **4**, "roster.yml is busy: changes.publish is still running", and nothing was done. (c) **`host.get {settled: true}`** (API 20) waits for the Inventory lock: the app's check after a timed-out `host.add` asked straight after it, which answered after the add while serve answered in order, and would answer before it now. `changes.diff` takes the lock anyway, so the check after a timed-out Publish waits for it. (d) **The job manager** (`jobs.py`) owns all background work: Mesh plan, apply and cut (with or without `background`: the waiting form waits for its job, so one Mesh run at a time holds either way) and Discover scans. A job has a kind (`mesh`, `discover`), an id within it (the Mesh's job number, the scan's `scan-N`, so `mesh.job` and `discover.status` are unchanged), an action, a state (`running`, `stopping`, `done`, `stopped`, `failed`), progress (`done` of `total`, `at`), a result or error, and a stop. One job of a kind at a time (111, 120 as before). `jobs.list` lists them; `jobs.stop {kind, job}` stops one; neither is grantable to a Plugin (no `mesh.`/`discover.` prefix), so no permission changes. **On serve's exit** it stops every running job at once (a Mesh job ends its ssh, `mesh.close`, and records what it didn't do in `mesh.json`; a scan kills nmap), waits up to 0.8 s for them together, and logs each to stderr; the core's 3 s grace (`EXIT_GRACE`) is unchanged and still enough. (e) **Pushed progress:** serve sends the notification **`job.progress`** `{kind, job, action, state, done, total, at, host?}` when a job starts, at most 5 times a second while it moves, and always when it ends. No result in it (a plan can be megabytes): the kind's own method has it. (f) **The Plugin subscription:** the event **`job_progress`** (Plugin API 3, `since: 3`) with that payload, forwarded by the app from serve. A Plugin hears a job only if it may already ask about it: a Mesh job with `serve:mesh.job`, a scan with `serve:discover.status` (the core filters each event by its kind). It tells the Plugin nothing a poll didn't. plugins/mesh, plugins/discover and plugins/topology follow their jobs by it and ask once at the end; each keeps its once-a-second poll only until the first `job_progress` arrives (a serve before API 20 sends none). (g) **Fairness:** serve can't tell a Plugin's request from the app's, so the core holds each Plugin to 2 calls into serve at once and all Plugins together to 4 (serve's 8 workers minus room for the app); a call waiting there is serve's wait (Q103b), so no Plugin is disabled while queued. `MAX_SERVE_REQUESTS` (8 waiting) and Q103's 100 calls and 300 s per call are unchanged. (h) **`:check` sections that answer later** (T93 item 2): a `check` may return `"later"` and answer with `roster.health.answer(e.check, lines)` from a request's `done`, within 30 s; the app shows the section once it answers, or an ERROR line after 30 s. plugins/discover's section asks `discover.plan` with `roster.serve.request` that way, so `:check` no longer blocks its thread behind a Publish. (i) **Serve API 20.** The app sends `settled` (an older serve ignores it and answered in order anyway), forwards `job.progress` (an older serve sends none), and the Plugins fall back to polling. (j) **Not here:** cancelling a request that is already running (only jobs stop), a per-Plugin lane inside serve (serve never learns who asked), and moving `host.check` into a job. **Amended after the review of PR #188 (2026-10-31):** (k) **Locking always succeeds:** `vault.lock` and `vault.logout` don't claim the vault up front; they forget every secret, askpass token, held typed password and sign-in at once (so a lookup from then on finds the vault locked), then wait with no limit and no code 4 for the requests that already share the vault (a waiting lock goes before new readers), then forget again what those held or made meanwhile; `vault.logout` signs the CLI out while it still holds the lock. Before, they could answer 4 after 50 s with every secret still in memory while the app showed its lock screen. (l) **One order, everywhere:** `host.verify_login` reloads `roster.yml` before it shares the vault (it claimed the vault first, then waited for `roster.yml`: with `host.add` holding `roster.yml` and waiting for the vault behind a waiting `vault.lock`, none ended). A reload for a read (`fresh`) waits for a write at most what's left of the request's deadline, then 4; a Mesh cut's `mesh: false` write too, and then its `change` says `roster.yml` was busy (the cut itself happened). A writer that gives up wakes the readers it held back. (m) **Exit order:** once stdin closes serve refuses every request still queued ("roster serve is closing", never run) and gives the running ones up to 1 s, then ends a sign-in and stops the jobs; a sign-in that starts or takes a step while serve closes ends its own `bw login`. Before, a queued `vault.login` could run after the clean-up and leave `bw login` alive with the credentials in its environment. A job's thread starts before the job is visible, so closing never meets one not started. (n) **Small races:** `inventory.watch` starts one watcher however many ask at once, `vault.undo_create` takes the item before it deletes it (two at once delete it once; the second is 63; one the vault refused can be tried again), and a worker outlives whatever a request raised. (o) **Fair shares, kept and in order:** a Plugin's share of serve is kept by its name across reloads, so the calls an earlier load left running still count, and callers get in in the order they came (a ticket each), so none is passed over. **Amended for ticket 108 (#189, 2026-11-01), the follow-ups of (j); serve API 21:** (p) **Cancelling a request:** `$/cancel {id}` (LSP's `$/` prefix: a protocol message, not a method a Plugin can be granted, and the ids are the core's own, so a Plugin can never cancel the app's requests). serve answers it on the thread that reads requests, so it works with every worker busy. A request still **queued**, or **waiting** for a lock (or for an unlock, (r)), never runs: it answers the new code **5** and nothing was done. Once **running**, only the waiting forms of jobs stop (`host.check`, and `mesh.plan`, `.apply`, `.cut` without `background`): their job stops at its next step or Host, as `jobs.stop` would. Everything else that runs, and so everything holding `roster.yml` or the vault, runs to its end: nothing is half-written, and the app's check after a timeout (`host.get {settled}`, `vault.status`) sees what it did. `vault.lock` and `vault.logout` are never cancelled ((k)). The core (`rpc.rs`) sends it as a notification (no answer) every time it stops waiting for a call; an older serve ignores it. (q) **`host.check` is a job** (kind `check`, ids `check-N`, one per Host at a time: a second ask while one runs joins it). The job, not the request, shares the vault, and `vault.lock` stops a running check at its next step rather than wait up to 45 s for it (the rest `skipped`, `ok` false). It sends `job.progress` as each step starts (`at`: the step; `host`: the Host; never a step's message). The waiting form answers as before; `background: true` answers at once and `host.check_job {job}` (a quick read, new code **49** for an unknown id) has the result. The core checks a Host that way from API 21 (asking `host.check_job` every 200 ms, and `jobs.stop` past its 90 s), so no worker of serve waits for a check. A Plugin hears a check's `job_progress` with `serve:host.check_job` (a read, grantable, not flagged), not with `serve:host.check`, which is user-call only and so could never be used from a listener. (r) **An unlock is never overtaken:** `vault.unlock` has a worker of its own, so it never queues behind 8 busy requests, and `vault.status` waits for every unlock sent before it, queued or running (at most its wait, then 4; a cancelled one is over at once). (s) **Vault items one at a time:** making or deleting a Vault item (`vault.create_item`, `.undo_create`, and the saves and undos of `host.add`, `quick.save`, `host.save_login`) holds a lock per provider, inside the shared vault lock (the order is now a job's slot, `roster.yml`, the vault, then a provider's items). bw keeps its local cache in one `data.json` and pass commits each insert to its git repo, so neither is known to take two writes at once; and two creations of one name would both pass the "no item by that name" check. Its wait is the request's, then 4. (t) The Publish tests that hold a push in the upstream's hook run on native Windows too (Git for Windows runs an sh hook), and hold it with a file the test writes instead of a sleep. **Amended after the iteration 9 review (2026-11-01); serve API 22:** (u) **A check shares the vault only for what needs it:** a password Login's `provider`, `vault-item` and `auth` steps (`diagnose.VAULT_STEPS`: the steps that fetch a secret or mint an askpass token), each on its own, and nothing between them; a key-login Host's check never takes it. It keeps no secret between steps (the `auth` step looks the password up again from this run's cache, under the vault, to mask it in ssh's output). So `vault.unlock` and `vault.lock` wait at most for the one vault step under way, never the rest of a check's 45 s: before, an unlock waited behind a whole check and the `vault.status` after it gave up (4) while the lock screen hung. A check that was stopped while waiting for the vault skips that step and the rest. (v) **Locking stops checks and outwaits the other readers:** `vault.lock` and `vault.logout` still forget everything at once and never fail (k); they stop running checks (no further step starts) and wait for whatever else shares the vault right now (`session.prepare`, `quick.prepare`, `host.add`, `quick.save`, `host.save_login`, `host.verify_login`, `vault.status`, `.targets`, `.create_item`, `.undo_create`). Each of those ends within its deadline or its vault CLI's timeout, and most write (an item, `roster.yml`), so stopping one could half-write it; forgetting first means none of them can fetch a new secret meanwhile. Mesh jobs and scans never share the vault, so a lock never waits for them. (w) **A shared check stops only when nobody wants it:** each request that asks for a check holds it: a waiting `host.check` until it answers, a `background: true` ask until the check ends. Cancelling a waiting form (p) lets its hold go, and the check stops only when no waiting request and no background ask holds it; a waiter that joined another's check and was cancelled answers when that check ends. The background answer says `joined` (API 22), and the core stops a check it gave up on (past its 90 s) only when it started it (`joined: false`; an older serve says neither, so it never stops one). (x) **A stopping check doesn't block a new one:** one per Host counts only a `running` check, so a check asked for after a lock stopped one is a new check (it joined the stopping one and answered `ok: false`, its steps skipped). Mesh runs and scans still count a stopping job as running. (y) **Replaced jobs are still found:** a job a newer one of its slot replaced stays findable by id among the last 32 (`host.check_job` answered 49 for a check that had succeeded once another check of its Host started, while the core was between polls), and closing stops those too. (z) **Small races:** `vault.undo_create` puts the item back on any failure, not only the vault's own (it was forgotten while still in the vault, and a retry answered 63), and its unexpected failures answer -32603 naming only the error's type; `vault.configure` walks a copy of the providers, so a reload registering one meanwhile can't break it (each provider's settings are replaced in one step and a lookup reads only its own provider's, so a reload never shows one half-updated). serve's code-4 errors come from one helper (`busy_error`) and its closing error from another (`closing_error`), and `discover.Scan` is a plain class (serve's job runs it). |
| Q113 | Rewrite the CLI in Rust? Type safety in Python instead (ticket 112, #196) | **Owner (2026-11-01): no rewrite now.** (a) **Checked types:** a type checker in CI (strict for new or rewritten modules) and Pydantic models for roster's data (the Inventory, serve's params and results, `--json`); every new data shape is a model, and existing dicts move over when a ticket touches them. (b) **Rust where Python falls short:** a feature Python can't do well (speed, concurrency, packaging, or something the app's core already does) is written in Rust in the Cargo workspace, not copied into Python; the first ticket that does this decides how the CLI reaches it (PyO3 module or helper binary). (c) **A full rewrite is revisited once the app has matured**, or sooner if installing Python blocks users, serve keeps hitting concurrency bugs, or the app needs to ship without Python. Builds on Q112. See [ADR 0009](../adr/0009-python-cli-rust-where-it-falls-short.md). (d) **How T112 did it (agent's calls, for the owner's review):** **pyright, not mypy**: it checks the bodies of unannotated functions in basic mode (mypy skips them unless told to), so the 12,000 untyped lines get checked today, and a per-file `strict` list is one setting; pinned in the dev group with its own Node (`pyright[nodejs]`), so CI and every box run the same checker. **Strict list:** `models.py` alone, the one new module; the others join it when a ticket rewrites them. **The models are a module of their own (`models.py`) that config.py fills**, not a rewrite of config.py: config.py keeps its checks and their messages (no test's expected error changed), then validates what it built into the models, which catch what it didn't check (`workers: lots` was a raw traceback). **`load_inventory` returns the models and `load_config` their dict view** (`Inventory.as_config()`, the same keys as before): serve's `hosts.list` and the `--json` commands send those dicts as they are, so moving 300-odd `h["…"]` reads at once would be the sweep (a) rules out; they move to the models when a ticket touches them. Host keys roster doesn't know stay on the Host (extra allowed); every other model refuses unknown keys, and all are read-only. **Pydantic is imported only when roster.yml is loaded**: it costs about 75 ms, twice `import roster.cli`. **ruff `S`**: `S603`/`S607` are off (every call runs a program from PATH as an argv list, roster's job), every other finding is fixed or has a noqa with its reason; `roster web` now refuses a URL that isn't http(s) before urlopen. (e) **How T174 and T176 did the first of serve's results and every `--json` document (#332, #334; agent's calls, for the owner's review):** **Two modules of their own:** `servemodels.py` (serve's params and results: `hosts.list`, `host.get`; the other methods move in as tickets touch them) and `jsondocs.py` (one model per `--json` document, the shapes from `mesh`, `diagnose` and `health` included, modelled there rather than by changing those modules: mesh.py is another ticket's). `jsondocs` is imported only when a `--json` command prints (about 8 ms over Pydantic), so `import roster.cli` loads neither (a test checks). **Same bytes, not just the same keys:** each model's fields are in the order the dicts had them, an optional key (`code?`, `fix?`, `line?`, a Link's `via`) is left out when it isn't set (`exclude_unset`) rather than sent as `null`, and the Host, group and resource are models.py's own. Every document and both methods' answers were recorded across the whole pytest run on dev, plus each local `--json` command and `hosts.list`/`host.get` (good and bad params, a broken `roster.yml`) against `examples/`, and replayed on the branch: 152 of 152 identical once temp paths and ephemeral ports are masked. **Strict:** no coercion (`"yes"` isn't `true`, a tuple isn't a list), unknown keys refused, `hide_input_in_errors` so a validation error never shows a value, and `repr=False` on Login references and on the messages that may quote one. **The producers still build dicts that the models then check** (`Model.model_validate(...)`): making `mesh`, `diagnose`, `health` and `discover` build models is their own move under (a). **serve's `State` keeps the loaded `Inventory` (`state.inv`) beside its dict view**, so `hosts.list` and `host.get` dump the models roster.yml loaded into; bad params keep their exact messages (`params.name must be a non-empty string`), from each params model's `MESSAGES`, and keys a method doesn't know are still ignored. `jsonout.emit` takes only a model (a dict is a `TypeError`); the redaction runs on its dump, unchanged. **Docs from the models where practical:** docs/serve-api.md's object tables (Host, SshLogin, Login, Service, ServiceProblem, Group, `hosts.list`'s `error`) are generated from each field's `description=` (`<!-- BEGIN generated:models ... -->`; a field without one fails the generator); a method's Params/Result row stays its docstring, since its prose says more than a field list would. **Strict for pyright:** `servemodels.py`, `jsondocs.py` and `jsonout.py`. `cli.py`, `serve.py` and `discover.py` stay in basic mode and on T175's list (#333): only their JSON output moved, not their 300-odd dict reads. API_VERSION and JSON_VERSION are unchanged. |
| Q114 | Serve concurrency follow-ups: cancel a running request, `host.check` as a job, the unlock's own lane, Vault items one at a time (ticket 108, #189) | **Recorded in Q112 (p)–(t).** Ticket 108 was given this number, but its calls amend Q112's job manager, so they were written there (PR #192, serve API 21); this row only says where they are, so the number doesn't read as unused. In short: (p) `$/cancel {id}` stops a request still queued or waiting for a lock (code 5, nothing done) and the waiting forms of jobs; anything else that runs, runs to its end, and the core sends it whenever it stops waiting for a call. (q) `host.check` is a job (`check-N`, one per Host, a second ask joins it), with `job.progress` per step and `background: true` plus `host.check_job` (code 49); the core checks that way from API 21, and a Plugin hears a check with `serve:host.check_job`. (r) `vault.unlock` has a worker of its own, and `vault.status` waits for every unlock sent before it. (s) Making or deleting a Vault item holds a lock per provider, inside the shared vault lock. (t) The Publish tests that hold a push run on native Windows too. The reasons, and what the iteration 9 review amended ((u)–(z)), are in Q112. |
| Q115 | A quiet message line, as Neovim's; `ui.messages` and `:messages` (ticket 111, #194) | **Owner (2026-11-01): tips like "Sidebar hidden, and kept that way. modes.standard.sidebar = false in init.lua makes it the default" should be off by default, with an option to turn them on; widened the same day: "we can have errors show like Neovim; the notification of every action can be a little distracting."** Agent's calls, for the owner's review. (a) **Always shown** (plain text): errors and refusals ("No Host named …", "… needs a newer roster", "Couldn't …"), warnings (init.lua's vault settings left out, a broken roster.yml, a save's "But …"), the answer to something asked (`:info`, `:config path`, "Nothing to discard.", "… is already reviewed", "Nothing changed on …", `:panel`'s "as wide as it goes"), and results that need attention: a Publish, a pull's result, "Wrote Windows Terminal's profile…", "Created init.lua…", a Host added unreviewed (it can't be opened yet), a move that keeps a group's settings on the Host, a group deleted with Hosts moved, `:source` failing, `:theme`'s note that init.lua also sets `theme`, What's new, `:plugins` and `:packages` results, `:cancel`'s "Stopping …", and whatever a Plugin says (Q126 narrows these last two to Neovim's rule: a Plugin's info notice without buttons, its echo marked `quiet`, and `:cancel`'s "Stopping …" are quiet; its warnings, errors and answers still show). (b) **Quiet by default:** a toggle's tip or confirmation (the Sidebar's "kept that way" and "for now: init.lua sets …", the status and Session bars, `:hints`, `:panel`'s place, width and reset) and a routine action's confirmation when nothing went wrong (a Host saved, added, saved to the vault, marked reviewed or unreviewed, removed, renamed or moved; a group added, renamed or deleted; Pending changes discarded; init.lua opened; a service opened in the browser; "Reloaded init.lua"; "Pulling…" and "Checking the Packages…"; a Plugin removed; a tutorial started over). Focus, `:mode` and the terminal's zoom already said nothing; "Copied" is a button's own label or a Plugin's notice, not the message line. (c) **`ui.messages = "quiet" \| "all"`, not `ui.tips`:** what's quiet is more than tips, and a level reads better than a boolean named after one kind. Not `"errors"`: answers and results show too. Same precedence as `ui.hints`: unset by default (quiet), `:messages all\|quiet\|toggle` decides and the State keeps it (`messages`); set in init.lua, it wins and `:messages` says to change it there. `:messages`'s own answer always shows, so going quiet still says so. (d) **`:messages` alone lists the recent ones, as Neovim's:** the last 200, quiet ones too (muted), oldest first, with the time, in a built-in view (`messages`, docked; `:messages clear` empties it). It's listed in the `views` finder like any view (taste: built-ins are registry entries). (e) **Screen readers (WCAG 4.1.3):** quiet, the message line (a live region) keeps a short form of what changed ("Sidebar hidden", "Saved esx-02", "Help: full window") as visually hidden text, so it's heard without being seen; with `"all"` it says the whole line. (f) **No motion change**, and nothing new drawn on the message line: it stays out of sight after a quiet message. |
| Q116 | Plugin network follow-ups: private sites, pinned certificates, the user's proxy, request counts (ticket 87, #151) | **Owner (2026-11-01): build all four: (1) a separate private-site form for names that resolve inside the LAN, with its own install warning; (2) an explicit, credential-free proxy setting; (3) a pinned certificate / SHA-256 fingerprint in the manifest for self-signed LAN appliances; (4) per-Plugin request counts by site in `:plugins` (no bodies, no secrets). Item 5 (one push path for async primitives) only if it fits.** Agent's design within Q81 and Q97, for the owner's review. Nothing changes for a Plugin that asks for none of it. (a) **Each is part of the `network:` permission itself**, so the install prompt, `:plugins`' switch per site, "an update that asks for more asks again" and `roster.has` need no new machinery: a new private site or a changed pin is a new permission. `roster.api_version` stays (no new Lua function); an app older than this refuses such a manifest as a malformed site. (b) **`network:private:NAME`** (`private:http://NAME:PORT` too) is a name, one label allowed (`appliance`), never an address (that's a site already) or a wildcard, never `localhost`. Every address it resolves to must be on a private network (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 100.64/10 for CGNAT and overlay VPNs, fc00::/7, and IPv4-mapped ones of those); loopback, link-local (cloud metadata), multicast and public addresses refuse it. The core's own resolver checks every address and the connection goes only to those (Q97c), so rebinding can't point it outside or at this computer. A name a private site lists is judged only by the private sites (a public `*.example.lan` or another port can't reach it), and a public name only by the public ones, so a public name still never reaches a private address; one manifest can't list a name both ways (it doesn't install). The prompt adds "It reaches inside your private network…". (c) **A pin is `#sha256:` and the certificate's SHA-256 (64 lower-case hex digits) after an https site** (a name, a private name or an address; never plain http or a wildcard), the fingerprint a browser or `openssl x509 -fingerprint -sha256` shows. Each pinned site gets a client of its own whose rustls verifier accepts only an end certificate with that hash for that server name, and still checks the handshake signature with that certificate's key, so a copied certificate without its key fails; issuer, name and dates are not checked (an appliance's expired certificate still works; a new one needs a new pin). It replaces OS trust for that exact site only: the shared client keeps the OS trust store, a request picks the pinned client by its exact site (before a wildcard over it), and no redirect leads to a pinned site or from it to another, so neither trust ever stands in for the other. One site has one pin or none in a manifest. The prompt shows the fingerprint's first 16 digits. The end certificate's hash rather than its key's (SPKI): it's what people can read off a browser or the appliance's page. (d) **`plugin_proxy = "http://HOST:PORT"` in init.lua is the user's, never a Plugin's:** the network edge belongs to whoever runs the computer, a Plugin choosing a proxy could send its traffic somewhere of its choosing, and a manifest field would be one more thing to prompt for. Only `http://` (https still goes end to end through it, as a CONNECT tunnel), never a user or password (refused, and the error doesn't repeat it), `Proxy-*` headers stay roster's, and only requests to public names use it; a private site and an address go direct. The proxy can't be trusted to keep Q97's "nothing inside by name", so the core still looks a public name up first, at the request and at every redirect, and refuses it exactly as without a proxy if any address is non-public; only when this computer can't look the name up at all (a network where only the proxy resolves names) does the request go to the proxy unchecked. That residual case, and the moment between roster's lookup and the proxy's, are the proxy's to get right, and the option's docs say so. The proxy's own name may resolve anywhere (it's the user's). The environment's and the system's proxy settings are still ignored. `plugin_proxy` needs its port written out. Each load is handed the setting (`PluginHost::load_with_proxy`, no shared host state), and a Config reload hands the new one to every running Plugin (`set_proxy`): its client builds its connections again at the next request, and a request going finishes as it began. A proxy that needs a login isn't supported. *Note (2026-11-03): superseded in part by Q161: the proxy is now asked for a tunnel to the address roster checked, never the name, and a name this computer can't look up isn't sent.* (e) **Counts**: the core keeps, per Plugin name and for the app's run (across reloads, never on disk), each granted site's requests sent, those with no answer (an HTTP error status is an answer), and when the last went, keyed by the site as granted, never by URL, so no path, query, header or body is kept. A request an unload or reload stopped counts as one with no answer; removing the Plugin (`forget`) drops its counts, so a new Plugin by that name starts at nothing. `PluginHost::requests_by_site` and `Status.requests`; the Tauri command `plugin_requests`; `:plugins` asks when it opens and every 3 s while it's open (the same counts redraw nothing) and shows one line under each granted site ("12 requests, 1 with no answer, the last 2m ago"), and per-Plugin lines sit in one list under the permissions for later ones (T98's data-folder size). (f) **Item 5 isn't folded in**: an HTTP answer is pushed by the core (`plugin-reply`) while a timer is kept by the frontend, and joining them means moving timers into the core or answers into the frontend's loop, neither of which this ticket needs; it stays a follow-up. (g) No motion: the counts are text in a panel opened from a key. Rejected: a separate `pins` manifest field (a second place to prompt and revoke); private wildcards (`*.lan` would let DNS decide which hosts inside are reached); proxy credentials and SOCKS; pinning by key (SPKI). *Owner confirmed (2026-11-03, #225 items 2 to 4): SPKI pins, private wildcards, and proxy login and SOCKS stay rejected; a proxy password would also put a secret in the core, which only Q129's `session.fill` may hold.* |
| Q117 | Packages follow-ups: a cancelled install is remembered, `:packages install NAME`, `:packages update NAME` (ticket 64, #111) | **Agent's calls within Q83 and Q94, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** (a) **A cancelled install is kept in the State** (`packagesDeclined`: name, git URL and `dir`), not in `roster.lock` or `init.lua`: it's this Box's answer, like what was granted, and `roster.lock` stays a list of what's installed. It holds while `init.lua` names that Package from the same repo and folder; another repo or folder under the same name is other code (as Q94(c) says of the lock), so it asks. A sync that finds the line gone (or pointing elsewhere) forgets the cancel, so the State keeps nothing stale and naming it again asks. A changed `tag`, `branch` or `commit` from the same repo doesn't ask again: the user said no to the Plugin, not to a version. (b) **`:packages install NAME` asks again** (the same install prompt; the clone is fetched again), for a Package `init.lua` names that isn't installed: one whose install was cancelled, or one `:plugins remove` took out this run. `:packages` says "cancelled" for such a Package (not a problem colour) with that command under it, and `install` completes the cancelled names. `:plugins remove NAME` while `init.lua` still names it is unchanged: it isn't remembered, and the next start asks. (Replaced by Q142 (a): it's remembered like a cancel.) (c) **`:packages update NAME`** fetches only that Package (`Packages::check_named` in the core, `packages_check { name }` on the Tauri side) and answers "NAME is up to date", an update prompt as before, or why not: init.lua names no such Package, or it isn't installed. `update` completes the installed names. (d) **Error rows 116–121** (errors.md, Packages and a new "A Plugin stops"): a repo that can't be fetched (and a lock commit the repo lost), no `git`, git's 120 s limit, an update that can't be checked or used, a Plugin that can't load, and one turned off after three errors or not answering. They're notices and `:packages`/`:plugins` lines, not serve codes, so the app's catalog (`catalog.ts`) has no entries for them, like rows 80–83 and 102–107. (e) **No serve or Plugin API change, no motion.** |
| Q119 | serve's hardening after the iteration 9 review: the askpass folder on every exit, a stopped check's login, a cancelled joiner, the built-in scanner's `quiet`, the Windows pipe client (tickets 113, 104, 63; #198, #182, #110) | **Agent's calls, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** (a) **The askpass folder goes however serve ends.** It was removed on closed stdin and SIGTERM only; SIGHUP (the terminal went away) left it, and so did SIGKILL, a crashed interpreter and, before T109, every test serve killed in teardown (~7,000 had piled up in /tmp). Now, from just before the channel opens until serve returns (when the handlers it found are put back), SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGINT end serve the way closed stdin does; a channel interrupted while it opens removes its own folder (jobs stopped and recorded, the folder removed), and once that has begun a second signal is caught and dropped (a handler that does nothing, not `SIG_IGN`, which a program started meanwhile would inherit), so it can't cut the clean-up short. SIGQUIT keeps its core dump. (b) **What a killed serve left, the next one removes.** The folder is named `roster-askpass-PID-XXXXXXXX` after the serve that owns it. A starting serve looks in each place it may put one (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, the temp dir, `/tmp`) and removes a folder only if every one of these holds: it's a real directory, never a link (checked with `lstat`, then opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and checked again by device and inode, so a swap in between is seen); it's this user's (uid); it holds only what serve puts there (`socket`, `askpass`, `logged-in`, `quick_known_hosts`, none a directory); its pid names no live process; and its socket doesn't answer a connect (refused or gone). The last check is what keeps a container sharing `/tmp`, whose pids mean nothing here, from losing its live serve's socket. A folder from before this, with no pid in its name, goes only once its socket is dead. Entries are unlinked through the folder's descriptor, so a link inside goes itself, never what it points at. Anything it can't tell (a path too long to connect to, a pid the OS won't take, an error) it leaves, and it never raises. After review: a socket younger than 10 s may sit between `bind` and `listen` (it refuses then too), and a folder with no socket may belong to a serve between `mkdtemp` and `bind`; in another pid namespace both would look dead, so a fresh socket is left, and a socketless folder goes only once it's 10 minutes old. A serve killed and restarted within 10 s leaves its folder for a later start, and that's intended (#222): a socket that young can't be told from a live serve's between `bind` and `listen`, and the folder left meanwhile is harmless (no listener, still the user's alone). The socket was never reachable by another user: `0700` folder, `0600` socket, the peer's uid checked; a left folder's socket has no listener. (c) **Windows isn't swept, on purpose:** the channel there is a named pipe, a kernel object that ends with serve however it ends (closed stdin, `TerminateProcess`), so no way to a password is ever left behind; a folder a killed serve leaves under the user's own `%TEMP%` holds only `quick_known_hosts`, Quick Connect's confirmed public host keys, no secret. A safe sweep there needs an `OpenProcess` liveness check, the folder's owner SID and refusing reparse points (junctions as well as links): more code with Windows-only failure modes for clutter, not exposure. A follow-up if the clutter matters. The cleanup on every exit and the sweep are Linux and macOS's, where the socket lives in the folder. (d) **A temp dir too long for a socket's path** falls back to `/tmp` (on POSIX) instead of serve failing at its first bind. (e) **A cancelled waiter that joined another's check answers at once** with `5` (Q112 (w) let it wait for the check to end, up to 45 s, holding a worker): the check goes on for whoever else holds it. The last holder's cancel still stops the check (if it isn't stopping or over already) and answers its stopped result, as before, with its skipped steps; `5` only ever means the check goes on for someone else. No API bump: the core ignores the answer to a request it cancelled. (f) **A stopped check ends its login's ssh.** The `auth` step shares the vault for its whole ssh login (Q112 (u)), so `vault.lock` waited up to ~35 s behind it. Stopping (a lock, `jobs.stop`, the last waiter's cancel) now ends that ssh within 0.1 s, and the step is `skipped`; a check stopped before its login never starts the ssh. On POSIX the ssh runs in a process group of its own and the whole group is ended (SIGTERM, so ssh closes its connection, then SIGKILL), so a ProxyJump's ssh goes too, and the ssh is always reaped (our ends of its pipes closed first, so a child holding them can't keep serve waiting). A Ctrl+C in `roster check` ends it the same way. This is the safe choice: the login runs `true` and nothing else, so nothing is half-done on the Host; it doesn't add an attempt for the Host's rate limiter (the one attempt is made or never will be, and a stopped check doesn't retry); and it means no ssh roster started still holds the password once the lock has answered. Waiting instead kept the lock screen hanging for the length of a slow login. `roster check HOST` from the CLI has no stop and is unchanged. (g) **Foreground `mesh.apply` on quit** (T104 item 1) needed no change: since Q112 (d) the waiting form is a job too, so closing serve stops it and records what it didn't do in `mesh.json` within the core's `EXIT_GRACE`. A test now holds that. (h) **The built-in scanner's `quiet` stays 0** (Q100 (g)'s follow-up, optional in the ticket): telling a refused connection (a TCP reset) from a timeout was tried and dropped after review, because a refusal isn't reliably a Host. A gateway or firewall that resets connections for addresses nobody has would make every address of a range quiet, and Windows retries a refused SYN for about 2 s, past the scanner's 1 s timeout, so the count would differ by platform. nmap's count (host discovery first) stays the one to trust. (i) **`roster discover --json` with a broken `roster.yml` found without `-c`** (`ROSTER_CONFIG` or the usual places) is error 1 with its fix (Q110 (e)), as with `-c`; with no `roster.yml` at all it scans as before, and without `--json` it still goes on without the Inventory. (j) **Windows: the askpass pipe client's process is checked** (T63 item 3): serve reads the client's pid (`GetNamedPipeClientProcessId`), opens it (`PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION`) and compares its token's user with its own, on top of the DACL; a client it can't identify is refused and logged. |
| Q120 | Native Windows: vault sign-in on a ConPTY, and the Mesh tested there (ticket 63, #110) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** (a) **`bw login` on a pseudoconsole:** the password and SSO flows of `vault.login` drive bw on Windows' ConPTY (`CreatePseudoConsole`, through ctypes in `roster/conpty.py`, no new dependency), the counterpart of the PTY on Linux and macOS; code 59 no longer stands in for "not on Windows". The child is created with empty standard handles (`STARTF_USESTDHANDLES`), or a child of serve (whose own are pipes) writes to serve's pipes instead of the console, and it runs in a job object that kills bw and its node with the sign-in. Secrets stay where they were: the master password in the environment block, the email and codes typed at bw's prompts, nothing in a command line; a screen line drawn by cursor position is a line break when roster reads the prompts. (b) **The session key from the screen:** bw's stdout is on the console too, so the key `bw login --raw` prints is the last line the console shows once bw has exited; a line that is something the person typed is never taken for it, and without a key the sign-in goes on to the master-password step, as on Linux. Once bw has exited, roster reads what's left for at most the console's 3 s close and moves on. conhost answers what bw asks the terminal itself (a cursor-position request), so roster types nothing but the answers. (c) **SSO has no link on Windows:** bw opens the page itself with PowerShell's `Start` by its full path (`open` 8.4.2), which no PATH entry can stand in for, and bw never prints the URL. So the `sso` step's `url` is `null` there (serve API 23), roster knows the page opened when Windows' TCP table shows a process of bw's job listening on `8065`-`8070` (IPv4 or IPv6; a box without IPv6 just has no such table), never by binding or connecting to the port, which could take it from bw, and the app says the page opened in the browser instead of showing a link to copy. `ROSTER_NO_BROWSER` can't stop it there. (d) **The Mesh's tests on Windows:** the stand-in Hosts (`tests/fakes/ssh`, `FAKE_HOSTS`) run their side with Git for Windows' MSYS sh (`Git\usr\bin\sh.exe`, `FAKE_SH`; not `Git\bin\sh.exe`, which puts Git's own commands before the PATH it's given), with its folder next on the Host's PATH so its awk, sed, sort, mktemp and ssh-keygen win over Windows' namesakes; a test's own tools for a Host go first with `FAKE_HOST_PATH`; the ESXi datastore link is a directory junction (no privilege needed, and Git's sh reads it as a symlink); a path a Host prints is in that sh's form (`/c/...`). NTFS keeps no mode bits, so the two mode tests skip there; the suite skips only when there's no Git for Windows. (e) **Found on the way:** closing serve mid-run killed only the ssh process it started, and on Windows an ssh that's a batch file is `cmd.exe` and its child, which kept the step (and its output pipes) alive; closing now ends the whole tree (`kill_tree`), as the step's timeout does. |
| Q121 | OSC 133 shell integration built: `kind` on `terminal_output`, `command_done`, read only (ticket 94, #167) | **Owner's call (2026-11-01): build Q105 as specified; roster only reads.** It never writes to a terminal or a Session for this: no shell hooks put on a Host (not on connect, not by a command), no keystrokes or escape sequences sent, no new `terminal.write` powers. It parses the OSC 133 marks the user's own shell prints and exposes them read only through `terminal.read`; the user sets the shell up (docs/plugins.md, "Shell integration", with bash, zsh, fish and pwsh snippets; `:help shell-integration`). **Coordinator's call after two reviews of #215: the marks only annotate.** They come from the Host, in its output, so anything it prints can forge or lose them; every way of holding typed rows back from `terminal_output` turned out to let forged or missing marks hold back and lose real output. So `terminal_output` never holds back or drops a committed row, as before ticket 94 (a no-echo password never reaches the screen anyway), and each run of lines carries `kind`: `"prompt"` (at most 3 lines after `A`), `"input"` (the one line committed after `B`, or the row a `C` came on before its line feed, pwsh's), `"output"` (from `C` to `D`), or `nil` (no marks, or nothing they say: a shell that marks prompts but never says `C` gets one `"input"` line per prompt and `nil` after, so it can't make its output look typed). Lines of one kind go as one event, in order. Plugins filter if they want; the docs say `kind` is a hint. recent-alerts skips `"prompt"` and `"input"` lines and keeps its prompt guess for the rest, `"output"` included, since an `ssh` or nested shell in a command's output may be echoing what's typed and the outer marks can't tell (so no typed-key signal from the frontend is needed). **How:** `committed.ts` registers an OSC 133 handler on each Session's hidden xterm (no new dependency). `D;CODE` makes a `command_done` `{ session, host, command, output, code }`: the command line read off the screen at `C` (from where `B` left the cursor; `nil` with no `B`, or when it scrolled away or a resize reflowed it), the output since `C` (its last 16 KB, in a ring, amortized O(1) a line; one `OUTPUT_MAX` and `lastLines` shared with `terminal_output`), plus what it printed before `D` on the cursor's row (never what comes after there: zsh's PROMPT_SP mark, the next prompt), which is then not sent again when that row is committed with the prompt on it. At most 20 per batch. A `D` without a `C` is no command; a mark sent twice counts once. **No `command_done` when the pairing is doubtful:** an `A` while a command runs is a nested shell's (`ssh` to a Host with integration), and the outer command gets none (emitting one would say it ended while it runs); `exit` and `logout` get none, since a shell that exits sends no `D` for them, so the `D` that follows is another shell's. **Order:** flushes run one after another, so a Session's events never interleave, and `command_done` follows the `terminal_output` with its lines; a Session that ends sends what it had before `session_exit`. This changes Q105(b), (c) and (d): no rows are held back, the command line is sent in `command_done` (the owner's ticket asks for exact command lines), and output is collected since `C` rather than re-read at `D`, since a 0-scrollback terminal can't re-read what scrolled away. **API:** `command_done` needs `terminal.read`; `roster.api_version` goes to 4 (the coordinator sets the number at merge); recent-alerts keeps `api = 3` (an older app sends no `kind`, and it falls back to its guess). Plugins that don't listen for `command_done` still cost the bounded collection: which Plugins listen is known only in the core. **Rejected:** holding typed rows back (forged marks lose output, and two budgets in a row could be gamed); injecting hooks on connect (writes to the Session; the owner's rule); a separate permission (nothing beyond what `terminal_output` already shows); a command-history view (roster isn't a terminal). |
| Q122 | `:check HOST` shows each step as it runs (ticket 110, #193) | **Agent's calls within Q112 (q) and taste.md, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** (a) **From the frontend, serve unchanged:** the app already forwards `job.progress`, so the panel follows a check's job by it (kind `check`, its Host, `state: running`); the core still asks `host.check_job` for the answer. Any running check of the Host is the one asked for or the one it joined (one per Host, Q112 (q)), so no job id is needed; another Host's job, another kind, a job's last word and anything after the answer change nothing. (b) **No layout shift:** all eight rows are drawn from the start; the summary line is one line while it runs and once it's over (cut with an ellipsis, the Host's whole name is in the heading), so the rows never move until a failure opens up. (c) **Honest while it runs:** progress names only the step that started, never how one came out, so the steps before it are *done* (a muted dot), not *passed*: a key Login's `provider` and `vault-item` are skipped by design. The answer then marks each row ✓, ✗ or –. (d) **The accent is the step under way only** (taste: apricot means live): an accent ring, as a Session's while it connects, with a 1 px accent edge drawn above it as it starts (`--dur-draw`, 120 ms, strong ease-out; no transition when it goes, so steps 200 ms apart never queue motion; none under reduced motion). No pulse or spinner. A passed step's ✓ and "All checks passed" become the text colour, not the accent. (e) **A step that hangs:** after 3 s the step under way says how long it has taken (`12 s`, Iosevka, tabular), ticking once a second. (f) **Where it stopped:** not ok with no failed step means a lock stopped it (Q112 (q)): "Stopped at Host key", a square on the first step serve skipped with "the check was stopped". When the app stops waiting (90 s, serve lost), the rows stay as they were with the square on the step under way, under the error. (g) **Screen readers:** one polite announcement per step at most, and only for a step under way 600 ms (the local steps take milliseconds); the outcome is the summary line's own change (the same element, `aria-live` polite), so `ui.messages` (Q115) is untouched: `:check` puts nothing on the message line. (h) **Asking again:** `:check` of the same Host while it runs keeps its place (serve joins it); another Host's `:check` replaces the panel, as before. `:check` takes one Host, so there's no several-at-once case. |
| Q123 | Your Plugins inside `:tutor`'s demo (ticket 66, #113) | **Agent's calls within Q76 and Q81, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** (a) **Which Plugins, with what:** the ones running in your workspace when the tutorial starts (the built-ins, local Plugins and Packages), each with exactly what you granted it, revocations too. One stopped, waiting for an update prompt's answer, or not installed doesn't come along, and the demo never prompts: what it runs was already answered. Grants changed in the demo (`:plugins revoke`) stay in the demo's State in memory and go with it. (b) **A Plugin host of its own:** the core runs them in a second `PluginHost` (`plugin/demo.rs`, `DemoHost`), beside yours, so a Plugin is loaded twice, each copy with its own Lua state; your workspace's copy keeps running behind the demo, untouched. Leaving the demo stops the demo's copies. (c) **roster serve is the demo's stand-in:** `hosts.list` and `host.get` answer from the demo's six Hosts (so `roster.inventory` reads them and its events name them); every other method, `roster.inventory`'s writes and `mesh.*`/`discover.*` too, is refused with "METHOD isn't on the demo Inventory: the tutorial's demo answers only hosts.list and host.get" (errors.md row 135). The permission checks run first as ever (a flagged method still only in the user's call). Not a bridge to the demo's fake Backend: that would call from the core into the window and wait on it, for little a tutorial needs. (d) **The network is never reached:** `roster.http` is there for a Plugin granted a site (`roster.has` says what you granted), every check runs as online (the site, method, headers, body size, timeout, no user or password in the URL) and so does the cap of 4 requests going at once, then nothing is sent and its function hears `nil` and "URL wasn't sent: the tutorial's demo reaches no network" (row 136), as it would a failed request: a Plugin handles that already, where a missing `roster.http` or a raised error would count toward stopping it. (e) **Files: a throwaway folder, not a copy.** Each demo gets a fresh, empty data folder in the temp dir (`roster-demo-PID-…/plugin-data/NAME`), newly made and the user's alone (0700), removed when the demo ends or the app quits; one a crash left behind is swept when the next demo starts (a real folder, not a link, this user's, its process gone, older than 10 minutes; on Windows the temp dir is the user's own and nothing is swept). Not a copy of your `plugin-data`: a tutorial should start the same for everyone, and nothing the demo does can reach or leak what a Plugin keeps for you. (f) **Packages** load from the clones you have, at the commit `roster.lock` pins; the demo never fetches, pins or removes one. (g) **A Plugin that fails to load in the demo** gets its notice there, like any load error (row 120), and the tutorial runs on; if the core can't start the demo's host at all, the demo runs with its own built-in Plugins only, as before, none of yours is put in its State, and one notice says why. Leaving stops the demo's Plugin manager first (its timers, listeners and UI), then the demo's host. (h) **No new permission, no `roster.api_version` change:** what a Plugin can do is unchanged; only what answers it differs. Tauri: `plugin_demo_start` (gives the demo's number, taken before its host is made) and `plugin_demo_stop(id)`; every plugin command takes `onDemo: id` and every `plugin-demo-reply` names its demo, so an old demo's calls and replies never reach a new one, and a start that finishes after a newer one is turned away (`DemoSlot`); the frontend's `Backend.demoPlugins`. No motion. |
| Q124 | Graph and Mesh follow-ups (ticket 99, #174) | **Agent's calls within Q91, Q99 and Q101, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** (a) **Opening a view at a node or row** (Q99 (k)'s follow-up): `roster.views.open(id, { select = ID })`, Plugin API 4. A graph opens the node's cluster if it's collapsed (kept, as going to a node from the list does) and centres the node at no less than full size; a table scrolls to the row. Asked again while the view is open, the view starts afresh there (it remounts: search and camera reset), since a Plugin asking for a node is a new place to look. An id the view doesn't have opens it as without `select`, silently: the Plugin's data may have moved on since the button was drawn. No motion: it comes from a key or a click (the animate gate). Topology's Reach section gets a heading button, "Show HOST on the map", with a new row icon `map` (the toolbar's three joined nodes), only on an app with Plugin API 4. (b) **A cluster that grows into its neighbours moves** (Q99 (d) amended): a cluster's saved centre stayed while later updates added nodes (Topology fills its clusters as Hosts' details arrive), so it outgrew its row and covered the next. Now a placed cluster that got new nodes and would overlap another moves, whole (its nodes keep their shape), to the nearest free place on rings around where it was, and the moved centre is saved. Only new nodes count: a cluster the user made bigger by dragging a node out of it stays where it is, and so does every neighbour, even overlapping (the user put it there); a layout an older version saved overlapping isn't healed either, since that can't be told apart from a drag. Considered: pushing the neighbours along the row (moves many clusters the user already knows for one that changed) and reserving room for growth (unknowable). (c) **Cluster titles side by side** (Q99 (m) amended): a fixed "a line higher" fell half over a bigger neighbour's title (circles of other sizes put their lines at other heights), so the middle of three small clusters was cut. A title now goes just above the circle, else just above the title in its way (twice at most), else just below the circle, never deeper than a line into the row above or below; else it's cut as before. (d) **`m` on a collapsed cluster** says "web is collapsed: o opens it, then m moves one of its nodes" under the graph and to a screen reader, until the next key; moving a whole cluster is a follow-up. (e) **`mesh.cut`'s `warnings`** (serve API 24): the cut Host's own side reports an earlier `mesh_dir:` it leaves (the `STALE` line roster mesh's apply already reports); `roster mesh cut` prints them and the Mesh controls show each as a warning notice. The Plugin reads `warnings` when present, so an older serve just says nothing; no app gate is needed. (f) **An old key that comes back is said** (Q91 amended; the coordinator's call after review): a source whose `mesh_dir:` is removed returns to roster's default directory, which may still hold the key from before that `mesh_dir:` (warned about then, never removed). roster used it again silently; now, while the `Include` still names the other directory and it's still there, plan and apply warn that the older key is used again and that `roster mesh --rotate HOST` replaces it. roster leaves the key alone: replacing it automatically was tried first, but the `Include` is rewritten only in an apply's second phase, so every plan, and every apply that stopped before then, would have replaced the key again, and peers would have held a stale one; a deleted old directory would also have replaced a good key. The owner may opt into rotating it later. *Owner confirmed (2026-11-03, #237 item 5): an older Mesh key that comes back keeps warning and is never replaced automatically; `roster mesh --rotate HOST` stays the way to replace it.* |
| Q125 | Panel layout follow-ups (ticket 100, #176): one width limit for every docked panel, floats side by side, keys for heading buttons | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review, except (g), the owner's.** (a) **One limit, shared:** the terminal keeps 400 px (about 50 columns) beside the Sidebar and every docked panel together, not beside each alone. Flexbox does the sharing: a docked frame may shrink (in proportion to its width) down to its 280 px minimum and the terminal (`.workspace`) has a 400 px minimum while a panel is docked, so the panels give way, never the terminal, and a window made smaller later needs no script. A panel's own limits for `␣ >`, `:panel width`, its separator and a drag count the Sidebar and the other docked panels against it, so widening one only takes room the terminal and the others can spare and never shrinks the others on release. (b) **Too many to dock float:** when the panels' 280 px each don't fit beside a 400 px terminal (three in a window under about 1,500 px with the Sidebar), they all float over the terminal, as below 1,040 px and in Focus (M5), rather than squeeze it or hide one; `arrange()` in `app/src/panel/layout.ts` decides from the window's width, the docked Sidebar and how many there are, so it doesn't flip back and forth. *Owner confirmed (2026-11-03, #232 item 6): two docked panels in a window between about 1,040 and 1,210 px float rather than squeeze the terminal.* Full window takes no part (the docked width it keeps underneath counts as the Sidebar does). (c) **Floats sit side by side** from their edge, in the order they'd dock in (the last on the right nearest the right edge, the first on the left nearest the left), never on top of each other, which was #176's item 2: several panels on one side were drawn at the same place, the one drawn last hiding the rest. Floats too wide together for the window less its 48 px sliver are narrowed in proportion; the panel inside narrows while its frame keeps its own width (and takes no clicks), so the next arrangement measures what each wants rather than what it was given. Nothing animates: arrangement follows a key or a click (the animate skill). (d) **Keys for heading buttons:** a view's or a Host-details section's `actions` take a one-character `key` (Plugin API 4, `HeadAction`), which runs the button from anywhere in that view or section: the table's or graph's own keys and its actions' run first (they stop the key), a text field keeps its letters, a held key runs nothing, and the button's tooltip and `aria-keyshortcuts` show it. A key may not be one a table or graph keeps (the union of both lists, since an update can swap one for the other), one of the view's table or graph actions' keys, or another button's. No automatic keys (Alt+1…4): a Plugin names the letter that fits, as table actions do. The Mesh controls' Cut on a Host's section is `x`, which the section's table had. (e) **The Mesh's Host-details section lists only the Host's Links:** its "this Host" row repeated the facts above it and carried a Cut the heading's button now has; a Host whose Links all work shows the sentence and no table. (f) **`mesh.*` and `discover.*` results are typed** in plugin-api.md and `types/roster.lua` (#160 item f): `MeshResult`, `MeshStarted` (a `background` run), `MeshCut`, `MeshStatus`, `MeshJob`, `MeshStop`, `DiscoverPlan`, `DiscoverStatus`, `DiscoverStop`, `DiscoverAdd` and their parts, from serve-api.md and serve itself; a field a later serve adds is the ticket's that adds it. (g) **Esc in a text field of a form made full window closes the form** (owner, 2026-11-01), as it does docked: a field's own Esc stays the field's, and only Esc elsewhere in a full-window panel docks it first. (h) **No `:has()` under the layout:** WebKitGTK only has it from about 2.44 (dev/linux.md), and the layering rule (Q106 (g)), the Sidebar's room and the terminal's minimum depended on it, so the app sets `data-full`, `data-sidebar` and `data-docked` on the body instead; the remaining uses are cosmetic. |
