| Q126 | A Plugin's messages follow `ui.messages` as roster's own (tickets 117 and 118, #202 and #203) | **Agent's calls within Q115, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** Q115 (a) left "whatever a Plugin says" and `:cancel`'s "Stopping …" always shown; this narrows that to Neovim's rule: what went wrong and what was asked show, a routine confirmation is quiet. (a) **`roster.echo(text, { quiet = true })`** (api 4) marks a routine confirmation; a plain `roster.echo` still shows. An options table, not a level: a Plugin's echo already carries its errors and answers (Folders' "No Host named nope-01", "folder rename needs …"), which must show, so the one thing to say is "this one is routine", and a table leaves room as `roster.notify`'s does. Plain echo staying shown is Neovim's `:echo` and keeps an api 3 Plugin's answers on screen; a Plugin written for api 4 that runs on an api 3 roster loses nothing (the table is ignored, the line shows). Not "plain echo is quiet": most of a Plugin's echoes are errors and answers, so the default would hide the wrong ones. (b) **An info notice is quiet** while `ui.messages` is quiet, unless it has buttons (a result to act on, "Plan ready: Show the plan") or the Plugin says `{ quiet = false }`; `quiet = true` quiets one with buttons too. **What a Plugin marks `quiet = false`** is what the user asked for, as Neovim shows it: the result of something they started ("Scan done: 12 Hosts answered, 3 new", "Applied: 3 Hosts changed.", "Cut esx-02 from 4 Hosts.", Folders' "Moved 2 Folders from folders.yml"), an answer ("No alerts", "Nothing to apply: the plan changes nothing.") and a refusal ("Keep a Host first …", "That's this Box …"); a routine confirmation when nothing went wrong ("Copied the line", "Dismissed 3 alerts") stays quiet. The bundled Plugins (discover, mesh, topology, recent-alerts) are marked so, each a one-line change, and their transcripts recorded again. Warnings and errors always show, whatever `quiet` says. Quiet, it isn't drawn as a toast: it goes on the message line as a quiet message (`PLUGIN: text`, its whole text the short form), so a screen reader still hears it (Q115 (e)), like roster's own quiet ones (which replace what the line held, as theirs do). *Amended by Q162 (owner, 2026-11-03): a quiet notice from a timer, an event or a request's answer, which nothing the user did asked for, no longer replaces a line that shows (an error stays); `:messages` still keeps it. One the user's own call brought still replaces the line, as here.* (c) **`:messages` keeps every notice**, shown or quiet, as it keeps the message line's: Neovim's `:messages` holds notifications too. (d) **Folders:** its confirmations ("Created Folder Lab", "Added esx-02 to Lab", "Removed …", renamed, deleted) are quiet; its refusals and answers ("esx-02 is already in Lab") show; its manifest says api 4. (e) **`:cancel`'s "Stopping …" is quiet:** the progress item itself says it's stopping, so the line repeats it; `:cancel`'s refusals ("Nothing running can be cancelled", "… can't be stopped") still show. (f) **No new ops or API version:** the `echo` and `notify` ops gain an optional `quiet` (the app ignores it from an older core, which never sends it); `roster.api_version` 4 is this release's (shared with Q121). No serve change, no motion, nothing new drawn. |
| Q127 | What a Plugin can read without waiting: open Sessions, a check's result, the Inventory once a call and without waiting (ticket 96, #169) | **Agent's calls within Q81, Q99, Q103 and Q112, fakes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-01).** The four gaps Topology found (Q102 (i)). (a) **`roster.sessions.list()`** (api 4, `since: 4`; `roster.api_version` stays 4) gives the Sessions open now, `{ session, host? }` in the order they opened, the ones open before the Plugin loaded too. **Permission: `inventory.read` or `terminal.read`**, the two that hear `session_open` and `session_exit` (Q81): the list is exactly what those events would have said, so it reveals nothing they don't, and a Plugin that may hear them may know their sum. Not `sessions.open`: that one is flagged because it *acts* (opens a Session on a Host), and gating a read behind it would make a Plugin ask for the power to connect only to learn what's connected; not a new permission, since that would be a prompt line saying nothing the event permissions don't. So `Needs` in api.rs takes `Needs::Hears("session_open")`, and the runtime gives the function to a Plugin that `may_hear("session_open")`: one table (the event's `needs`) decides the function, its reference and its stub. **Where it lives:** the core (`OpenSessions` in plugin/mod.rs), as a snapshot: the app sends the whole list (`plugin_sessions`, `[id, host]` in the order it saw each Session open, a reconnect going last) on start and whenever it changes, and each list replaces the last. Keeping it from `session_open`/`session_exit` was the first cut and the review turned it down: those are separate fire-and-forget calls (an exit waits for its output to flush, an open doesn't), so a reconnect, which keeps its tab id, could drop a live Session or keep a dead one, and a reloaded webview's tab ids start at 1 again, leaving ghosts. The events stay for Plugins as they were. The manager sends a list before the events that changed it and makes those events (and any load) wait for it, so a listener's list agrees with its event and a Plugin loading reads the latest list. The core never asks the app or serve, so the call never waits. Considered: the app handing the list to each load (stale for a Plugin that reloads by itself) and a list in the frontend (Lua can't wait for it). (b) **A check's result needs no new function:** since Q112 (q) every check is a job (serve API 21), the app's `:check` included, so a Plugin granted `serve:host.check_job` (a read, not flagged) already hears each check end (`job_progress`, kind `check`) and asks `host.check_job` once with `roster.serve.request`. What was missing was the result's type: `CheckJob` is now declared (its `result` is `host.check`'s `HostCheck`), with a recipe in plugins.md. Putting `ok` into `job.progress` was considered and dropped: Q112 (e) keeps results out of progress, and a serve change (API 25) for one boolean a quick read already gives isn't worth it. T110's live steps stay the app's panel; a Plugin doesn't redraw them. A stopped check (the vault locked under it) says nothing about the Host, so it isn't a verdict. (c) **The Inventory once a call:** `roster.inventory.hosts()` and `.groups()` keep serve's `hosts.list` answer for the rest of the call (a load, an event, an action, an answer) and use it, so both are one read; any call into serve that can change something (a method `serve_user_only` names: `inventory.update` and the other writes, `mesh.cut`, `mesh.apply`, `discover.add`, …, by `roster.serve.call` or `.request`) forgets it, it goes when the call ends, and the next call asks again. Not a cache across calls: only the app knows when the Inventory changed (`inventory_changed`), and a stale list across calls would be worse than a second read. Considered: a second return value of `hosts()` (an interface change every caller would have to learn for what a memo does unseen). (d) **`roster.inventory.request(done)`** (api 4) is the async form, as `roster.serve.request`: `done(list)` (a `HostList`: `hosts`, `groups`, `error?`) or `done(nil, "roster.inventory.request: MESSAGE (code N)")`, on the Plugin's thread as a call of its own, carrying the user's call one hop (Q103 (d)), counted in the 8 waiting requests (error row 108), with `inventory.read` alone (no `serve:hosts.list` grant). One function for the whole list rather than an async twin of each read: the list is what a Plugin waiting on a busy serve wants, and `host(name)` is a lookup in it. (e) **Lua is built optimised in debug and test builds** (`[profile.dev.package.mlua-sys]` and `mlua`, `opt-level = 3`): the interpreter ran about six times slower unoptimised, so plugin_topology's 2,000-Host test ran with six times the app's budget. Now it runs with the app's own `Limits` (its longest call about half the budget in a test build, a quarter in a release one); the rest of a test build's cost is the core's own Rust, unoptimised. Cheaper than a deterministic count of Lua instructions, which would need a second budget the app doesn't have. (f) **plugins/topology** (1.1.0, api 4) uses (a) at load and (b): a Host whose check failed is down, its note "check failed at STEP", until a check of it passes or a Session to it opens. It asks `host.check_job` only for a check whose `state` is `done` (a stopped or failed one says nothing about the Host, and each question takes one of the 8 request slots), and applies an answer only if no later check of that Host has ended since (by the order serve said they ended, not their ids, which start again with a new roster serve); it asks for `serve:host.check_job`, so updating it asks once. It keeps its synchronous reads, which (c) makes one. No serve change (API stays as it is), no UI change, no motion. |
| Q129 | Filling a password prompt in a Session from the vault (ticket 119, #208) | **Owner's idea (2026-11-01): roster may write into a Session's terminal when it helps. Owner's ruling after the security review (2026-11-01): the core may hold the secret for `session.fill` only. Coordinator's rule after that review: fill only a prompt that is provably this Host's own; refuse whenever in doubt (a refusal is cheap, a leak isn't). Agent's design within them, fakes only.** (a) **Only on a key, only at sudo's prompt.** `␣ p` (`fill`) types the Host's password; nothing is ever typed on its own, at load, on an event or from a Plugin (no Plugin may run `fill`; its serve allow-list has no `session.*`). The offer, a quiet note like Save to vault's with the key and a Fill button that leaves focus in the terminal, shows only while the cursor's line is `[sudo] password for USER:` with USER the Host's ssh user, read off a hidden terminal's screen (`app/src/session/prompt.ts`, as `committed.ts` reads lines), only for Host Sessions on a roster with the fill API. A tried prompt is spent: the offer goes at once and comes back only for a new prompt. (b) **Where the secret goes: vault → serve → core → PTY (the owner's ruling).** The frontend sends the Session's id and the prompt line and hears only the Host's name. serve's `session.fill` (API 25) returns the password, the one serve result with a secret. The core (`SessionManager::fill`) asks for the Host it recorded at open, holds the value in a `Secret` (no Debug, Display, Clone or Serialize; bytes overwritten on drop), writes it and Enter in one write, and drops it. The PTY is the core's (on Windows a ConPTY in the app's process, serve in WSL), and a PTY takes input only from its owner. **Rejected:** writing from serve (it can't reach the PTY; passing the master's descriptor works on Linux only), a wrapper process relaying input from askpass (changes every Session's command), TIOCSTI (off in current kernels), the frontend typing it. (c) **Which prompts and which password.** serve fills `[sudo] password for USER:` (USER the ssh user) from the Host's `sudo: {auth: REF}` (cascading like `ssh:`, a vault reference only, kept beside the Hosts so `host.get` is unchanged), else its ssh Login; and `USER@HOST's password:` only for the ssh user at the Host's own name or address (not `localhost`: a tunnel's may be anywhere), from the ssh Login; the app doesn't offer that one, since it can't tell where the ssh went. A bare `Password:` (su's, a login's, telnet's, a nested keyboard-interactive's) names no one, so it's refused: **su is never filled.** No saved password is 131; everything else 130. (d) **The Session must still be on that Host.** `session.prepare` returns `login: {address, port, user}` (API 25); the core keeps it with the Session and sends it with the fill; serve refuses (130) a Host edited since (a new address, port or user may be another box), as Save to vault refuses with `NOT_VERIFIED`. A serve that doesn't say it means no fill. (e) **Threat model.** *A forged prompt* (the Host prints it): it can only narrow; whatever it says, roster fills only this Host's own Login, for a prompt naming that Login. *Another machine* (the leak that matters): with OSC 133 marks the offer needs the running command, read off the screen at `C`, to be sudo itself (after `VAR=value`), with no word that reaches another machine or runs another program (`ssh`, `mosh`, `telnet`, `docker`, `kubectl`, `nc`, `su`, `env`, `time`, …), no shell syntax (`|`, `;`, `$(`), and nothing printed since `C` but sudo's own lines (so `sudo -i` fills at its first prompt, never after its shell ran something); at the shell's prompt nothing is offered; a marked shell starting inside a command makes roster unsure for good. Reading the command off the screen, not rebuilding it from the echo, keeps history recall and redraws honest. Without marks roster can't tell you've left the Host: the offer says so in words, and T120 (#209) will tell Hosts apart. *An echoing prompt* would put the secret on screen, in scrollback, in `terminal.read` Plugins' output and the frontend's xterm: the core types a probe (Ctrl+U, a letter, Ctrl+U) that a no-echo prompt swallows silently (clearing anything half-typed), and types the secret only if the Session printed nothing and no key was sent from before the vault lookup until 800 ms after the probe. The local PTY's echo flag can't be the signal (ssh keeps it raw). *Marks* are the Host's too: they only ever take the offer away. *A compromised webview* could press the key, as it can already type into any Session; the CSP is that defence. *Slow links:* an echo after 800 ms comes too late. *Memory:* serve keeps its existing cache until lock; the core's copy lives for the call; the JSON line it arrived in isn't wiped. (f) **Locked vault:** 14; the app shows the lock screen and says to press the key again, so nothing is queued. Errors: serve codes 130-131 (errors.md rows 143-144) and the app's or core's refusals (row 145). (g) **With shell hooks (Q130):** the fill is typed by the core, never through `Tabs.write`, so the hooks don't take it for the user typing and never hold it; the hook line is never sent while a password prompt is up or a fill runs (`:integrate` answers no prompt then); a hook line written meanwhile makes the fill refuse, as any key does. Hooks put marks on, which turns the unmarked case into the checked one. **Changed by Q131 (ticket 120, after #251's review):** marks carrying the Session's token (roster's hook line) are the Session's own shell's, and its own `D` ends a command for good, so (e)'s "unsure for good once a marked shell starts inside a command" applies only to marks that aren't roster's; with those, every fill stops once a command that may reach another machine has run (the residual forged-`D` risk in (e) is closed for roster-hooked Sessions), and without marks the note says a nested shell can't be ruled out. **Changed by Q136 (ticket 137):** the core now also checks, before the vault is asked, that the cursor sits right after the prompt for the Session's own Host's fill, as a terminal draws its output (`SessionManager::fill` takes the prompt; refused with 130, row 163, so the prompt stays offered). **Changed by Q144 (ticket 154):** (e)'s probe takes sudo's `pwfeedback` mask for its letter, drawn and taken back (read by the core's terminal parser: one `*`, spaces, cursor moves, erases within the line, the cursor's hide and show, ending with the cursor and line as they were), as no echo, only for the Session's own Host, at sudo's own prompt for its ssh user when that prompt is the whole line; what the Session prints during a fill is kept from Plugins until its next line break. |
| Q130 | Opt-in shell hooks: roster sends one line that turns on OSC 133 and OSC 7 (ticket 121, #210) | **Agent's calls within the owner's idea (2026-11-01, #210, which lifts Q121's "no hooks injected on connect" for an opt-in), for the owner's review.** (a) **Opt-in, three ways:** `shell_hooks: true\|false` in `roster.yml` on a Host, group or `defaults` (passed through to the app in the Host as any field is, so no serve API change; roster checks it's a boolean), `terminal.shell_hooks` in `init.lua` (unset: the Host decides; `true`: every Session, Quick Connect too, but a Host's `false` wins, so a team can keep network gear out; `false`: never), and `:integrate` for the Session on screen, which sends whatever they say (the user asked) and answers honestly once it knows: on, sent but no marked prompt came back, or why nothing went. Not a Plugin power: `:integrate` isn't a line a Plugin may run. (b) **When:** once per Session attempt, when the login has printed nothing for 0.4 s, within 20 s of the start, the user hasn't typed (anything left of what the terminal sent once escape sequences are taken out: so its reports, mode and mouse answers, focus and cursor keys don't count; the prompt check catches a recalled line), the shell hasn't printed an OSC 133 mark (looked for in a 1 KB tail, so one split across reads counts), and the line the cursor is on is a prompt it knows: PowerShell's `PS …> `, fish's default `user@host ~> ` (or `#`), or `$`, `#` or `%` right after the prompt's text (or a `$ ` alone, a two-line prompt's second line), never after a space, which is tcsh's `host:~ % ` and a menu's `Choice # `. Anything else (a forced command, a menu, a password prompt, a network CLI with no space after `#`/`>` or a `user@router> `, ESXi's `[root@esx-02:~] `) gets nothing. **Detection is the prompt, not a probe:** running something to ask the shell would be visible and slow; a POSIX prompt is sent one line whose bash and zsh halves are each a single-quoted string eval'd behind `${BASH_VERSION-}` or `${ZSH_VERSION-}`, so dash, busybox ash and ksh parse it and run neither. A shell that runs it and marks nothing, or doesn't take it (tcsh with a bash-like prompt prints an error; a single-line POSIX/csh polyglot couldn't be tested here, no tcsh), gets the echo, at most 4 lines of error and the same prompt cleared after 2 s, so nothing visible is left. (c) **What's sent:** the docs/plugins.md snippets squeezed to one line, plus OSC 7 at each prompt. bash's and zsh's wrap PS1 from a hook that runs last at each prompt, and again whenever it has lost the marks, so starship and oh-my-* keep them; the exit code comes from a hook that runs first; both work under `set -u`, and bash before 4.4 gets no `C` (no PS0). Each starts with a space (left out of history by bash's `ignorespace`, zsh's `HIST_IGNORE_SPACE`, fish), shown verbatim in the docs; PowerShell's uses `[char]27` so Windows PowerShell 5.1 doesn't print stray letters, keeps it in its history, and calls the history handler that was there (the user's, or PSReadLine's default with its secret filter) rather than replacing it. Never written to a file: it lives in that shell process. (d) **The echo:** the screen (and its replay) holds the Session's output from the send until the new prompt's `A` and `B` marks, checks that everything before `A`, without escapes and whitespace, is exactly the line (with zsh's PROMPT_SP mark after it), and then writes `CR`, up as many rows as the prompt has line feeds, erase-below, and the output from `A` on, keeping any private-mode set/reset (bracketed paste) from the dropped part, and the bytes from `A` on as they came (a character split across reads stays whole); so the screen looks as before. Anything else first, 2 s without the marks (unless it was just the echo, a short error and the same prompt, cleared as in (b)), 16 KB held, the user typing, or the Session ending shows everything as it came. Plugins (`terminal.read`) always get the output as it came, the echoed line included, as Q121 holds nothing back from them. (e) **OSC 7:** parsed from every Session's output (a shell or prompt that writes it itself counts), `file://HOST/PATH`, percent-decoded where it decodes, control characters dropped, 1 KB at most; shown as the Session row's faint right-hand detail (the last folder) and in its tooltip and the tab strip's (the whole path). No motion: it changes on every `cd`. Not given to Plugins here: that's a Plugin API change, left as a follow-up. (f) **Code:** `app/src/session/shellHooks.ts` (detection, the lines, the hold, OSC 7) with one small hook-up in `tabs.ts` (its own write path, separate from typed keys). **Tested:** the bash line in a real bash in CI, and that a POSIX shell that isn't bash ignores it; the PowerShell prompt in Windows PowerShell 5.1 by hand. **Changed (2026-11-02, #277):** CI now also runs the zsh, fish and PowerShell 7 lines and the rc-file snippets in those shells. **Changed by Q131 (ticket 120):** every mark the line makes carries the Session's token (`roster=…`, new per attempt), the PS1 re-wrap looks for that token rather than any `133;A`, `:integrate` sends the line into a shell whose own integration already marks it (roster's marks go round them), and Plugins get the output without the line's echo (left out from the send until roster's first own prompt, which changes (d)'s "Plugins always get the output as it came") and with the token dashed wherever else it shows. |
| Q131 | A nested ssh inside a Session: recognise Inventory Hosts, show where the Session is, fill their login (ticket 120, #209) | **Owner's idea (2026-11-01, #209); within Q129's rule (fill only a prompt that is provably that Host's own, refuse whenever in doubt) and Q121 (marks only annotate). Agent's design, fakes only; reworked after the focused review of #251 (coordinator's direction: a per-Session token in roster's own marks, fail closed without it).** (a) **Which shell a mark is from: the Session's token.** Each Session attempt gets 96 random bits (`newNonce`, hex); roster's hook line (Q130) puts `roster=TOKEN` on every mark it makes (`133;A;roster=…`, `B`, `C`, `D;CODE;roster=…`), and re-wraps a prompt that lacks *its* token (so `:integrate` in a shell whose own integration already marks it adds roster's marks round them). The token lives in that shell process only: no other machine sees it (a nested ssh's Host gets none of the outer shell's memory or output), so a mark with it is provably the Session's own shell's, and every mark without it is another shell's (the far Host's after an ssh, a container's, the user's own integration) or forged. It counts only if the line can only have gone to the Session's own shell: sent before anything was typed, or while marks showed that shell at its prompt with nothing run that may reach another machine; otherwise the token's marks are treated like any marks that aren't roster's. Plugins never see it: from the hook line going out until the first prompt mark with the token (or the hooks giving up), nothing the Session prints reaches them (the echo is the line itself; kept out even when it can't be matched), and after that `TokenMask` dashes the token wherever it shows (found through a terminal's wraps: a line break, cursor moves or erases inside it), holding back output that ends part-way into it until the next read or 50 ms, and never letting a held part out as it was (dashed when it's 6 characters or more, or follows `roster=`). (b) **Detection, with roster's marks only.** The command line is the one Q129 reads off the screen at the outer shell's own `C`, after an Enter you typed since its `B` (else the Host may have drawn it: shown as an ssh roster can't resolve). It's read with ssh's own rules (`app/src/session/sshTarget.ts`, sharing `commandLine.ts` with prompt.ts): flags with and without a value, clustered (`-tp 2222`, `-lroot`), options after the destination, `--`, the last `@` splitting user from host; `-l`, `-p`, `-o User=/Port=/HostName=` honoured, harmless `-o` options and flags skipped. Refused: `-J`, `-F`, `-S`, `-W`, `-f`, `-G`, `-V`, `-Q`, `-O`, `-P`, any other `-o` (ProxyCommand, ProxyJump, StrictHostKeyChecking, UserKnownHostsFile, HostKeyAlias, ControlPath, …), an unknown flag, a quote, `\` or `$`, a wildcard, a `%` token, `ssh://`, shell syntax, two different users or ports, no destination. Then matched against the Inventory by name (case-insensitive, as ssh lowercases it) or address and by port (22 when none is given): no match, a name at another port, or two Hosts at one address and port is no Host; loopback never is. (c) **The breadcrumb** shows from that ssh's `C` until **the outer shell's own `D`**, however the ssh ended (`exit`, Ctrl+D, a reset connection, a reboot, `kill`, an alias): marks without the token never end it, so the far shell's `D` before its first prompt (roster's own hook, kitty and VS Code print one), its commands' `D`s and `exec bash` there change nothing. The Session's Sidebar row and tab show an edge drawn from the Session's Host to the Host it's on now (Iosevka, no accent; dashed and muted when roster can't resolve it), "now on esx-02 through ssh" in the tooltip and accessible name, a polite status once; the edge draws over `--dur-draw`, the name fades in, both go at once; reduced motion keeps the fade. Display only. (d) **The nested ssh's login prompt** is offered only while that ssh is logging in: the line at the cursor is exactly `USER@TARGET's password:` with USER the resolved user (`-l`/`user@`/`-o User`, else the Host's ssh user) and TARGET the destination ssh prints (lowercased, after any `-o HostName`); the Host's Login has a saved password (a key or agent login types nothing: the breadcrumb only; agent forwarding stays the user's flag), the resolved user is that Login's, the Host is reviewed; no shell has marked a prompt there yet; nothing typed into the ssh since its `C` (a host-key `yes` included, so a fill happens only to a Host the first one already knew); after its first prompt only ssh's own lines; once per ssh (a refusal from serve, which typed nothing, offers it again). A host-key question is never answered. (e) **That Host's sudo** at sudo's prompt for its user only at the first command its own shell runs after marking a prompt, while that command is sudo itself and has printed only sudo's lines: once a command there has ended, what it ran (another ssh, a shell) could have reached a deeper machine that marks like that shell, so roster refuses for the rest of the ssh. From that Host's `sudo: {auth}`, else its ssh Login (serve's 131 if neither has a password). The Session's own Host's sudo is offered again once the outer `D` has come (the token proves it); while a command it runs has started a marked shell, it isn't. (f) **Marks that aren't roster's (fail closed).** With the shell's own integration, or a hook line whose token doesn't count: once a command that may reach another machine has started (`commandLine.ts mayReachElsewhere`, best effort: `ssh`, `mosh`, `telnet`, `docker`/`podman`/`nerdctl`/`kubectl` and `lxc`/`incus` exec, `nsenter`, `chroot`, `virsh console`, serial consoles (`cu`, `minicom`, `picocom`, `screen`), `console`/`conserver`, `ipmitool` (SOL), cloud shells (`aws ssm`, `gcloud … ssh`, `az ssh`), `nc`, `tmux`, a shell, `su`, `doas`, `sudo -i`/`-s`, `eval`, `$(…)`, a line roster couldn't read; that command's own sudo prompt is still judged as Q129 judges it, so `sudo -i` fills at its first prompt), roster can't tell which shell any later mark is from, so it fills nothing more in that Session, the Session's own sudo included, and says why and how ("reconnect with roster's shell hooks on: `shell_hooks`, or `:integrate` before you ssh"); before that, Q129's checks as they were. No breadcrumb and nothing nested there. An alias that runs ssh is beyond it (roster reads the line, it doesn't run the shell): with marks that aren't roster's, that's a residual risk the docs name; with roster's, the outer `D` decides regardless. **Without marks at all** nothing changes from Q129 (sudo's exact prompt only), and the note now says a nested shell can't be ruled out; a nested ssh's prompt gets a note saying roster can't tell where that ssh went. (g) **Where the secret goes and what's checked.** As Q129 (vault, serve, core, PTY), plus: the frontend sends the prompt line and `hop: {host, address, port, user}`; serve (`session.fill`'s `hop`, **API 26**) still checks the Session's own Host (130 if edited since it connected), then that the `hop` Host exists (2), is reviewed (20) and still has that address, port and user (130, "changed since this ssh started"), and judges the prompt against that Host's own name or address (case-insensitively, as ssh prints it) and user; the core refuses a `hop` outright to a serve before API 26 (it would ignore `hop` and answer for the Session's own Host), and types a nested fill only if the Session's output, read as text (its last 4 KB; escape sequences out, a cursor-forward as a space, whitespace runs as one: ConPTY's redraws included), **ends with** that prompt, the cursor right after it (`SessionManager::at_prompt`, `fill_at_prompt`, which names the hop Host in the result), before the vault is asked, then the probe and the 800 ms wait. The app re-reads the prompt from its watch when the key is pressed, and a key typed into the Session takes the offer away at once. An app on serve API 25 shows the breadcrumb and says the fill needs a newer roster. Before API 25 there's no breadcrumb either, though it needs nothing from serve: the app follows a nested ssh only in the watch that looks for prompts to fill, and it watches no Session on a serve without `session.fill` (#243 (e); showing the breadcrumb there would be a feature). Errors: the app's and core's refusals are errors.md rows 148 and 149; serve's are rows 143-144's codes 130-131. (h) **Threat model.** *Forged marks:* only the Session's own shell knows the token, so a forged mark is a foreign one: it can't end a nested ssh, start one, or move the Session's shell; at most it ends the login offer (a "shell started there") or allows that Host's own sudo at its first command. Without roster's marks, everything after a remote-capable command is refused. *ProxyCommand, jump hosts, mux sockets, an ssh config roster can't see:* refused when on the line; the first Host's own `~/.ssh/config` and DNS are named as limits (a config's `HostName` shows in ssh's prompt and is refused; a config `Port` or jump doesn't), with ssh's host-key check on that Host tying the name to a box and a first connection's `yes` ending the offer. *A compromised first Host lying about its output:* it knows the token (it's its own shell), so it could fake the whole thing; it also relays every byte of a real nested ssh in the clear, and the same fake would collect a password typed by hand; the note names the Host roster believes it is. *A compromised far Host:* never knows the token, so it can't make roster think the Session is back on the first Host. *Slow echo:* as Q129. *A compromised webview* could name another Host as the `hop`; the core's printed-prompt check means the Session must really have shown that prompt; the CSP stays the defence. *The token in Plugins' hands* would let a Plugin tell a far Host what to forge; Plugins never get it. **Rejected:** heuristics for the ssh's end (ssh's farewell line, `exit`/`logout`, which shell's `D`): an earlier draft used them, and the review found a far shell's leading `D` ended the hop and leaked the first Host's password into it, and a reset or an alias left it stuck; the banner or OSC 7's host name; guessing the user or port from the first Host's ssh config; filling after a typed `yes`; nested sudo after a command there ended; answering a host-key question; turning agent forwarding on. **Changed by Q136 (ticket 137):** (a)'s `TokenMask` now follows the token across reads and through any escape sequence, so after `roster=` no character of it reaches Plugins however the reads split it (elsewhere at most its first 3, as Q136 states); (g)'s printed-prompt check is now `SessionManager::fill`'s for every fill, against a screen the core keeps as a terminal draws it rather than the last 4 KB as text (`fill_at_prompt` and `at_prompt` are gone). |
| Q132 | init.lua's documented style becomes Neovim-like; one shape table is the source of every option's docs and types (ticket 141, #247; T142 and T143 build on it) | **Owner's decision (2026-11-01): init.lua's documented style becomes Neovim-like (`roster.o.terminal.size = "16pt"`, `roster.packages.add`, `roster.folders.add`); the returned table keeps loading. Agent's calls for ticket 141, for the owner's review.** (a) **One source:** `crates/roster-core/src/config/shape` declares every option path once, as an object, an open map, a list or a leaf, with its LuaCATS type, default, allowed values (one of, a range, or a form in words), a one-line summary and an example, plus every name of init.lua's `roster` table (values and functions with their parameters). Colour tokens and key contexts come from the schema's own lists. (b) **Written out:** `cargo run -p roster-core --example gen_config_options` writes `docs/app/options.md` (Neovim's `:help 'option'` layout), `examples/types/roster-config.lua` (LuaCATS, the same summaries, so the editor's hover says what `:help` says) and the frontend's data; `:help options` is that reference with each option's value now and where it was set beside it, each entry a topic (`:help terminal.size`, `:help roster.keymap.set`, `:help roster.config.Theme`; a name another page has is quoted, `'keys'`, `'roster.os'`). (c) **Can't drift:** `tests/config_shape.rs` asks `Config`'s own Deserialize for every path it takes (serde names a struct's fields for an unknown one) and fails for any path the shape doesn't declare with a type, default, summary, example and a place in the reference, so a new schema field fails CI until it's documented; it loads every example, every default and both ends of every range through the real loader, checks config.md's option tables name the same options, checks the `roster` table the runtime builds, and fails on a stale generated file. (d) **`:options`** is a read-only finder: each option that holds a value, its value now, where it was set (`file:line`, `init.lua`, the State, or default), its default and type in the footer; Enter opens its help. **`:config types`** writes the stubs as `types/roster-config.lua` in the config folder, never through a link. (e) **The hook for T142:** `NEOVIM_STYLE` in the shape is false until `roster.o`, `roster.packages.add` and `roster.folders.add` exist; their entries are declared already, and flipping it shows them, switches every example from `roster.set(…)` to `roster.o.… =`, and lists each option's methods by kind (an object or leaf: get, set, reset; a map: get, set, remove, has, keys, reset; a list: append, prepend, insert, remove, has, clear, reset). The runtime check fails until the runtime has them. (f) `modes.*.sidebar` is an object in the shape (its table form has `side` and `sections`) that also takes `true` or `false`. **Ticket 142 (#248), the runtime, agent's calls for the owner's review:** (g) **One store** (`config/store.rs`): `roster.o`, `roster.set`, `roster.keymap`, `roster.themes`, `roster.packages` and `roster.folders` write to it; it keeps what they add up to and a log of each write's path, file and line (never a copy of the value, so a list changed in place thousands of times stays cheap); the last write wins, `nil` or `reset` takes a write away (back to the default), and the loader lays it under the returned table, which still wins (kept: nothing argued for changing it; T143 documents it). (h) **`roster.o`** (`config/api.rs`) is a userdata per object, map or list on the shape; reading a value gives a copy or nil, reading a table gives its table with its kind's methods (`Kind::methods`: an object `get`/`reset`; a map `get`/`has`/`keys`/`reset` plus `m.KEY = nil`; a list `get`/`append`/`prepend`/`insert`/`remove`/`has`/`clear`/`reset`), and `roster.o.reset(name)` resets any option. A name is checked when it's written or read, and every list in a written value that takes each item once is checked for repeats; a value's type is checked when it's written (a plain value of a plain type directly, anything else by the schema for that option alone); forms, ranges and what depends on other options are checked once the file has run, still at the write's line. A write's line is where its statement starts (Lua reports a table over several lines at its last line, so the brackets that line closes are followed up to where they opened); a name inside such a table is reported at its own line; a write never moves to an earlier statement. Assigning one roster.o table to another option copies what it holds (what's set, else its default); one that holds neither is an error, never a silent removal. A list method on an unset list starts from the default when that is a list; `statusbar`, `toolbar` and unset sidebar sections must be assigned first (they mean "all"). `statusbar` and `toolbar` became lists in the shape; `terminal.font`, sidebar sections, statusbar, toolbar and a Folder's `hosts` take each item once. roster.o doesn't reach inside a list's items (`folders[1].name`): they change through their list. A map's key named like a method (`get`, `has`, `keys`, `reset`) reads as that key once it's set and as the method until then; `roster.o.reset(name)` always resets. `roster.o.packages:has`/`:remove` take a Package's name or source. (i) **Collections:** names are unique (a second add, or `themes.define` of a defined id, names both lines and says to use `update`: `define` no longer lets a later call win, noted in deprecated.md; `update` changes a Theme, a bundled one too, as `{ base = id }` plus the fields); `remove`/`update`/`del` of what isn't there is an error; `keymap.del` takes off only init.lua's bindings (a default is unbound with `false`); `themes.get` gives init.lua's spec or nil; `themes.list` gives the bundled ids (`shape::BUNDLED_THEMES`, checked against the app's list) then init.lua's; `packages.update`/`folders.update` take their fields as `add` takes a spec (the lazy.nvim form, no function as `config`) and check the item; with a tag, branch or commit `packages.update` drops the other two. (j) `roster.version` and `roster.config_dir` are read-only fields, and the `roster` table and its sub-tables are read-only. `roster.api_version` stays 4: it is the Plugin API's version too. `roster.set` checks names at its line like `roster.o`. (k) The coverage test also runs every option roster.o reaches through its kind's methods and its example (assign, read, reset), and checks every function, field and method has a help entry and a stub with the same summary. Every example in the reference is written out as `examples/config-check/init.lua`, which CI type-checks against the stubs with lua-language-server at Hint level; in the stubs `roster.o` is a class (`roster.o : roster.config.Options`) and a method's `self` is `any`, since lua-language-server has no separate read type for a field that also takes the plain forms. 5,000 writes load in well under the 1 s budget. **Ticket 143 (#249), the docs, agent's calls for the owner's review:** (l) config.md opens with "Writing init.lua" (one file, modules under `lua/`, errors and `:options` at the module's line, `:help options`, `:config types`, feature detection by the name being there, else `roster.version`, never `roster.api_version`) and states one precedence rule: calls run in order and the last wins, and the returned table wins over every call. Its option tables keep the words the reference's one-line summaries don't have, but lose their Default column (the generated reference has it), so nothing hand-written can carry a stale default; `config_shape.rs` still checks they name the same options, and the frontend's parser of those tables, which only its own test used, gives way to a check of its Config against the generated reference. `examples/init.lua` is one `roster.o` line per option, so `:config`'s commented-out starter takes any one line uncommented; `examples/init-split` is a split Config with a load test; `:config import-terminal` writes `roster.o` lines in the same marked block. A test loads every `lua` block of config.md, recipes.md and keyboard.md as an init.lua (a block starting `-- lua/….lua` is that module), and CI type-checks `examples/init.lua` and `examples/init-split` as a user's editor would (`types/` and a `.luarc.json` beside init.lua). |
| Q134 | The Plugin API's collections are complete: removing what isn't there is an error from Plugin API 5, and every collection reads back (ticket 145, #257) | **Agent's calls within the owner's "Lua objects are complete" rule (CONTRIBUTING.md "Lua API"), for the owner's review (2026-11-02).** (a) **By the manifest's `api`:** a Plugin whose manifest says `api = 5` gets an error at its line when it takes away what it never added (`roster.themes.remove`, `roster.keymap.del` or `set(…, false)`, every collection's `remove`, and `roster.views.update`/`open`/`close` of a view it doesn't have), worded as `init.lua`'s (`the Plugin has no Theme "dusk"`, `the Plugin binds no "w w" in leader`; errors.md row 159), and when it names a key context other than `leader`, `list` or `list_vim` (row 160; the app refused one with a notice). A Plugin that says `api = 4` or older keeps the quiet removals: the first-party Plugins (mesh and topology at 4, discover at 3) and the examples call `roster.status.remove`, `roster.progress.remove` and `roster.views.close` on what may be gone already, and third-party ones may too; turning a working Plugin into one that errors (and, at the third error, is disabled) for an upgrade of the app it didn't ask for would break the promise that `api` is what a Plugin was written against. Raising to `api = 5` is the author's step, and `docs/deprecated.md` lists the old behaviour. The alternative, errors for every Plugin at once, was rejected for that reason; recording why the Plugin API differs from `init.lua` forever was rejected as the rule's exception, which is the owner's call. (b) **`roster.api_version` is 5**, one number with `init.lua`'s module, which gains nothing. (c) **Reads are the Plugin's own:** each collection has `get`, `has` and `list` (keymap's take a context; `details.get(id, host)`, `has(id, host?)`, `list(id?)` for its Hosts). They say what the Plugin set, not what the app showed: a set the app refused (an id another Plugin has) still counts, since the runtime doesn't hear the app's answer, and a notice already told the user. `get` gives a deep copy of the table the Plugin gave (its functions the same functions), taken when it was set, so changing either later changes neither; a failed call's changes to them are undone with its ops. (d) **No `roster.views.get`:** a view's state is its set plus every update after it (rows by id, removals, a graph's nodes and edges), which only the app keeps; reading it back needs the core to apply updates as the app does, a new capability left as a follow-up. `has` and `list` are there. (e) **Left as follow-ups**, being new behaviour rather than consistency: `roster.themes.update` (a Plugin's `define` replaces), `roster.badges.get`/`list` (badges are set per Host with no id of the Plugin's), and `has` on `init.lua`'s `roster.keymap` and `roster.themes`. |
| Q135 | Config follow-ups from T141–T143: a read that always works, what wins over an import, examples through the real loader (ticket 144, #256) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review.** (a) **`roster.o.get(name)`** reads any option by its dotted name (a copy, or nil), as `roster.o.reset(name)` resets one: a map's key named like a method (`get`, `has`, `keys`, `reset`) shadows `:get()` and `:keys()` once set (Q132 h), so it is the read that always works and config.md's `pairs()` advice now points at it. An unknown name is an error at its line; `roster.o.get = …` is an error like `roster.o.reset = …`; `roster.o:get()` still gives everything set. A key with a dot in it can't be named this way, but it can't also be a method's name, so `roster.o.views["a.b"]` reads it. **Why not forbid those key names:** a View or Theme id is the user's (or a Plugin's) to pick, and refusing `get` would be a new rule. (b) **`:config import-terminal`'s preview** names a `roster.o` line after the block that sets an option the import sets (running the import again replaces the older block in place, so a line below it stays later and wins), at its `file:line`, beside the returned table's (named without a line: the loader's line for a returned table's value can point at the block). (c) **Examples load:** lua-language-server lets through a string outside a `"a"\|"b"` type, so a test loads every example of the `roster` table and its methods (alone, or after the examples that add Themes, Packages, Folders and keys when it changes one of them), and every init.lua block in `docs/`, `plugins/` and `examples/` (a block outside the Config's guides counts when it uses only init.lua's `roster` names), through the real loader. It found two examples that didn't load (`List:insert` on unset sidebar sections, `List:clear` on `terminal.font`), now `terminal.font:insert(2, …)` and `folders:clear()`. (d) errors.md's note says a new row takes the highest id plus one, so it no longer names a number that goes stale. |
| Q136 | Prompt-fill safety fixes: the token through split reads, and every fill at a prompt the cursor is right after (ticket 137, #243 (f) and (b)) | **Coordinator's ticket (2026-11-02, fixes only, within Q129's rule: fill only a prompt that is provably the Host's own, refuse whenever in doubt; Q129's exception unchanged: the core holds the secret for `session.fill` only). Agent's design, reworked after the focused review of the branch (coordinator's directions: never garble ordinary Plugin output; keep following the token after a hold; state the residual for the owner).** (a) **`TokenMask` (#243 f).** Q131 (a) let output that ended part-way into what may be the token out after 50 ms with the held part as it was unless it was 6 characters or more or followed `roster=`, and the rest of the token, in the next read, didn't start with the token's first character, so it wasn't recognised at all: an unlucky split could pass the whole token to Plugins. Now the mask is a stream matcher: it follows every start of the token across reads, through line breaks and any escape sequence a terminal or multiplexer draws inside a wrapped line (CSI of any length; OSC, DCS, APC, PM and SOS strings to their BEL or ST, so OSC 8 links and long titles; `ESC ( B` and other designators, as tmux's sgr0; two-byte escapes). Output from a start on is held until the read that decides it, at most 50 ms and 4 KB; output that can't be the token is never held (no added latency) and is passed as the same bytes. When a hold runs out, a start that follows `roster=` (skipping a wrap's line breaks and CSI) or has 4 characters or more is taken as the token: dashed, and every later character of it dashed as it comes, unheld; a shorter one elsewhere goes out as it was and is still followed, so the rest of the token is dashed. A hold that passes its 4 KB bound fails toward masking: every start it holds is dashed, however short, and followed on (the re-review of #266: a long OSC, DCS or APC string after a short start released it in clear). **Residual, for the owner:** at most the token's first 3 hex characters (12 of 96 bits) can reach a Plugin, only where the token shows without `roster=` before it (roster's hook line puts it after `roster=` everywhere, so a shell printing its prompt settings, functions or history always has it) and only when the output pauses 50 ms right after those characters (the one case: never at the size bound). Accepted by design here because the alternative garbles ordinary output: dashing every held start would turn about one echoed key in 16 into a dash for Plugins. Tests: every cut point and every pair of cuts across the token, the hold timing out after every read, byte by byte, for tokens with self-overlapping starts, and through each kind of escape sequence; ordinary output (echoed keys, hex ids, counters, progress lines) unchanged at every split; a stream of cursor moves holds once at most; a hold that never resolves is bounded. **Rejected:** dashing any held start when its time is up (the review: garbles ordinary output); dashing only from 6 characters with no follow-on (the leak); holding until the next read however long (unbounded latency); caps that end a match (a long OSC 8 link inside a wrapped token released both halves: the review). (b) **Every fill needs the cursor right after its prompt (#243 b).** Q129 checked a prompt only through the frontend's screen read and serve's judgement of the line; Q131 (g) added the core's check, for nested fills only, that the output's last 4 KB read as text ended with it. Now every fill goes through one check, in `SessionManager::fill` (which takes the prompt line and an optional `hop`; `fill_at_prompt` and the public `at_prompt` are gone, so no caller can check apart from filling): the core keeps a screen of the Session's output as a terminal draws it (`screen.rs`: cursor addressing, column sets, erased, inserted and deleted characters and lines, carriage-return overwrites, backspaces, tabs, scrolling, the alternate screen, the PTY's size; colours, titles and other strings draw nothing), and the cursor must sit right after the prompt on its line with nothing drawn after it, as the frontend's own check reads xterm's screen. So ConPTY's redraws count (the 4 KB text check refused them: the review), and a prompt that went by, or one overwritten without being erased, doesn't. The output and write counters are noted before the check, so output arriving during it refuses the fill as an echo. That refusal typed nothing, so it comes back as code 130 (serve's "won't fill this prompt", `backend::fill_refusal`, errors.md row 163; a nested ssh's in row 149) and the app offers the prompt again; refusals after the probe (an echo, a key) still have no code and stay spent. The fake backend makes the same check first, on its output's text. No serve API change. (c) **Not done: a login offered after a first-time host-key `yes` (#243 d).** Q131 (d) and (h) and security.md make the `yes` end the offer on purpose: ssh's host-key check on the first Host is what ties the name to a box, so roster never fills a login to a Host the first one didn't already know. Offering it after the `yes` would fill a password into a box whose key nobody verified before, a weaker guarantee; doing it without weakening needs a new capability (say, comparing the fingerprint ssh prints with the key roster recorded for that Host, which needs a serve API change), so it's left to the owner. |
| Q137 | This Box syncs after every Inventory change serve applies, outside edits included (T150, #269) | **Agent's call, for the owner's review (the owner asked: "I'd like it to run roster sync after I add a host").** After every change `roster serve` writes to `roster.yml` (`host.add`, `host.update`, `host.review`, removing, renaming and moving Hosts, the group methods, `discover.add`, Quick Connect's "Save to vault", saving an `auth: prompt` Host's Login, `mesh.cut`'s `mesh: false`, `changes.discard` and an `inventory.pull` that updated), it runs `roster sync` for this Box (its ssh config and Claude files), as `changes.publish` already did (Q13), so a Session to a Host just added opens without `roster sync`. One helper does it for every method. The sync runs after the change is written, so Q43's order (test login, Vault item, then the change) is untouched; a failed sync never undoes the change: the result's `sync: {ok, error?, fix?}` says so and the app shows it as a warning. **Outside edits (Q45) sync too, the same way, but only after a good load:** with `auto_reload` the watcher's reload syncs when the file loads (`inventory.changed`'s `sync`), and so does `:source` (`inventory.reload`) when the file changed. A file that doesn't load (a half-written save, a typo) keeps the last good Inventory (Q45) and syncs nothing, so the ssh config never follows a broken file and never moves back to an Inventory the file no longer says; the next good load syncs. An outside edit that isn't applied (no `auto_reload`, not `:source`d) syncs nothing until it is. *Why yes:* the ssh config should follow `roster.yml` as serve has it loaded, whoever wrote it; otherwise a Host added in vim shows in the Sidebar but its Session fails. *Alternatives:* syncing only serve's own writes (leaves the vim case broken); syncing on every change the watcher sees, loaded or not (would write from a broken file or a stale Inventory). No new API number: `sync` is a new result field the app reads only when it's there. |
| Q138 | Ctrl+V in a terminal: paste on Windows, the program's elsewhere (ticket 152, #271) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review (2026-11-02, fixes only; reworked after the focused review of #273).** (a) **What Ctrl+V does:** a new terminal action, `terminal.pasteIfText`. With text on the clipboard it pastes it through xterm.js's paste, as `terminal.paste` does (each line end as Enter, the whole paste between bracketed-paste marks when the program asked for them). With no text (empty, an image only, or the WebView refused) the key goes to the program as ^V (0x16), so an app's own image paste and Vim's block selection still work. It is not `terminal.paste`, because that one does nothing on an empty clipboard; Ctrl+Shift+V and Shift+Insert keep that. (b) **Windows only, by default:** Windows Terminal pastes on Ctrl+V. Linux and macOS terminals paste with Ctrl+Shift+V (or Cmd+V) and leave Ctrl+V to the program, where shells (`quoted-insert`) and Vim users rely on it. So the built-in binding `Ctrl+v` → `terminal.pasteIfText` is registered only when the app runs on Windows (known at start from the WebView's user agent, `platform/os.ts`). The action can be bound on any OS (`roster.keymap.set("terminal", "Ctrl+v", "terminal.pasteIfText")`) or unbound on Windows (`roster.keymap.set("terminal", "Ctrl+v", false)`). Alternative: everywhere; lost because it takes ^V from Linux users who never asked. (c) **The WebView pastes, roster doesn't read:** the Ctrl+V keydown is not prevented (xterm is told not to send ^V), so the WebView runs its own paste and fires a paste event at once with the clipboard as it is at the key. Checked in headless Chromium: the event comes before the keyup and before a 0 ms timer; an image-only clipboard gives an empty `text/plain` with type `Files`. With text, xterm's own paste listener pastes it, and the WebView is kept from also putting it in xterm's hidden text field. With no text, the program gets ^V. If no paste event comes by the keyup (or 500 ms with no keyup), the program gets ^V, and a paste event that still comes for that key within a second is swallowed, so the program never gets both ^V and the text; nothing waits. A repeat of a held Ctrl+V is prevented, so it pastes once. The first version read the clipboard itself (async `readText`), queued the keys typed meanwhile and merged a Ctrl+V and a paste event within 500 ms. The review found that it could freeze the terminal on a read that never answered, drop a dictation, and paste a stale clipboard (a tool that puts the old clipboard back after injecting Ctrl+V, where that old clipboard may hold a password). Letting the WebView paste removes all three: no read, no queue, no time window. (d) **Ctrl+V only:** `terminal.pasteIfText` works on Ctrl+V, the key the WebView pastes with. `init.lua` binding it to another key gets a warning and the binding is left out; on any other key (a Plugin's) the key goes to the program as if unbound. Ctrl+V is matched by its physical key too, so Ctrl+м on a Russian layout pastes as in Windows Terminal; every terminal chord on a letter matches that way, except with AltGr (Ctrl+Alt on Windows), which types its character (AltGr+A is ą on a Polish layout). (e) **Security unchanged:** the text goes only from the clipboard to the Session through xterm, never into a log, the State or a message ([security.md](security.md)). |
| Q141 | First-party Plugins on Plugin API 5, `roster.badges` complete, `has` on init.lua's keymap and Themes, Topology's Inventory without waiting (ticket 149, #262; #232, #239, #222) | **Agent's calls within the owner's "Lua objects are complete" rule (CONTRIBUTING.md "Lua API") and Q134, for the owner's review (2026-11-02).** (a) **roster's own Plugins say `api = 5`:** mesh, topology, discover and the examples (recent-alerts, pinned), and their stand-ins in the fake Backend, so they show and are tested under what a new Plugin gets. A removal of what may already be gone (a status segment, a progress) checks first with `has`; a test fails when one of them falls behind the current API. Their permissions are unchanged. (b) **`roster.badges` is a collection by Host:** `remove(host)` takes the Plugin's badge off, `get(host)` gives its text, `has(host)` and `list()` (its Hosts, sorted) say which it has; only its own, as every collection's reads are. `has` wasn't asked for in #262 but every collection has it (Q134 (c)). `set(host, nil)` stays as `remove`'s old spelling and is checked as `remove` is, as `roster.keymap.set(…, false)` is checked as `del`: on api 5 taking off a badge it doesn't have is an error at its line (errors.md row 159), on api 4 or older it does nothing. Rejected: a new error row of its own (it's row 159's kind of error), and leaving `set(host, nil)` quiet (two ways to remove, one silent, is the no-op the rule forbids). They join Plugin API 5, which no Release has shipped yet, so `roster.api_version` stays 5. (c) **init.lua's `roster.keymap.has(context, keys)`** is true for any binding init.lua made in that context, `false` (one of roster's own taken off) included, since `del` takes that off too. **`roster.themes.has(id)`** is true for any Theme `list` names, bundled or init.lua's, so `has` and `list` agree; whether init.lua has a spec of its own is `get`'s answer. (d) **Topology reads the Inventory without waiting** (`roster.inventory.request`, Q127), the Hosts first and then the Mesh, so one draw has both; an answer to a read made before an `inventory_changed` is asked again rather than drawn. (e) **The Mesh's 1,000-Host draw fits a Plugin call's 250 ms** in a release build even on a fifth of one core (performance.md, "A Plugin's call"), so nothing changed but an ignored timing test that measures it. Left as follow-ups, being features: `roster.views.get` and Plugins' `roster.themes.update` (#262). |
| Q142 | Packages and `:check` fixes: `:plugins remove` remembered like a cancelled install, a joined check's step at once (#213 item 1, #221 item 2) | **Coordinator's batch (2026-11-02, fixes only), agent's calls for the owner's review.** (a) **`:plugins remove NAME` on a Package `init.lua` still names is kept in the State's `packagesDeclined`** (name, git URL, `dir`), exactly as a cancelled install is, replacing Q117 (b)'s last sentence ("isn't remembered, and the next start asks"). Asking again at every start for something the user just removed was the same nagging Q117 (a) fixed for Cancel. Everything else is Q117 (a)'s rule: it holds while `init.lua` names the same repo and folder, another repo or folder asks, a sync that no longer finds the line forgets it, and `:packages install NAME` asks again and forgets it. The in-memory "removed this run" set is gone: the State answers both. `:plugins remove` now says "init.lua still names it: :packages install NAME brings it back". No new UI: `:packages` already shows such a Package as cancelled with that command. (b) **`:check HOST` asks `jobs.list` once as it starts** (serve API 21 or newer), and when the panel has heard no step yet and serve has a running check of that Host, the panel shows its step under way, as Q122 (c) and (h) mean it: honest while it runs, and in place when joined. A `job.progress` that came first wins (the list is older), and another Host's job, another kind or a check that ended changes nothing. The frontend asks through a new Tauri command `jobs_list` (`call` to serve's existing `jobs.list`, a quick read since API 20): its answer is the shape of `job.progress`, which already reaches the frontend, so nothing new crosses. No serve or Plugin API change, no motion (the step draws in as Q122 (d) already says). |
| Q143 | Graph layout: user-placed nodes, old overlaps, dense titles (#237 items 1 and 3) | **Agent's calls within Q99 and Q124, fixes only, for the owner's review (2026-11-02).** (a) **The State knows which nodes the user placed** (Q124 (b) amended): each graph in the State's `graphs` gets `moved`, the ids of nodes the user dragged or placed with `m` (a State saved before has none, and reads as before; a list that can't be read is ignored). Such a node keeps its place when its cluster moves, and is left out of the cluster's size (`r`) when clusters are kept apart; the circle drawn still takes it in, as a drag always made it. `m` then Esc puts the flag back as it was. (b) **An old overlapping layout heals** (Q124 (b) amended): with drags told apart, any placed cluster that overlaps another moves, whole, to the nearest free place, not only one that got new nodes; clusters that didn't grow settle first, so a grown one is the one that moves. A layout saved before (a) that has a dragged node over a neighbour can't be told apart from an old overlap, so on its first opening one of the two clusters may move once (the dragged node with it); after that the flag holds. Considered: healing only a State without `moved` (it would still move clusters the user dragged into, and keeps two rules). (c) **Dense-row titles give way instead of covering each other** (Q99 (m) and Q124 (c) amended): zoomed out on many clusters a title is far wider than its circle and the row gap is thinner than a line, so not every title fits; one that couldn't was cut to its circle (at least 80 px) and drawn anyway, over its neighbours' (500 clusters at zoom 0.2: 450 titles, 101 pairs over each other, 384 cut). Now a title that can't be whole is cut to the widest room that covers no other title, down to 80 px, and with no room it isn't drawn, as a node's label that would cover another isn't (same: 314 titles, none over another, 131 cut); zooming in brings it back. The selected node's cluster's title goes first and is always drawn. Considered: growing the layout's GAP with the title's height (the fitted zoom shrinks with the layout, so at 2, 3 and 4 times the row gap as many titles were cut at the fitted zoom; text is in screen pixels, the gap in the graph's) and hiding every title below a fixed zoom (drops titles that do fit). |
| Q144 | Prompt fill at sudo with `pwfeedback`: its `*` for the probe isn't an echo (ticket 154, #279) | **Owner's request (2026-11-02, #279: the sudo they use daily has `Defaults pwfeedback`, and roster refused it as an echo); coordinator's ticket, fixes only, within Q129's rule (fill only a prompt that is provably the Host's own; refuse whenever in doubt) and its exception unchanged (the core holds the secret for `session.fill` only). Agent's design, reworked after the focused security review of #283 (coordinator's directions: decide from what the terminal draws, not from byte strings; the whole line must be the prompt; own Host only this round; keep the stars from Plugins).** (a) **Why it was refused.** Q129 (e)'s probe (Ctrl+U, `x`, Ctrl+U) must print nothing. sudo's `tgetpass.c` with pwfeedback reads in cbreak mode with echo off and, in `getln()`, writes `*` per key and `\b \b` per `*` on the kill key (Ctrl+U); sudo's development branch since 2026-05 (commit 0abd1b1, after 1.9.17p2) also writes `ESC [ K` after every kill. So the probe drew a `*` and took it back, which the core counted as output, hence an echo (errors.md 145). On Windows the bytes reach the core through ConPTY, which renders its own stream, so the same drawing may come as other cursor moves and erases. (b) **The rule** (amended by Q151: (4) is now "the screen at the probe is exactly the screen at the check", and (6) compares the whole screen). `SessionManager::fill` now accepts output after the probe as "pwfeedback, not an echo" only when all hold: (1) no `hop` (the Session's own Host); (2) the prompt the fill names is exactly `[sudo] password for USER:` (whitespace runs as one, none at the ends) with USER the Session's ssh user (`sudo_prompt_for`), the same prompt serve accepts and Q129 (c) offers; (3) the cursor's whole line, read off the core's screen, is that prompt and nothing else (`Screen::line_is`, beyond the existing "cursor right after it"), so `Enter vault PIN for [sudo] password for ops:` doesn't pass; (4) nothing was printed between the fill's start and the probe (byte count and screen are under one lock with the reader, `Shown`); (5) from the probe to the end of the 800 ms wait, the screen's own parser (T137's `screen.rs`, `watch_mask`/`end_mask_watch`) saw at most one `*` drawn (one per printable key of the probe), any spaces, and otherwise only: backspace, carriage return, CSI cursor moves `A`-`H`, `a`, `d`, `e`, `f` and the backtick (HPA), CSI erases within the line `K` and `X`, each with only digits and `;` as parameters, and CSI `?25l`/`?25h` exactly; any other character, a line feed, tab or other control (also one inside a CSI), any other CSI (SGR, `J`, a mode other than `?25`) or one with private or intermediate bytes, any ESC sequence that isn't a CSI, an OSC/DCS/APC string, a broken or overlong CSI, or a resize marks it as not a mask; (6) the cursor (row, column, pending wrap) and its whole line end exactly as they were when the probe went. No key may have been sent meanwhile (`FillError::Typed`), as before. Otherwise `FillError::Echoes`, as before. Nothing is stripped from the stream: a hide inside another sequence (`ESC [ ESC[?25l K`) is read as a terminal reads it (a broken sequence, a hide, a `K` drawn) and refuses. (c) **Still refused:** the letter echoed (with or without its erasure); another mask, two stars, a colour or anything else drawn; the mask never taken back, or taken back after the 800 ms; output during the vault lookup; the mask at any other prompt (`Password:`, su's, a nested ssh's login prompt, sudo's for another user, a custom `sudo -p` prompt, a line that only ends with sudo's prompt); a nested Host's sudo with pwfeedback (the review's #4: a stale `hop` with the same user could send the hop Host's password to the outer Host's sudo; that root cause predates this ticket and is left to its own ticket, so the mask isn't extended to nested fills); a mask that leaves the cursor or line different (a prompt ending one column short of the edge). (d) **What shows, and why it's safe.** The mask shows the letter wasn't drawn: sudo read the key with echo off and drew a `*`. The password, typed next, is drawn as one `*` per character, taken back at Enter. So the password itself reaches no screen, scrollback, Plugin, frontend, log or error. Its length does show in the Session's terminal while sudo reads it, exactly as when the user types it at that sudo by hand: the user's own terminal, accepted. Plugins don't get it: the frontend (`tabs.ts`, `fillWindow`) keeps what the Session prints from Plugins from the fill's start until the Session's next line break (sudo's own after Enter) or the fill's refusal, and drops at most 4 KB if no line break comes. Which Host's password is asked for and where it goes are unchanged (Q129 (b)-(d), Q131 (g), Q136 (b)). A program that draws exactly this at exactly sudo's prompt and then prints what it reads is security.md's existing limit; it gets nothing a silent one doesn't. No serve API change, no new error code: refusals keep row 145. (e) **Alternatives.** Exact byte strings (the first version: the review found ConPTY likely re-renders `\b` as cursor positioning, so Windows, the owner's case, would always refuse, and stripping cursor toggles byte by byte could join a sequence across another); any run of `*` and backspaces (unbounded); the screen's end state alone without the parser's record (lets an echo erased at once through); a probe without the letter (proves nothing); turning pwfeedback off (not roster's to change); leaving it refused (the owner asked). (f) **Risks and limits.** Tested on Linux PTYs with ConPTY-style redraws (column sets, cursor back, erase characters, carriage return and cursor forward, cursor position, hidden cursor); real ConPTY output is unverified: a redraw outside the allowed set (a colour, a whole-line repaint with the prompt's text) refuses as an echo, never leaks. The fake backend mirrors it (`pwfeedback` per Host: sudo's prompt draws `*` and takes it back; the fake's fill types the probe first and accepts the mask only for the own Host at sudo's prompt for its ssh user, when the drawn line is that prompt again). |
| Q146 | Config fixes from T144: the returned table's line when it wins, and `OptionName`'s text (ticket 148, #261) | **Agent's calls, for the owner's review; fixes only.** (a) **The line `:options` shows** (`config.lines`) for a value the returned table sets over a `roster.*` call is found in the file's text, as before (Lua keeps no line for a table's field), but no longer inside a `roster.*` statement: `:config import-terminal`'s block defines a Theme whose `terminal = { … }` came before the returned table, so the search for `terminal.size` stopped in it and named the block's `roster.o.terminal.size` line. A statement starts on a line outside brackets and runs to where they close; one starting `roster.` is skipped. The same search places a schema error in the returned table, so that line is right too. A value no `roster.*` call won still gets no line rather than a wrong one (ticket 140). **Why not track the returned table's lines in Lua:** Lua keeps none for fields, and a debug hook per table constructor would cost every load for a best-effort line. (b) **`roster.config.OptionName`** says it's for `roster.o.get`, `roster.o.reset` and `roster.set`, and ends with `string`: it lists the fixed names for completion, and a map's keys are the user's (`"views.files.width"`), so any dotted name type-checks and the loader checks it at its line. Generated by `gen_config_options`. Q135 (b)'s preview still names the returned table without a line (a follow-up can give it this line now). |
| Q148 | Plugin network: the core follows redirects itself, so the lookup through the proxy holds up no thread (#225 item 6) | **Agent's calls within Q97 and Q116, a fix only, for the owner's review (2026-11-02).** (a) **The problem:** Q116 (d) looks a public name up here before the proxy gets it, at every redirect too. reqwest asks its redirect policy synchronously, so that lookup ran blocking on a network thread (`block_in_place`): while the system's resolver took its time, that request's own timeout couldn't fire and an unload couldn't stop it, and the first request's own lookup had no timeout at all. (b) **The fix:** the client follows no redirect (`redirect::Policy::none()`); the request's task follows each one itself, and before anything is sent to the next hop it runs the same checks in the same order as before: at most 5 redirects, the hop is one of the Plugin's sites (`allowed`), it stays on the same client (no redirect to or from a pinned site), then, through the proxy, the public name is looked up (the system's resolver on tokio's blocking pool, awaited) and refused if any address is inside (`proxied`). Every hop's connection still goes through the checking resolver (Q97 c) and the pinned verifier (Q116 c). None of the rules is looser; the messages are the ones errors.md already lists. (c) **One timeout for all of it, and lookups bounded:** the request's `timeout` (at most 30 s) now covers each lookup and each hop, so a slow lookup ends the request as "no answer within N s", and an unload ends it too. The request stops waiting, but the OS lookup itself can't be cancelled: it runs on, on tokio's blocking pool, until the system's resolver gives up on its own. So each Plugin has at most 4 lookups through the proxy waiting on the resolver at once (`MAX_LOOKUPS`, kept by the host across reloads), counting those a request already gave up on; with all 4 held, a request through the proxy fails at once (errors.md row 179) instead of piling up threads. (d) **What reqwest did on a redirect is kept, now written in roster-core:** a POST answered with 301 or 302, and anything but HEAD answered with 303, goes on as a GET without its body or its `Content-Type`/`Content-Length`/`Content-Encoding`; 307 and 308 keep the method and body; a 3xx without a `Location` that parses (read as UTF-8, as reqwest did) is the answer, and its own body (up to 64 KB) is read before the next hop so the connection can carry it. (d2) **Stricter than reqwest: to another scheme, host or port, only the Plugin's headers that carry no credential go along**: `Accept`, `Accept-Language`, `Cache-Control`, `Content-Language`, `Pragma`, `User-Agent` (what the answer should look like, not who asks), and `Content-Type` only with the body it describes. reqwest dropped only `Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Cookie2`, `Proxy-Authorization` and `WWW-Authenticate`, so a Plugin's `X-Api-Key`, `Private-Token` or `X-Auth-Token` went on to any of its other sites; a header name doesn't say whether it's a credential, so the list says what may go rather than what may not. A header dropped stays dropped for every later hop, back at the first site too. A Plugin that needs a header at the second site requests it there itself. Also stricter: a user or password in a `Location` is removed before the hop is sent, since the client would otherwise turn it into an `Authorization` header. (d3) **Each hop is counted by its own site** in `:plugins`' counts (Q116 e): a redirect from one granted site to another counts a request at each, and a failure at the hop where it failed. A refusal of an address with no origin names its scheme ("redirected to a file: URL"), never "null". (e) **Not done here:** #225 item 5 (CONNECT to the address roster checked, with SNI) stays the owner's call; the proxy still resolves the name itself after roster's check. *Note (2026-11-03): done in Q161.* Tests: `a_slow_lookup_at_a_redirect_holds_up_nothing_and_still_times_out`, `every_hop_through_the_proxy_is_looked_up_and_checked_before_its_followed` (network.rs), `lookups_given_up_on_cant_pile_up`, `a_redirect_elsewhere_keeps_only_headers_that_carry_no_credential` (network.rs), `a_redirect_keeps_or_drops_the_method_and_body_as_http_says`, `a_user_or_password_in_a_redirect_isnt_sent`, `a_redirect_to_another_site_takes_none_of_the_plugins_own_credentials_along`, `a_location_in_utf8_is_followed`, `a_redirect_to_a_file_data_or_protocol_relative_address_is_judged_as_any`, `each_hop_is_counted_by_its_site`, `a_redirect_on_the_same_site_reuses_its_connection`, `a_pinned_site_is_redirected_only_to_itself` (tests/network.rs). |
| Q149 | A program sets the clipboard with OSC 52, and right-click under mouse reporting is the program's (T157, #293) | **Agent's calls for a fix the owner asked for (2026-11-02: "Can't copy in Claude Code"), for the owner's review; the coordinator runs a security review.** (a) **The problem:** a full-screen program that turns on mouse reporting (Claude Code, vim with `mouse=a`, tmux with `mouse on`, htop) gets the drag itself and draws its own selection, so xterm.js has none, and the program copies it by sending the text in an OSC 52 escape, which roster ignored. Claude Code 2.1.316 does it when a drag ends (its `copyOnSelect`, on by default) and on Ctrl+C when that's off: it sends `ESC ] 52 ; c ; <base64 of the UTF-8 text> BEL` (ST under kitty; under tmux the same, plus tmux's DCS passthrough, which xterm ignores), read from its bundle. Windows Terminal handles OSC 52 writes (`OutputStateMachineEngine.cpp` `_GetOscSetClipboard`: it ignores the selection parameter, drops a `?` query without answering, and drops bad base64), which is why the same drag copies there. (b) **What a Host can now do:** set the clipboard's text, nothing else. Write only: a read request (`?`) is ignored and answers nothing, so no Host ever reads the clipboard (it may hold a password). (c) **The risk: clipboard poisoning.** A Host, or anything that prints into the Session (a file you `cat`, a log, a web page fetched with `curl`), can put a command on the clipboard that you later paste into another shell. Bracketed paste doesn't stop it: a pasted line still sits at the prompt, and you press Enter. (d) **The limits that keep it narrow:** only the Session on screen, decided where the sequence starts (hold.ts says which output it held while the tab was hidden, and osc52.ts `HeldOsc52Filter` takes out every OSC 52 begun in held output, up to its end, before xterm reads it, so one started in a background tab sets nothing even if it ends once shown; OSC 52 draws nothing, so the screen is unchanged and Q37 holds); only text that decodes as UTF-8; at most 1 MiB decoded (a full 10,000-line scrollback at 100 columns is about 1 MB, so no honest copy is near it, and it keeps a Host from pushing hundreds of MB through the WebView); selection `c`, `p`, `q`, `s`, `0`-`7` or none, all to the one clipboard (Windows Terminal takes any); an empty payload sets nothing (some terminals clear the clipboard with one; nothing honest needs that from a Host). It never reaches a log, `:messages`, the State or a Plugin: the Plugins' `terminal_output` comes from a terminal of its own that drops OSC sequences, which a test checks; the output roster keeps to explain a failure (tabs.ts `preAuth` and `late`, a Problem's tail) has OSC 52 taken out as it arrives (`Osc52Stripper`, split sequences too), and `terminalTail` also drops any base64-looking run of 64 or more characters and, for output not stripped that way, a payload-shaped remnant of an OSC 52 whose start was cut off (never a shell's bell after a prompt) (#295 review). Both strippers end a sequence exactly where xterm.js does: BEL, ST, ESC, CAN, SUB, or any other C1 control, which is then kept. `terminal.osc52 = false` turns it off. On by default, as Windows Terminal, because copying in these programs is the bug. (e) **Right-click under mouse reporting:** Windows Terminal sends a click without Shift to the program while it has mouse reporting on, right-click included, before its copy-or-paste rule (`ControlInteractivity.cpp` `PointerPressed`, `_canSendVTMouseInput`: Shift held, or no mouse mode, and it's the terminal's). roster now does the same: with nothing selected, it's the program's (xterm sends it the click) and nothing is pasted, which was the second half of #293 (the drag then right-click pasted). Shift+right-click pastes, and Shift+drag still makes a terminal selection (xterm's `shouldForceSelection`; on macOS that key is Option, and only with xterm's `macOptionClickForcesSelection`, which roster reads rather than assumes; roster turns it on, Q155). Only a pointer's context menu is the program's: one from the Menu key or Shift+F10 pastes as before. `rightClickSelectsWord` is off (xterm's default on macOS would select a word first and copy it).
