One difference: with a terminal selection (a Shift+drag), a right-click without Shift still copied it, where Windows Terminal would hand it to the program; there the selection can only be one you just made, and copying it is what you meant. *Superseded by Q166 (owner, 2026-11-03): roster now matches Windows Terminal, and that right-click is the program's.* (f) **Copied lines drop their trailing spaces** (every copy route: the keys, copy-on-select, right-click, and the WebView's own copy event, which roster handles before xterm's listener), as Windows Terminal's (`TextBuffer` `CopyRequest`, `trimTrailingWhitespace` for every selection but a block one): a full-screen program pads its rows with spaces, which xterm keeps. A line that wrapped still copies as one line. (g) **Pastes drop control characters** (coordinator's direction after T158's e2e suite found it): xterm.js puts a bracketed paste's text between the marks as it is, so a clipboard holding `ESC [ 201 ~` ended the paste early and what followed ran as typed, which matters more now that a Host can set the clipboard. Every paste route (Ctrl+V's paste event, now pasted by roster instead of xterm's listener, Ctrl+Shift+V, Shift+Insert, right-click) goes through `clipboard.ts pasteFilter`: every C0 but tab, CR and LF, DEL and C1 go, bracketed paste or not, exactly as Windows Terminal (`ControlCore::PasteText` calls `FilterStringForPaste(…, CarriageReturnNewline \| ControlCodes)`; `src/types/utils.cpp`). (h) **Alternatives:** OSC 52 off by default (lost: the owner's case works only after finding an option; Windows Terminal has it on); ask before each write (a prompt on every drag in Claude Code, and a Host could make one appear at any time, training you to say yes); only while the window has focus (a program copies when its drag ends, and the window has focus then anyway); `@xterm/addon-clipboard` (it also answers reads unless told not to, and checks nothing of the above: a handler of our own on xterm's `registerOscHandler` is 30 lines we control); a smaller cap such as 100 KB (a long log or diff copied in vim could pass it). |
| Q150 | Shell hooks: the prompt read off the Session's screen, and never at a line that asks for a secret (ticket 159, #296) | **Agent's calls within Q130's rule (send only at an empty prompt roster knows; when in doubt, send nothing), fixes only, reworked after the coordinator's security review of #298, for the owner's review (2026-11-02).** (a) **The prompt is read off a terminal, not the output's text.** Before, the check took the text after the last `\r` of the last 1 KB of output, which is empty at every real fish prompt (fish ends it with `ESC[K \r ESC[<n>C`), so fish's was never seen. Each Session's shell hooks now feed all its output to their own xterm `Terminal` (`promptScreen.ts`, the same library and size as the tab's, as the fill's `PromptWatch` does, ticket 119) and read the cursor's line from its buffer: `cursorY`/`cursorX`, the line's wrapped rows, the two rows above and the row below. Wrapping, cell widths, scroll regions, resets, inserts and deletes are a terminal's own. A hand-written line model came first and lost: the review reproduced nine ways it read a line the screen didn't show (unknown cells as blanks after a move, unhandled sequences, a cut tail, code points for cells). It's the Session's screen, not the view's: a tab that isn't on screen has it too, and the view's terminal (`app/src/terminal/`) isn't touched. Output it hasn't drawn yet isn't judged: the idle check looks again later, `:integrate` waits for it. (b) **Huge counts are capped before they reach it**: each CSI parameter is given as at most 9999 (more than any screen's rows or columns, so nothing draws differently; leading zeros go, as xterm reads `0005` as 5), as xterm 6 takes over a minute on `ESC[99999999M` and doesn't end `ESC[99999999L`. A CSI begun with the C1 introducer U+009B is clamped as one begun with `ESC [`. Nothing of a CSI ever reaches the screen as text: it goes whole at its final byte, or unfinished where ESC, CAN, SUB or a C1 control ends it, as a terminal ends it; past 256 bytes of parameters, more are dropped (xterm reads 32 at most), and the sequence still waits for its final byte. So the screen draws what the tab draws (tests compare it with a plain xterm). The tab's own terminal has no such cap yet (T163, #305). (c) **Only at the end of an idle prompt**: refused when anything is on the cursor's line after the cursor (its wrapped rows included: a recalled command after Up then Home, a right-hand prompt such as zsh's `RPROMPT`, which was never sent to before either), or when the screen is the alternate one (a full-screen program). The pattern must match the line up to the cursor, so the cursor is at the prompt's end. (d) **A line that asks for a secret is never one**: the cursor's line, the two rows above it and the row below are checked for `password`, `passphrase`, `passcode`, `Passwort`, `Kennwort`, `mot de passe`, `contraseña`, `senha`, `密码`, `パスワード`, `one-time`, `verification code` anywhere, and `PIN`, `OTP`, `token` (optionally `… code`) as a word of their own at the end of what's asked or before `:`, `?`, `>`, `#`, `$`, `%` (so `Enter your PIN` above a `$ ` refuses, a branch `(pin-deps)` or a folder `~/tokens` doesn't). A folder or branch with one of the long words in it refuses too, which fails safe, and so does a shell prompt right below a password prompt (after sudo, until two more rows are printed). (e) **fish's pattern takes its default prompt's parts, strictly**: the version-control part is ` (…)` with no space, parenthesis, `<` or `>` inside but one nested pair (a detached HEAD's `((f5c479ed))`), then a failed status (` [1]`, a pipeline's ` [1\|0]`), then `>` or `#`. An informative git prompt with spaces (`(main\|REBASE-i 1/3)`) and anything else in parentheses with a space (`(mysql db)`) isn't fish's. A branch-like word (`(mysql)`) can't be told from a branch, and passes. (f) **Escape sequences with intermediate bytes** (charset designators such as fish's `ESC ( B`) are taken out whole in the echo check; before, the final byte stayed in the text. The hook line itself is unchanged. |
| Q151 | Prompt fill under Windows' ConPTY: only sudo's own prompt for the Session's own Host, through repaints; a two-step probe; keys held during a fill; the screen compared whole (ticket 155, #284 item 1, the owner's Windows log, the security review of #299) | **Coordinator's ticket (2026-11-02, urgent, fixes only), from the owner's own Windows build; (i) is the coordinator's ruling on the focused security review of #299. Within Q129's rule (fill only a prompt that is provably the Host's own; refuse whenever in doubt) and its exception unchanged (the core holds the secret for `session.fill` only). Amends Q136 (b) and Q144 (b) (4) and (6); everything else in them stands.** (a) **What the owner saw.** At `[sudo] password for USER:` with `pwfeedback` on the Session's own Host, one press of the fill key logged (#288's diagnostic) `prompt fill refused at sudo's prompt: the probe drew nothing; 579 bytes printed before the probe`. ConPTY renders the Session's output on its own screen and sends the app only what changed there, so sudo's `*` and its erasure, drawn and taken back between two of its frames, arrived as nothing. What refused was Q144 (b) (4), "nothing printed between the fill's start and the probe", a byte count: ConPTY repaints regions and the viewport it already drew (here while the vault answered), so bytes arrive though nothing on the screen changed. A second press logged a full redraw during the probe (`Sorry, try again.` and a new prompt: sudo had taken an Enter); that must keep refusing, and does. (b) **Before the probe.** As the fill checks that the cursor is right after the prompt, under the same lock as the reader (`Shown`), the core notes its whole screen and the cursor (`Screen::snapshot`): every cell's whole character and style, the size, main or alternate screen, the cursor (row, column, pending wrap), how many resizes there have been and how many things it doesn't model it has seen. When the probe is due, under the same lock: **off ConPTY** (Linux, macOS) nothing may have been printed since the check and there may have been no resize, as before (the review's #4: a repaint is no reason to discard real evidence there); **under ConPTY** what was printed must have left all of that exactly as it was (`Screen::matches`), so a repaint of the same content passes and any changed cell or style, a cursor elsewhere, a resize (even one put back), the alternate screen, or anything unmodelled refuses. Either way a refusal here comes **before anything is typed** (the review's #3: `FillError::Changed`, code 130, errors.md row 163), so the prompt stays offered and nothing reaches it. (c) **The mask watch compares the whole screen (#284 item 1).** `Screen::end_mask_watch` compared only the cursor's row, so `ESC[1;1H*ESC[2;26H` (a `*` drawn on another row, the cursor put back) passed. It now needs the whole screen and the cursor exactly as at the probe; its log says `another line changed`. (d) **Why the screen rule is as strict as the byte rule, under ConPTY.** The byte rule's job before the probe was to catch the Session doing something while roster looked away: a new prompt, a message, the prompt gone. Each of those changes what the screen shows or where the cursor is, so it refuses here too. What passes is output that leaves every visible cell, its style and the cursor as they were, which shows the user, and the fill, the same prompt in the same place. The comparison is with the screen at the check, not with the prompt alone, so an identical-looking prompt drawn lower down, a scroll, or a changed line elsewhere refuses. The screen (the review's #5) keeps each cell's whole UTF-8 character and the style (SGR: intensity, italic, underline, blink, reverse, hidden, strike, overline, the three colours) it was drawn with, and erases leave blanks in the current style; anything it doesn't model counts as a change: a repeat (`REP`), insert or newline mode, a scroll region, the alignment test and double-size lines (`ESC #`), a charset other than ASCII or a shift, origin, wrap, reverse-video or 132-column mode, tab stops, a full reset, a style it can't name, any CSI it doesn't act on except reports, device attributes, window ops and the cursor's shape. (e) **The log (ticket 155).** The app's log line names the step that refused, never text: `prompt fill refused before the probe: what was printed after the prompt check (N bytes) changed the screen`; at sudo's own prompt for the own Host, `with the probe's letter pending, the screen showed more than sudo's prompt and at most its `*`; the probe drew …`, `the cursor's line isn't only sudo's prompt; …`, or `the probe drew …, not sudo's pwfeedback mask as roster takes it` (with `; N bytes printed before the probe repainted the screen as it was` when there were some); the description says `the line changed`, `another line changed` or `something roster's screen doesn't model`. (f) **Alternatives.** Dropping the byte check before the probe (lets a changed screen through); allowing a list of repaint byte forms (ConPTY's vary with what changed and when); comparing only the cursor's line (a new prompt or a message elsewhere passes); waiting for ConPTY to go quiet (adds latency and still needs a rule for what was drawn); a screen rule after the probe (an echo erased at once leaves the screen as it was); the screen rule off ConPTY too (discards real evidence: the review's #4). (g) **Tests.** Linux PTYs with stand-ins that repaint like ConPTY (the viewport line by line with erases, the prompt's line with erased characters, the cursor alone, the cursor hidden and shown) and draw nothing for the probe fill at sudo's prompt with pwfeedback under ConPTY's rules; changed screens (sudo's retry, another line, a line below, the cursor elsewhere), a repaint off ConPTY, and a resize refuse before anything is typed (a stand-in records every byte it reads: none). Screen tests: another character with the same first byte, another style, and each unmodelled sequence break the match; equivalent SGR resets (`ESC[m`, `ESC[0m`, `ESC[0;1m`) don't. (h) **The probe in two steps (the coordinator's review of #299).** ConPTY sends only what changed between frames, so a prompt that echoed the probe's `x` and took it back (its Ctrl+U) within one frame would send nothing. `SessionManager::fill` now writes Ctrl+U and the letter, waits the whole `wait` (800 ms in the app, `FILL_WAIT`), checks, then writes the last Ctrl+U (always, so the line ends empty) and waits again before the final check. With the letter pending: off sudo's own prompt nothing may have been printed; at sudo's own prompt for the own Host the mask watch must have seen nothing it doesn't allow, every row but the prompt's must be as it was, and the cursor's line must be the prompt alone or the prompt and one `*`, the cursor right after it (`Screen::mask_pending`); otherwise the fill refuses at once. A resize at any step refuses (the review's #6). This keeps an echoed letter on the far terminal for a whole `wait` whatever the round trip, which makes an echo far likelier to be drawn in a frame; it doesn't make that certain (a network stall can still bring the echo and its erasure together, the review's #1), which is why (i) exists. A fill takes about two `wait`s (1.6 s). Tested with a stand-in that sends only frame diffs (it redraws its line every 50 ms, only if it changed): the one-write probe typed the secret into its echoing prompt; now it refuses, and silent and pwfeedback prompts fill. (i) **Under ConPTY, only sudo's own prompt for the Session's own Host is filled (coordinator's ruling, 2026-11-02, on the review of #299, #1).** *The rule it narrows:* Q129 lets a fill type at any prompt serve accepts once the probe shows it doesn't echo. *What's allowed under ConPTY:* only the Session's own Host's fill (no `hop`) at exactly sudo's own prompt for its ssh user (`sudo_prompt_for`), with every check above. *What's refused:* everything else, before anything is looked up or typed (`FillError::ConPty`, no code, errors.md row 183): a plain `Password:`, su's, ssh's `USER@HOST's password:`, a nested ssh's Host's login or its sudo, sudo's prompt for another user. Linux and macOS keep every prompt Q129 and Q131 allow. *Why:* the probe is roster's only evidence that a prompt doesn't echo, and under ConPTY that evidence can vanish: ConPTY sends what changed between its frames, so an echo and its erasure that arrive together (a network stall bunches them, whatever the probe's timing) send nothing, and an echoing prompt would look silent; roster would then type the password where it's drawn on screen. sudo's own prompt is different: not echoing there is sudo's contract (it turns echo off itself, or draws `*` with pwfeedback, which the mask path handles), so the probe there checks a promise rather than being the only evidence, and the prompt check, the whole-line rule and the Session's own Host keep it to that sudo. *Alternatives:* longer or more probe steps (no timing beats a stall); a probe the prompt must answer visibly (none is portable: a silent prompt draws nothing by design); ConPTY's passthrough mode (experimental, only in recent Windows builds, and not exposed by roster's PTY layer); refusing all fills on Windows (loses the owner's daily sudo, the case #284 is for). *Risks and limits:* a sudo whose prompt was customised to look like sudo's own and that echoes is a sudo configured against its contract; on Windows the other prompts are typed by hand, as before #299; whether a Session is under ConPTY is decided by the platform (`cfg!(windows)`, `SessionManager::new`), so a Windows Session is always under these rules, and tests apply them on Linux PTYs (`SessionManager::with_conpty_rules`). (j) **The rest of the review of #299.** Keys sent to a Session while a fill checks its prompt are held in the core (`SessionManager::write`) from the fill's start until it ends, then sent in order, and still refuse it (`FillError::Typed`) (#2): an Enter typed during the wait can't land between the probe's letter and its Ctrl+U, where sudo would take `x` as an attempt. The core is where every writer meets (keys, paste, Plugins), so the hold is there rather than in the frontend; a second fill while one checks is refused (`FillError::Busy`; T155 item 3). However a fill ends, its mask watch ends and the held keys go (#8, a drop guard). The pending step's label says what it checked (#7); one comparison serves every check (#9). No serve API change. (k) **ConPTY's repaints, and what the refusal says (ticket 155c, #284 item 5, coordinator's ticket, fixes only).** *What the owner saw:* on their Windows build (dev at e9c91ca, with #299 and #308), at `[sudo] password for USER:` with pwfeedback on the own Host, three presses logged `prompt fill refused before the probe: what was printed after the prompt check (655 bytes) changed the screen` (and 696, 792): ConPTY repainted while the vault answered and the core's screen came out different from its snapshot. *Why, from ConPTY's renderer (microsoft/terminal, `src/renderer/vt/`):* it repaints each dirty row as its text, then its trailing blanks as `ECH`/`EL` (or as spaces, under nine of them or on a new bottom row) with the brush its own buffer holds for those cells, whose foreground and intensity can be anything (conhost erases with the current colours); its SGR forms are `ESC[m`, `ESC[1m`/`ESC[22m`, `ESC[3Xm`/`ESC[9Xm`, `ESC[38;5;Nm`, `ESC[38;2;R;G;Bm`, `ESC[39m`/`ESC[49m`, `ESC[4:3m` and `ESC[58:5:Nm`; it moves the cursor with `CSI H`, `CR LF`, `CR`, `LF`, `BS` and `CUF`, hides and shows it, sets the title (`OSC 0`) and hyperlinks (`OSC 8`), and sends the cursor's blink (`?12`) and shape (`CSI q`) and `ESC[3J`. The core's screen erased with the whole current style and kept a space with its foreground, so a blank repainted in another colour, though it looks the same, was a changed cell; and it took `ESC[3J` as clearing the screen. *The fix:* an erase leaves the background colour alone (as xterm.js erases); a space keeps only what shows on a blank (its background), unless it's reversed, underlined, struck through or overlined, when it keeps all of its style; SGR values are canonical (numbers, `;` or `:` forms alike, a true colour's colour space ignored as xterm.js does), with 16-colour, 256-colour and true colour kept apart (bold in a 16-colour green draws bright, in a 256-colour one it doesn't); `ESC[3J` right after `ESC[2J` (ConPTY's `cls`) draws nothing; a CSI too long to keep and a colour out of range or cut short count as unmodelled (they refuse). Everything that shows differently still refuses: a blank in another background, underline, reverse or strike, another character or style, the cursor elsewhere. Nothing else is widened: under ConPTY still only sudo's own prompt for the own Host, the two-step probe, held keys, the mask watch's rules. *The log:* `FillError::Changed` carries what differed (`Screen::diff`), so the line goes on `changed the screen: ` and names the cursor (`the cursor moved from 2:26 to 3:1`), the size, resizes, the alternate screen, each changed row (at most 8, then how many more) with its columns and `characters` or `styles only` with the first changed cell's styles as SGR parameters (`fg default -> 37, bold off -> 1`), and the last 16 unmodelled things named as (e) names sequences (`unmodelled: CSI[3?`: parameters only when they're digits and `;`, a command only when it's in (e)'s list, else `?`), never a character; the line is cut at 1200 bytes. If the owner's next log still shows a refusal, it says which of these differed. *Tests:* ConPTY-repaint stand-ins in those forms (coloured blanks erased and written, SGR in other forms, 256 and true colour set and back, titles, the cursor's blink and shape, `ESC[3J`) compare equal and fill through a PTY; the same forms drawing something that shows refuse; the diff never holds printed text. (l) **After the focused security review of #318 (coordinator's principle: wherever the core's screen can't mirror xterm.js exactly, it counts the output as unmodelled and refuses; it never guesses).** The screen now follows xterm.js 6.0.0's own code (`InputHandler.ts`, `Params`, `UnicodeV6.ts`) where it acts, and refuses where it doesn't: `EL` and `ED` with a parameter xterm.js ignores do nothing (#1); a CSI with sub-parameters outside SGR, a byte other than digits and `;`, a marker other than `?` on a DEC mode, more than 32 parameters or 32 sub-parameters, an intermediate (but the cursor's shape) is unmodelled (#2, #5); SGR is read as `charAttributes` reads it (`4:` and `4:N` past 5 are a single underline, sub-parameters only on 4, 38, 48 and 58; SGR 6 and the other attributes xterm.js ignores are unmodelled) (#3); colours only in the forms `_extractColor` reads plainly (`38;5;N`, `38;2;R;G;B`, `38:5:N`, `38:2:CS:R:G:B`), `38:2:R:G:B` (which xterm.js reads as RGB(G,B,0)) unmodelled (#4); `ESC[3J` only right after `ESC[2J`, else unmodelled (#6); UTF-8 is decoded whole, a stray, cut-short or invalid byte unmodelled (#7); the log shows only (e)'s commands (#8). Styles are values in each cell and each snapshot, with no table to fill, so a long true-colour session can't exhaust it and the log names the snapshot's own styles after a reset (#9, #10). The same reading of xterm.js found more, fixed the same way: a pending wrap (the cursor past the last column) survives SGR, strings, DEC modes, tabs, scrolls and erases as in xterm.js, and an erase to the end from it erases nothing on that line; C0 controls inside a CSI act; BEL ends only an OSC; `IL` and `DL` put the cursor at the line's start; `SD` scrolls in default blanks; DECSC and DECRC keep the cursor and the colours per screen as xterm.js does (a restore after the main screen scrolled, of an unknown alternate-screen save, or with an underline's kind or colour involved is unmodelled); `?1049` saves, fills the alternate screen with the erase's background and restores; `?47`, `?1047`, `?1048` and reverse wrap (`?45`) are unmodelled; a CSI with a private marker xterm.js doesn't register (`?A`) is unmodelled; a palette change (OSC 4, 10 to 19, 104 to 119) is unmodelled; a character xterm.js gives two columns or none is unmodelled, and while one is on the screen it never matches (columns after it wouldn't line up). *Checked against xterm.js:* one table of cases (`crates/roster-core/tests/fixtures/repaints.json`, from `scripts/gen-repaint-cases.py`) runs through the core's screen and a real xterm.js (`app/src/terminal/repaints.test.ts`), each case marked the same, changed, refused, or refused whatever xterm.js shows; and 1000 random repaints (`repaint-fuzz.json`, a fixed seed) carry the core's verdicts, written by the Rust test, which xterm.js must agree with wherever the core says the same (80,000 random repaints locally: no disagreement). (m) **After the second review of #318.** Once anything the screen doesn't model is printed (a mode, a charset, a scroll region, an SGR it can't read, a wide or zero-width character, synchronized output `?2026`…), even long before a check, it may no longer line up with xterm.js, so it notes that it lost track (sticky) and nothing matches until a full reset (`ESC c`, RIS), which xterm.js and it both start over from (#1, #2, #6, #8; this replaces the per-check scan for wide characters). A palette change doesn't change how later output is read, so it counts only between a check and the probe. The log says `roster's screen lost track of the terminal at …` with what did it. A screen that lost track also never says a prompt is at the cursor (`cursor_after`, `line_is`, the probe's pending mask), so every fill refuses at the prompt check, before anything is looked up or typed (`FillError::LostTrack`, code 130, errors.md row 163, its log line `prompt fill refused at the prompt check: roster's screen lost track of the terminal at …`). That holds on every platform: on Linux and macOS too, a Session that printed such a thing (a CJK character, an emoji with a variation selector, a curses program's line-drawing charset…) refuses every fill until `reset` in it. That's the price of never guessing. The prompt checks (`cursor_after`, `line_is`) compare whole characters, not first bytes (#4). Debug on what the log is made of says what Display says, never the bytes (#5). `ESC[3J` counts as right after `ESC[2J` only if no other sequence came between, an ESC in any state or a C1 CSI included (#7). `ED 1` from the last column is unmodelled: xterm.js 6.0.0 throws there with no scrollback yet and drops the rest of its input. The shared cases can hold raw bytes in chunks (a stray `0x80`, `0xC0`, `0xF5` and up, a character cut short, a C1 CSI split across chunks), xterm.js's side reads them through the app's own output pipeline (`screenWriter`: the OSC 52 filter and CsiClamp), and the random repaints also record where the core reads the prompt (`cursor_after`), which xterm.js must agree with wherever the core hasn't lost track (24,000 more random repaints locally: no disagreement). The mask watch's own rules (which sequences the probe may draw) have no xterm.js counterpart; its screen comparison is `matches`, checked above. |
| Q153 | A huge CSI count is capped at 9999 before every xterm roster feeds a Session's output to (ticket 163, #305; found by T159, #298) | **Agent's calls for a fix the coordinator ticketed, for the owner's review (2026-11-02, fixes only).** (a) **The problem.** xterm.js 6 works through a CSI count one step at a time: IL, DL, SU and SD take about 0.75 µs per count (`ESC[99999999M` over a minute, all of it on the webview's one thread), IL at that count runs out of memory, and REP is slower per count still. Anything a Session prints can carry one (a remote Host, `cat` of a crafted file, a log), and it froze the tab's visible terminal and with it the whole app; the same output also reaches PromptWatch's terminal (the fill's prompt check), CommittedLines' (the Plugins' `terminal_output`) and the shell hooks' prompt screen. (b) **The fix.** One streaming filter, `CsiClamp` (`app/src/terminal/csiClamp.ts`), in front of each of those terminals, caps every CSI parameter and sub-parameter at 9999. 9999 is more rows and columns than any screen has, so a move, erase, insert, delete, scroll, tab or CUP draws exactly as the count sent would; only REP repeats fewer times past 9999 (a real program never asks for more). (c) **It changes nothing xterm draws.** It is T159's prompt-screen clamp (#298), moved to one shared module and used by all four. It follows xterm.js's own parser (`EscapeSequenceParser.ts`) for where a CSI starts and ends: `ESC [` (a control between the two runs there, and it's still a CSI) and the C1 form U+009B, anywhere, since xterm.js reads U+009B as a CSI even inside an OSC or DCS string; the CSI ends at its final byte, ESC, CAN, SUB, a C1 control or a character past ASCII (xterm.js drops the character and leaves the CSI, so the digits after it are text and stay as they came). A CSI is held until it ends and comes out whole, so nothing of it is ever shown as text the tab wouldn't show; controls inside it come out where they stand, as xterm runs them there. Leading zeros go (`0005` is 5 to xterm), and parameter bytes past 256 are dropped (xterm reads at most 32 parameters, which fit well inside), so a CSI that never ends holds a bounded amount. OSC and DCS contents (OSC 52's base64, a title, OSC 133's marks) are never touched, and output with no CSI changes nothing (the same array). Tests draw each case through the clamp and through a plain xterm and compare the screens. (d) **Alternatives that lost.** Cancelling a CSI past some length (an early version of T159's clamp): what xterm then shows of its tail differs from the tab's terminal. A CSI handler in xterm.js that refuses big counts: it can't hand a smaller count to xterm's own handler, so it would have to drop the sequence (drawing differently from any terminal) or redo xterm's work. Clamping in the Rust core: the PTY's bytes also go to logs and the CLI as they came, and the cap answers xterm.js's weakness, so it sits next to each xterm. (e) **Limits.** Only CSI parameters; a DCS's parameters aren't capped (xterm.js runs no counted DCS). Upstream: this is xterm.js's to fix (a count past the screen's size could be capped where it's parsed, as xterm and Windows Terminal do); a report to xterm.js is drafted for the owner to file, and the filter stays until a release fixes it. |
| Q154 | Prompt fill follow-ups: Windows offers only what the core fills, a nested ssh's fill only while that ssh runs, nothing left set when a Session closes mid-fill (ticket 162, #304; #284 items 2 and 4) | **Agent's calls for fixes the coordinator ticketed, for the owner's review (2026-11-02, fixes only; nothing more may be filled anywhere).** (a) **Windows offers only sudo's own prompt for the Session's own Host** (#304 item 1). Under ConPTY the core refuses every other prompt (Q151 (i), errors.md row 183), so offering them showed a fill that always refused. The core says, when a Session opens, whether its PTY is ConPTY (`session_open`'s `conpty`, from `SessionManager::conpty`, so the rule's source is the core that enforces it, not a second guess of the platform); the frontend's PromptWatch then turns every prompt the core won't fill there into one roster won't fill, with why (row 185): no Fill button, and `␣ p` says so without asking the core. A prompt it already refused keeps its own reason. The core's refusal stays the guard. Output that arrives before the open answers is judged again once it does (and `␣ p` always reads the prompt afresh). (b) **The fake models it** (#304 item 2): `FakeScenario.conpty` makes each Session say `conpty` and its fill refuse as the core does (no code, before anything is looked up or typed), so fake-mode tests (App.fill.test.tsx) catch the app offering such a fill again. (c) **A nested ssh's fill only while that ssh runs** (#284 item 2, the review of #283, #4). The frontend's view of where the Session is lags the core's by a moment: the ssh can have ended, or another command started, while the frontend still shows the hop, and the first Host's prompt can read the same (`[sudo] password for ops:` with the same user), so the hop's password would go to the first Host. The core now knows positively: the frontend passes the attempt's token (Q131 (a)) to `session_open` (`marks`), the core's screen parser follows the OSC 133 marks with `roster=TOKEN` in the output (ended by BEL, or by ESC as xterm.js ends an OSC; CAN and SUB abort one; a screen reset keeps what it has seen), and counts their `C`s. The hop the frontend sends carries `run`, which of those commands its ssh is (PromptWatch counts every `C` with the token, the alternate screen's too, as the core does). A hop is filled only while the last mark with the token is a `C` and the count is `run`; otherwise `FillError::NotInHop` (row 184, code 130: nothing typed) before the vault is asked. Only the Session's own shell knows the token, so neither the far Host nor anything it runs can make or fake those marks; a mark the core misses or counts that the frontend doesn't only refuses. Output after the check refuses the fill as before, so the check needs no repeat. `run` never reaches serve (it gets the Host only). Alternatives that lost: the token sent with the fill (the core would have to remember every token-shaped mark, which output could flood); only "the last mark is a `C`" (the first Host's own next `sudo` starts with a `C` too, so the count is what tells that ssh from a later command); doing it all in the frontend (the frontend is what lags). The Session's own Host's fill isn't changed (that would be new reach for this rule; listed as a follow-up). Extending sudo's pwfeedback mask to nested fills, which #284 suggested once this holds, would widen what's filled and is left to the owner. (d) **Nothing left set when the writer is gone** (#284 item 4). T155b's `Filling` guard already ends the mask watch and takes the held keys however a fill ends; the tests now pin it: a Session closed while its fill checks (the PTY's writer gone, the Session still listed because its process ignores the hang-up) leaves no mask watch and no held keys, keys sent after fail as for any closed Session, and the next fill isn't Busy. (e) **After the focused security review of #308.** What (c) proves, exactly: that the Session's own shell is still in the same command run `run`, by marks only a shell that got roster's hook line should make; with the last token mark that command's `C`, no `D`, `A` or `B` of the shell's came since, and the prompt check every fill makes (the cursor right after the prompt the app judged) still holds. What it doesn't: that the nested ssh itself still runs (inside a longer command, a shell function or script running `ssh` then `sudo`, the ssh can end with the command still running), or that the marks are the Session's first shell's (a hook line sent into a nested shell, by `:integrate` there, makes that shell's marks carry the token, and the core counts them). So the frontend's hop tracking (Q131) stays the primary guard, and the core's check is defence in depth; security.md and errors.md row 184 say so. Hardening from the review: the core follows only a token of 8 to 64 lowercase hex characters, else none (an empty or short one could be guessed); a hop with no `run` (an older app) is refused as `NotInHop`, not as a malformed call; the core reads marks exactly as xterm.js's parser does (CAN and SUB abort a string and the text after is drawn, the C1 controls U+0080 to U+009F act as xterm.js's do, among them ST ending an OSC and U+009D starting one, the C0 controls xterm.js ignores inside an OSC are dropped, the id is read as a number so `0133` is 133), checked against PromptWatch's count on one shared set of cases (`crates/roster-core/tests/fixtures/own-marks.json`, run by both); only an OSC that may be a mark is kept, and only while a token is followed. On Windows the offer judges sudo's prompt by the user the core fills for (`session_open`'s `user`, `session.prepare`'s login), not the Inventory's, which can differ. |
| Q155 | Copied lines end with CR LF on Windows; on macOS, Option takes the mouse back from a program (T160, #302; follow-ups from T157, Q149) | **Agent's calls for two fixes the coordinator ticketed (2026-11-02, fixes only), for the owner's review.** (a) **Line ends on copy:** Windows Terminal ends each copied row that didn't wrap with `\r\n` (microsoft/terminal `src/buffer/out/textBuffer.cpp`, `TextBuffer::GetPlainText` appends `L"\r\n"` between rows; `_RowCopyHelper` leaves it out after a wrapped row), so Notepad and other Windows apps get Windows line ends. roster already copied `\r\n` there, but only because xterm.js joins rows with `\r\n` when `navigator.platform` says Windows (`SelectionService.selectionText`). It's now roster's own rule, in one place: `clipboard.ts copiedText` ends every line with `copyLineEnd()`, `\r\n` when `platform/os.ts runsOnWindows()` (the test Ctrl+V's binding uses, Q138) and `\n` elsewhere, as macOS and Linux terminals; every copy route (the keys, copy-on-select, right-click, the WebView's copy event) goes through it. A program's OSC 52 copy is not changed: Windows Terminal puts that text on the clipboard as sent (`Terminal::CopyToClipboard` → `ControlCore`'s `WriteToClipboard`, no conversion), so roster does too. Alternative: leave it to xterm.js (lost: an xterm change, or a WebView whose `platform` differs from its user agent, would change it silently, and nothing tested it). (b) **macOS: Option takes the mouse back.** xterm.js's `macOptionClickForcesSelection` is off by default, so on macOS nothing let you select in the terminal, or paste with a right-click, while a program had mouse reporting on (Q149 (e) read the option rather than assume it). roster now turns it on: Option+drag selects in the terminal and Option+right-click copies or pastes, as Shift does on Windows and Linux, and as Option does in iTerm2 and Terminal.app (the macOS terminals' convention; Shift is the program's there, as in xterm.js). The cost: with it on, xterm.js no longer makes a block (column) selection on Option+drag on macOS (`SelectionService.shouldColumnSelect`), which roster never documented; Option+click at a shell prompt still moves the cursor (`altClickMovesCursor`). No new option: VS Code has this as a setting off by default, but roster's Q149 promise (a key that takes the mouse back on every OS) only holds with it on. Alternative: leave it off and document that macOS has no such key (lost: copying from Claude Code's screen by hand, or pasting with the mouse in vim with `mouse=a`, would be impossible on macOS). Checked by e2e tests on a page that looks like macOS to xterm.js (`navigator.platform` MacIntel); a real Mac is on the owner's hand-check list. |
| Q156 | A Host row's menu instead of its buttons (T166, #314) | **Owner (2026-11-03, on the Windows app): a selected Host's ⓘ, ✓, ✎ and × on its Sidebar row go; a right-click (and the Menu key / Shift+F10) opens a menu with them and the Host's other actions. Amends Q88(1) and Q71(a) for Host rows, and Q65's "visible control" for them: the menu is the visible control.** **What changed:** (a) a **Host's row** (the Inventory's, and a Plugin's Host row such as a Folder's, including a Folder's entry for a Host roster doesn't have) shows **no buttons**, selected or not; groups, Folders, headings and Sessions keep theirs, as Q88 says. (b) **A right-click** on a Host's row selects it and opens its menu at the pointer; **in App mode, the Menu key or Shift+F10** opens the cursor row's menu under it, through a new command **`menu`** (area Sidebar, bound in the `list` context, rebindable like every key), which on another row says only a Host's row has one. (c) **The items are existing command lines, nothing new** (Q36): Open (`open HOST`), New Session (`connect HOST`), or Mark reviewed (`review HOST`) first on an unreviewed Host; Details (`info HOST`), Check (`check HOST`), Edit… (`edit HOST`); the row's own actions (Mark unreviewed while a review is only a Pending change, a Plugin's row actions); Rename… and Move to another group… (`host rename|move HOST`, the command line asks for the rest); Add to a Folder… (`folder add HOST`); then Remove from the Inventory… (`host remove HOST`, which asks first). What this roster can't do (no Live Inventory: API 8; no `:edit`: API 5) and Add to a Folder without the Folders Plugin are **left out, not greyed**, so nothing in the menu only says no. **Agent's calls, for the owner's review:** (d) **the ARIA menu pattern** (WAI-ARIA APG): `role="menu"` named after the Host, `menuitem`s with `tabindex=-1`, separators between the groups (open, look, change, remove); ↑/↓ wrap, Home/End (and PageUp/PageDown) jump, a letter goes to the next item starting with it, Enter or Space runs, Esc and Tab close; opened by a key, focus goes to the first item, by the pointer to the menu itself (↓ then starts), as native menus do; focus always goes back where it was (the terminal, or the tree in App mode, which stays in App mode) before the item's command runs, so a command that moves focus moves it from there. The window's key handler leaves the menu its keys. (e) **Keycaps** beside the items whose command has keys (`␣ i`, `␣ E`, `␣ R`, `␣ M`, `␣ a`, `␣ D`), from the keymap, hidden with the hints (`ui.hints`): taste.md's "keycaps sit next to the things they do". (f) **It stays in the window**: at the pointer, moved left or up to fit; from a key, under the row, flipped above it where there's no room; never past the top or left edge, and it scrolls when taller than the window. A press outside it, a scroll, a resize or the window losing focus closes it; it goes with its row (removed, folded away). (g) **Look:** the Picker's surface (panel fill, strong hairline, the floating shadow), Inter labels, the active item raised like the Picker's selected row, no accent (it isn't live), Highlight outlines in forced colours. A long Plugin label is cut with its whole text in the tooltip and the item's name. (h) **Motion (animate):** opened by the pointer, it fades in from 0.97 at the pointer over 120 ms on the strong ease-out (`--dur-fade`, `--dur-move`): tens of times a day, the click is confirmed; opened by a key it appears at once; it always closes at once, as a native menu does; reduced motion keeps the fade and drops the scale. **Alternatives:** buttons on hover (refused by Q88); a ••• button on the selected row opening the menu (a button again, which the owner asked to drop; the Menu key and right-click reach it without one); greying out unavailable items (they'd only say no; the command still says why when typed). **Not done (follow-ups):** "copy address" (no such command yet); a Plugin API for adding items to every Host's menu (Plugins add to their own rows only, as before); the same menu on Session rows and in the Picker. |
| Q157 | Several named Inventories, one active: the CLI and serve side (T115 part 1, #201) | **Owner (2026-11-01): one person may have several Inventories (the team's for work, their own for home), one active at a time; the app switches only after you confirm; roster warns when the same machine is in two. The calls marked (agent) are for the owner's review; PR #335's security reviews (2026-11-03, four passes) changed (a), (c), (d), (e) and (f) as noted.** This row covers the CLI and serve (the issue's items 1-5); the app's confirm step, the always-visible name, the serve restart and the vault lock (items 6-9) come in part 2. (a) **The list (agent):** `inventories.json` in roster's own folder (`~/.config/roster/`, `%APPDATA%\roster\` on Windows), never in `init.lua` or a `roster.yml`: `{active, inventories: [{name, path}]}`, frozen Pydantic models (Q113), written whole (a temp file of its own, mode 0600, renamed into place). It is checked as it loads, not only by `inv add`: a name follows the Host name rule matched whole (no trailing newline), doesn't end with a dot, and is unique without letter case; a path is absolute with no quote or control character. (b) **`roster inv add|list|use|remove|which` and `roster --inv NAME` (agent details):** the first Inventory named becomes the active one. `add` refuses a taken name (letter case aside: `Work` and `work` are one file on Windows and macOS), a name ending with a dot (Windows drops it), a file that's named already, a missing file, and a file in the folder of another named Inventory (`ROSTER.md` is written next to each). `remove` of the active one leaves none active rather than silently picking another, and removing the last one says to run `roster sync`. `-c PATH` naming a named Inventory's file uses its files. `inv use` says so when `$ROSTER_CONFIG` is set, since it comes first in the lookup order the issue sets (`-c`, `--inv`, `ROSTER_CONFIG`, `./roster.yml`, the active one, then `~/.config/roster/roster.yml`). (c) **This Box's files, one set per Inventory (agent):** a named Inventory's ssh config is `~/.ssh/roster-inventories/NAME` (not in `config.d`, where a user's `Include ~/.ssh/config.d/*` would read it and add up its keys again; the first version's `config.d/roster-NAME` moves there) and its known_hosts `~/.ssh/roster_known_hosts-NAME`; `ROSTER.md` stays next to its `roster.yml`. A `roster.yml` that isn't named keeps the files roster always wrote, and with no named Inventories nothing is written or read differently (a test compares them byte for byte with dev's). `~/.ssh/config` includes one generated index, `~/.ssh/config.d/roster.inventories` (a name no named Inventory's file can have), which holds the Host blocks of the active Inventory first, then the others in the order they were named, then the unnamed file's. **A Host name an earlier Inventory already has is left out of a later one** (said in the index, by warning 146, and by `:check HOST` for that Host), so ssh reads exactly one roster block per name: ssh takes most options from the first matching block, but `IdentityFile` adds up over every one, so a second block would offer the other Inventory's key to the active one's Host. **roster itself never reaches a left-out Host by its name**: `roster run` (and `--sudo`, whose password would otherwise go to the other Inventory's machine) refuses it, `check`, `setup`, `revoke` and the Mesh skip it and say why, and serve's `session.prepare` and `:check HOST` answer code 21 saying so. Its sections are marked `#roster-from PATH`, which no Host's comment (`# TEXT`) can produce. The index is a copy, so a roster run that finds it older than a file it copies (an older roster on the Box synced the unnamed file) rewrites the index file and nothing else: only `sync`, `setup` and `roster inv add/use/remove` write `~/.ssh/config`, and one that can't be written (a read-only `~/.ssh`) is warning 148 (row 206), never the command's end. Every roster on a Box should still be upgraded together. Each Inventory's `ROSTER.md` (the named ones' and the unnamed one roster last synced) marks its left-out Hosts (Claude must not `ssh` to them by name) and is rewritten when that changes (`roster inv use/add/remove`, another Inventory's `sync`), only when its text changes; it's per Box, so it stays out of the shared repo. It is built for its Inventory by name: the process's own Inventory, which serve's other threads read (known_hosts, the index), is set once at start and never switched. Old-layout files are moved or deleted only when roster's header says roster wrote them. The index takes the place of the `Include` line roster wrote before. `Include` lines are matched whole (a user's own `Include ~/.ssh/config.d/roster.inventories` counts, so it isn't doubled), and `~/.ssh/config` is rewritten whole or not at all (a temp file renamed over it), mode 0600 as roster always left it (OpenSSH refuses one others can write); every file roster writes in `~/.ssh` goes the same way. The Box key stays one per Box. (d) **Host keys (agent):** before named Inventories every `roster.yml` on a Box shared `~/.ssh/roster_known_hosts`, so each Inventory named starts with a copy of it, whichever file roster last synced: no key the Box trusted is trusted on first use again. A named Inventory's next `sync` deletes the unnamed ssh config written from that same file. `remove` deletes the Inventory's ssh config and sets its known_hosts aside (`roster_known_hosts-NAME.removed-TIME`, and says so), so a new Inventory of that name, for another file, never inherits its keys. With none left (or `inventories.json` deleted by hand), `~/.ssh/config` includes roster's one file again and the index goes. (e) **The warnings (agent details):** `roster check --box` (its `inventory` section, so `:check` shows them with no new section for the app to know), `setup` and `sync` compare the Inventory in use with every other named one. The same host-key fingerprint (from each Inventory's own known_hosts) warns "`pi` in home and `build-pi` in work have the same host key: the same machine?"; the same Host name warns (unless that pair already got the same-key warning), says which one `ssh NAME` reaches and that the other is left out of the ssh config; the same address and port warns only when both have recorded keys and none match. Another named Inventory that isn't there or doesn't load is a warning that it wasn't compared, never an error, and never repeats its error text (a YAML error quotes a line of that file): it points at `roster --inv NAME check --box`. Hosts with no recorded key aren't compared by key. Fingerprints only: no Login or secret is read. Codes 140-147 in `errors.py` (140 unknown name, 141 taken, 142 bad name or no file, 143 the list doesn't load, 144-147 the warnings), catalog rows 188-195; a check's `topic` is its row. (f) **The app and the CLI agree on the file (item 5, agent):** the app's probe asks roster: `roster inv which` (new: prints the `roster.yml` roster uses; exit 1 when it isn't there), run with the override's arguments from `$HOME` (serve's folder), in WSL and on Linux inside the probe's login shell, and on native Windows from the home folder (one process start there, where the probe ran none before). Either way it gets 15 s (`timeout` in the probe, or perl's `alarm` over its own process group where there's no `timeout`, as on macOS; a killed process on Windows), so a roster that hangs never leaves the app connecting. Only a roster too old to answer, one that hangs, or one that finds no file, leaves it to the old list, which adds `~/infra/roster.yml` (kept so a Box whose `ROSTER_CONFIG` never reaches the probe keeps working); the two can then differ only where the CLI finds nothing at all. (g) **serve (API 27, agent):** `inventories.list` says the named Inventories, the active one, and which one (and file) this serve runs on; `inventories.use` makes another one active and answers `restart: true`. A serve runs on one Inventory for its life; switching is the app restarting it (part 2), which then finds the active one, or starting it with `--inv NAME`. `inventories.use` locks no vault and touches no Session: that's the app's, around the restart. (h) **ssh config values (security, found in the review, on dev before this ticket):** every path roster writes into an ssh config (`IdentityFile`, `UserKnownHostsFile`, `ControlPath`, `Include`) goes through one quoting function that refuses a quote or a control character and writes a `%` as `%%` where ssh expands tokens; `key_path:` in `roster.yml` is refused at load when it holds one, and so is a Host's `ip` or `user` with a quote or a control character, or a `user` with a `%` (an older OpenSSH, as Windows' own, would take `%%` literally); an `ip`'s `%` (an IPv6 zone, `fe80::1%eth0`, the only one allowed) is written `%%` in `HostName`. The Host and group name rule matches whole names (a trailing newline was let through). (i) **Not changed:** `mesh.json` stays one per Box (a follow-up), and nothing shows two Inventories at once (out of scope). |
| Q158 | An ED 1 at the screen's end no longer stops xterm.js, and a throw inside xterm.js or roster's own handlers skips one sequence instead of stopping the terminal (ticket 172, #323; found by T155c, #318; reworked after the review of #327) | **Agent's calls for a fix the coordinator ticketed, for the owner's review (2026-11-03, fixes only).** (a) **The problem.** xterm.js 6.0.0's `eraseInDisplay` for ED 1 (`ESC[1J`, and DECSED `ESC[?1J`) with the cursor at the last column clears the next row's wrap flag at `lines.get(y + 1)`, the screen row without the scrollback's offset. On the bottom row (where ED moves the cursor: into the scroll region in origin mode) of a terminal that keeps scrollback, before anything has scrolled off, that line isn't there and it throws. A throw inside xterm.js's write queue stops the queue for good: no callback, and the rest of that write and every later write never reach the screen (worse than the issue's "the rest of the write is dropped"). It hit the tab's terminal, PromptWatch's (the fill's prompt check) and the shell hooks' prompt screen; CommittedLines' (no scrollback), a tab with `terminal.scrollback = 0` and the alternate screen don't throw, since their buffer is exactly the screen and the lookup wraps around to row 0. With scrollback, the same line clears the wrong row's flag (drawn the same; left upstream). (b) **The fix (agent, after the review).** xterm.js has no public hook for a handler that throws, so a `CsiClamp` given its xterm (`new CsiClamp(term)`, done by all four) wraps the handler methods of that xterm's InputHandler (`_core._inputHandler`, not public API; pinned by the xterm.js version, and a test fails if an upgrade moves them) in a try/catch. When ED 1 throws, it is run again as ED 2 with the same protect flag (DECSED keeps protected cells): it throws only with the cursor, as ED itself moved it, at the end of the bottom row with nothing above the screen, where a correct ED 1 erases every row, which is what ED 2 does. No log for that one. Any other throw skips that one sequence, parsing goes on with the next byte, and the first throw per xterm writes one `console.warn` naming the error's type only (never its message or the output: Session text never reaches a log). roster's own handlers on those xterms (PromptWatch's and CommittedLines' OSC 133 marks, OSC 52) are wrapped the same way, sharing the once-per-xterm log. `screenWriter` also catches a throw out of `write` itself (xterm.js refuses a write once 50 MB is queued): logged once the same way and that chunk dropped, where before it threw into the output listener. The bytes the clamp writes are unchanged. Tests: every cursor position (with and without a pending wrap, lines scrolled off, the alternate screen, origin mode in a scroll region, scrollback kept or none, both EDs) against a plain xterm.js where it doesn't throw and a blank screen with the cursor unchanged where it does; the cursor left below the scroll region in origin mode; PromptWatch still finds sudo's prompt after one; the 417 fuzz repaints #318 skipped now all go through. (c) **The first version, replaced.** The clamp wrote each ED 1 as a tagged ED 1 and ED 2 (second parameter 10000, past any Session's capped CSI) and handlers drew the ED 2 where they predicted a throw. The prediction clamped the cursor to the screen, not to the scroll region as xterm.js does in origin mode, so with the cursor below the region it erased rows xterm.js keeps, and terminals with and without scrollback drew differently (the review of #327). Rerunning on the throw needs no prediction. (d) **Alternatives that lost.** Rewriting ED 1 to ED 2 always: erases rows below the cursor. Moving the cursor off the last column around the ED 1 (`CUB`/`CUF`, `BS`): not reversible at column 0 or with a wrap pending. Saving and restoring the cursor around it (`ESC 7`/`ESC 8`): overwrites a program's own saved cursor. Mirroring `_restrictCursor` in the prediction: more private state (the scroll region) for the same result. (e) **Limits.** ED 2 also clears a marker on the cursor's row (roster sets none there). A handler that throws may have drawn part of its sequence first, and a throw that isn't ED 1's is fail-open: the terminal goes on from there. Upstream: an xterm.js report is drafted in #323's PR for the owner to file; the guard stays until a release fixes it. |
| Q159 | Packages and `:plugins` UI: `:packages update NAME` offers the install prompt, Fix buttons for errors.md rows 116-121, a hint for Plugins written before api 4 (#213 items 2 and 3, #228 item 1b) | **Owner's go-ahead (2026-11-03, batch P1), agent's calls marked (agent) for the owner's review.** (a) **`:packages update NAME` on a Package that isn't installed runs `:packages install NAME`'s own path** (one code path, inside the same serial queue): it forgets a cancel, syncs, and shows the same install prompt (Q81, Q87); nothing installs until it's allowed, and a prompt already up isn't asked twice. (agent) It forgets a cancel as `:packages install` does: typing the Package's name is the same explicit ask. (b) **(agent) Try again for "can't fetch" runs `:packages install NAME`, not `:source`**: `:source` re-syncs only when init.lua's `packages` changed, so after a network failure it did nothing; `:packages install` fetches the same line again and, for a Package not installed, shows the install prompt. One that was installed and fetches again loads as it was and says "Fetched NAME". It also forgets a cancel, for a cancelled Package that later can't be fetched: the prompt still asks. errors.md rows 116 and 118 no longer offer `:source` alone for a retry. (c) **(agent) Which rows get a button:** `:packages`: "can't fetch" Try again (rows 116-118; for 117, missing git, it just says the same until git is installed), "cancelled" or "not installed" Install… (`:packages install NAME`; the ellipsis because a prompt follows), a stopped Package's Plugin Reload (`:plugins reload NAME`) and Check for an update (`:packages update NAME`) (rows 120-121). `:plugins`: Reload on a stopped Plugin, except a Package init.lua no longer names (reloading it would run code init.lua dropped; `:packages clean` is the way, unchanged). Row 119 is an answer on the message line, so it has none. A button runs the command line its tooltip shows, through the same `run` as typing it, so every check and prompt of that command still applies; plain buttons, never the accent, no motion (the panels don't animate, Q81). Its accessible name starts with its visible words and names the row ("Reload (alerts)"); when a button goes away under focus (a reload that worked), focus goes back to the panel. (d) **(agent) The api < 4 hint** shows under a non-built-in Plugin whose manifest `api` is below 4: "Written for api N: its info notices are quiet now unless it marks them quiet = false, and :messages keeps them", with What changed running `:help plugins#upgrading-a-plugin-written-before-api-4`. The note #274 added (decision Q126) moved under that heading in plugins.md so `:help` has a topic for it; the `api` paragraph links to it. It shows whatever `ui.messages` is (the panel doesn't know the option, and the note is true for authors either way). #228's other question (item 2) stays the owner's. |
| Q160 | `m` and a drag move a whole collapsed cluster (#237 item 2) | **Agent's calls within Q99, Q124 and Q143, for the owner's review (2026-11-03; the owner authorised the feature, not these calls).** Q124 (d) amended: `m` on a collapsed cluster no longer says to open it first; it moves the cluster, whole, with `h` `j` `k` `l` (Shift further) until Enter (or `m`) keeps the place or Esc puts it back, and a drag on its node does the same. (a) **Its nodes go along**: each node's saved place moves as far as the cluster's centre, so opened it looks as it did; nodes the user placed in it go too (it moves whole, as Q143 (a) asked of a grown cluster) and stay flagged as theirs. (b) **Remembered as the user's**: each graph in the State's `graphs` gets `movedClusters`, the clusters the user placed, the last one placed at the end (a State saved before has none and reads as before; a list that can't be read is ignored). A list of its own, not `moved`, since a node and a cluster may share an id. Esc puts the flag back as it was. (c) **It stays where the user put it**: clusters the user placed settle first, the last one placed first, so a cluster it was put on is the one that moves, whole, to the nearest free place (Q124 (b)). Considered: the moved cluster moving to the nearest free place instead (the issue says it stays), and refusing an overlapping place (a key or a drag that does nothing is worse). (d) **Held while it moves**: until Enter, Esc, a click or the pointer lets go, the cluster stays exactly under the user and pushes nothing, so passing over clusters doesn't scatter them; the neighbour it's on makes room once it's let go. (e) **No cascade**: a cluster that has to move avoids the clusters still to settle too, where they are, so it no longer lands on the next one and pushes it on in turn (dropping one of 16 clusters on another moved 4 before this, 1 now); this applies to every heal (Q124 (b), Q143 (b)). (f) **No motion**: it's a key or a pointer action (the animate gate), so the cluster and the neighbour that makes room move at once, as Q124's heal does. A drag on an open cluster's empty area still pans: only the collapsed one has a node to grab. |
| Q161 | Plugin network: through the proxy, a tunnel to the address roster checked, never the name (#225 item 5) | **Owner (2026-11-03): in scope.** Agent's calls within Q97, Q116 and Q148, for the owner's review. (a) **The problem:** Q116 (d) looked a public name up here and refused it if any address was inside, then handed the proxy the name (`CONNECT api.example.com:443`, or `GET http://api.example.com/…` for plain http), and the proxy looked it up again: a proxy whose DNS answered differently (split-horizon, a poisoned cache, a name re-pointed between the two lookups) could lead the request to an address roster never checked, inside the network too. And a name this computer couldn't look up at all went to the proxy unchecked. (b) **The fix:** the core asks the proxy for a tunnel to the address it checked (`CONNECT 45.10.20.30:443`, `[…]:443` for IPv6), never the name, and speaks through the tunnel itself: TLS with the name as SNI and the certificate checked against the name (the OS's trust store as the shared client, or the site's pin, Q116 c), then HTTP/1.1 with the name only in `Host`. Every hop does this, the first and each redirect, after Q148's checks. The reqwest clients no longer get the proxy at all, so nothing hands the proxy a name. (c) **Fails closed (agent):** a name this computer can't look up, or that has no address, isn't sent (errors.md row 200); this replaces Q116 (d)'s residual case, since with no address there's nothing roster checked to tunnel to. A network where only the proxy resolves names no longer works for Plugins' requests; the docs say to point this Box's DNS at a resolver that answers. A proxy that answers the CONNECT with anything but 2xx, with something that isn't HTTP, or with a head over 8 KB, fails the request (row 201), and roster never falls back to handing it the name. A proxy that can't be reached is row 202. (d) **Plain http goes through a tunnel too (agent):** the absolute form (`GET http://NAME/…`) makes the proxy resolve the name, so an `http://` site is `CONNECT IP:80` too. A proxy that allows CONNECT only to 443 refuses it, and the request fails closed. (e) **Several addresses (agent):** they're tried in the order the resolver gave them, each a new tunnel, until the proxy opens one; if none, the last reason is the request's. All of it within the request's one timeout (Q148 c), and 10 s for each connection to the proxy and each CONNECT answer. (f) **The client for the tunnel (agent):** reqwest can't be told what to CONNECT to (its connector layer can't produce a connection of its own) and rewriting the URL to the address would lose SNI and check the certificate against the address, so the core opens the tunnel with tokio and speaks HTTP/1.1 over it with hyper and tokio-rustls; all were in the lock already (reqwest's), so no new crate is built. A tunnelled hop opens a new tunnel (no pool), its connection task is stopped when its answer is dropped or the request is stopped by an unload, and the caps (1 MB back, read while reading) and timeouts are the same as direct. No HTTP/2 or ALPN, as the client. (g) **Tests stand in for this computer's resolver (agent)** through `PluginHost::with_lookup`, which exists only with the crate's `test-hooks` feature, turned on only by its own dev-dependency on itself; the app is never built with it. (h) **What's left:** the proxy is trusted to honour the CONNECT target, as any tunnel is; it's the user's own (Q116 d). For https the certificate must still match the name, so a proxy that tunnelled elsewhere would fail TLS; for plain http nothing can tell. (h2) **After the security review (agent):** a Plugin's error never names the proxy ("your plugin_proxy wouldn't open a tunnel…", "couldn't connect to your plugin_proxy: …"), since Q116 (d) says a Plugin can't see it; an answer through the tunnel is judged by its body's real size (0 for HEAD or 304, whatever `Content-Length` says), as the direct client does; `Accept: */*` goes unless the Plugin sets its own, as without a proxy; an IPv4 address the resolver gives as IPv4-mapped IPv6 is asked for as IPv4 (`CONNECT 45.10.20.31:80`), the form every proxy reads. A public address listed as a site still goes direct, never through the proxy (Q116 d), and config.md says so. (i) No serve change, `roster.api_version` unchanged, no UI and no motion. Tests: `through_the_proxy_a_request_reaches_only_the_address_roster_checked`, `through_the_proxy_https_goes_to_the_address_checked_with_the_name_as_sni`, `a_proxy_that_wont_tunnel_to_the_address_fails_the_request_closed`, `a_name_roster_cant_check_never_reaches_the_proxy`, `through_the_tunnel_the_body_the_cap_and_the_timeout_hold`, `a_proxy_that_answers_the_connect_wrongly_fails_the_request`, `a_proxy_that_never_answers_the_connect_is_given_up_on`, `bytes_a_proxy_sends_after_its_200_dont_pass_for_the_site`, `a_proxy_that_cant_be_reached_isnt_named_to_the_plugin`, `a_bad_certificate_through_the_tunnel_fails`, `a_head_through_the_tunnel_isnt_judged_by_its_content_length`, `every_address_is_tried_and_written_as_a_proxy_reads_it`, `a_redirect_through_the_proxy_takes_a_tunnel_of_its_own_or_goes_direct`, `through_the_tunnel_accept_is_any_unless_the_plugin_says` (tests/network.rs, against a fake proxy whose own DNS leads every name to a server roster must never reach); `every_hop_through_the_proxy_is_looked_up_and_checked_before_its_followed`, `a_public_name_through_the_proxy_is_checked_here_or_not_sent` (network.rs). |
| Q162 | A quiet message from the background keeps a line that shows (#228 item 2) | **Owner (2026-11-03): errors stay visible. Q115 (a) "errors always shown" wins over Q126 (b), which let a quiet message replace whatever the message line held.** **What changed:** a Plugin's quiet info notice that nothing the user did asked for (one from a timer, an event, a request's answer, or a load) no longer takes the place of a message line that shows something: the error (or answer, or result) stays, and `:messages` keeps the notice as before. On an empty line it goes as Q126 (b) says: a quiet message, heard but not seen. A notice from the user's own call (an action, a command, a finder item, or one a Plugin's `roster.cmd` carried from it) replaces the line as before, since the user just did something new; so does any message roster says itself. With `ui.messages = "all"` nothing changes: the notice is drawn as a notice, beside the line. Agent's calls: (a) **Every shown line, not only errors:** the message line holds plain text for errors, warnings, answers and results alike (Q115 (a) puts them all in "always shown") and can't tell an error from an answer, so a background quiet notice keeps any of them; adding a level to every message to keep only errors would change every caller for no case the owner raised. (b) **Only Plugin notices:** `roster.echo` runs only in a call the user started (the core refuses it elsewhere), so it can't come from the background; roster's own quiet messages that come unasked ("Plugins: couldn't tell roster its own commands; trying again", init.lua reloaded on save) are left as they are, the ruling being about #228's Plugin notices. (c) **A kept line isn't spoken again:** while it stays, a screen reader doesn't hear the background notice's short form, since the live region keeps saying the line that shows; `:messages` has it. (d) Where: `lineAfter` in `app/src/ui/messages.ts` decides what the line holds; the Plugin manager marks a reply as background (`backgroundQuiet`). No API, op or serve change, no motion. *Widened (#349, owner's go-ahead 2026-11-03), replacing (b): every quiet message roster says without the user asking keeps a line that shows too, and `:messages` keeps it; it's made with `backgroundQuiet`, not `quiet`. (agent) Which ones: only one exists today, "Plugins: couldn't tell roster its own commands; trying again" (said when a built-in registers late or the core host is built anew, never as the answer to a command). An automatic init.lua reload (`auto_reload`) says nothing on the message line (its error is the Config problem toast), so it has no notice to change; its other unasked messages (init.lua's vault settings left out, a Host's services left out, roster.yml changed outside the app but not synced) are warnings, plain text that always shows (Q115 (a)), so they still replace the line, as an error does. Every quiet message from a command, a key, a finder item or a button stays `quiet` and replaces the line, as before.* |
| Q163 | A map key that holds a dot (#261's feature) | **Owner (2026-11-03): close it, no new syntax.** A dotted name (`roster.o.get`, `roster.o.reset`, `roster.set`) can't address a map key that itself holds a dot: `keys.leader..` is an unknown name, and #261's `views.a.b` reads as views, then a, then b. No quoted or escaped form is added: it would be new API surface for a rare key, and the bracket form already works on every map: `roster.o.keys.leader["."]` reads it, `roster.o.keys.leader["."] = "connect esx-02"` sets it, and `= nil` takes it away. **What changed:** the generated `:help` for `roster.o.get`, `roster.o.reset` and `roster.set` (from `crates/roster-core/src/config/shape/table.rs`) says so, each with a bracket example; no behaviour change. Agent's call: the examples use a key map's `.` rather than #261's `roster.o.views["a.b"]`, since a view id may hold only letters, digits, `-` and `_` (init.lua refuses `views["a.b"]` when it loads), so that example wouldn't load; key bindings are the maps where a dot in a key is real. |
| Q165 | init.lua gets its own version, `roster.config_version` (T147, #259; from T142, Q132) | **Owner (2026-11-03): yes, init.lua's version is separate from the Plugin API's `roster.api_version`.** `roster.api_version` is the Plugin API's version, and init.lua's table showed it as its own: it rose for Plugin-only changes (4 to 5 changed nothing in init.lua's names that a Config would check) and didn't rise when init.lua's names changed in v0.3.0 (`roster.o`, the collections' functions). **What it is:** `roster.config_version`, a read-only integer on init.lua's `roster` table only (a Plugin's table doesn't get it); writing it is an error at the caller's line ("roster.config_version is read-only"), like `roster.version`. It has its `:help` entry, the editor types' line and its row in the shape table; `roster.api_version` stays, now documented as the Plugin API's. **(agent) It starts at 4,** the value `roster.api_version` had when v0.3.0 shipped `roster.o`'s shape, so a Config's old `roster.api_version >= 4` check reads the same with `roster.config_version`; an app before it (v0.4.0 and older) reads it as nil. Alternatives: 1 (a fresh count) loses that one-for-one reading; 5 (today's `api_version`) would mark a Plugin-only change. **(agent) What raises it:** a change to init.lua's `roster` table or an option that a name can't tell: a name removed or renamed, or a name that stays but takes, gives or does something else (a new type for an option, a function's new return). A new name doesn't raise it (it reads as nil in an older app, which is how a Config checks for it: `if roster.themes.update then`), so v0.4.0's additions (`roster.o.get`, `roster.keymap.has`, `roster.themes.has`) leave it at 4; nor does any Plugin API change. Raising it is `CONFIG_VERSION` in `crates/roster-core/src/config/mod.rs` with a `docs/news.md` bullet that names the new number. |
| Q166 | Right-click as Windows Terminal: with copy-on-select it always pastes, and under mouse reporting it's the program's even with a selection (#302 items 1 and 2; follows Q149 (e)) | **Owner's ruling (2026-11-03, on #302's two questions: "match Windows Terminal").** (a) **Copy-on-select: right-click always pastes.** Windows Terminal's right-click (`ControlInteractivity.cpp` `PointerPressed`, without `RightClickContextMenu`) copies the selection and clears it, then pastes when `copyOnSelect` is on or there was nothing to copy (its comment: "CopyOnSelect: right click always pastes"). roster's right-click with a selection only copied and cleared. Now, with `terminal.copy_on_select` on (the default), `clipboard.ts rightClick` copies the selection (trimmed, with Q155's line ends), clears it, waits for the clipboard write and, once it has landed, pastes the clipboard, so the selection lands at the prompt; with it off, a right-click still copies a selection or pastes when there's none. The paste is an ordinary one: it goes through `pasteText` (`pasteFilter`, Q149 (g)) and xterm's bracketed paste, so text the selection held can't end a bracketed paste early, and an OSC 52 rule is untouched (a right-click never writes what a Host sent, only what you selected on screen). (b) **Under mouse reporting, a right-click is the program's, selection or not.** Windows Terminal checks `_canSendVTMouseInput` (a mouse mode on, Shift not held) before its copy-or-paste rule, so with a terminal selection (made by Shift+drag) a plain right-click goes to the program. roster copied that selection instead (Q149 (e)'s one difference). Now `rightClick` with `programHasMouse` returns before the selection is looked at: nothing is copied or pasted, and xterm has already sent the click; Shift+right-click (Option on macOS, Q155) copies or pastes as a right-click without the program, with (a)'s rule. Only a pointer's right-click is the program's: a context menu from the Menu key or Shift+F10 copies or pastes as before. (c) **Agent's calls:** **It fails closed** (coordinator's review of #338): the paste after the copy happens only when the copy landed. If the clipboard refuses the write, the selection is still cleared and nothing is pasted, as with copy-on-select off, because the clipboard still holds what it held before (a password copied earlier, say), and pasting that into a remote shell is not what the right-click meant. Windows Terminal pastes whatever the clipboard holds then; roster parts from it here on purpose. *(agent)* The same for a selection of only spaces (nothing left to copy once trimmed): it's cleared and nothing is pasted, with copy-on-select on or off, for the same reason (the clipboard would still hold the old text). *(agent)* The right-click doesn't clear the selection itself under mouse reporting; xterm.js clears it anyway, because it clears a selection on any input it sends the program (`SelectionService`, `onUserInput`), the click's report included, so the highlight goes (an e2e test pins it); the clipboard keeps what it held. *(agent)* No new option: both follow existing ones (`copy_on_select`, `right_click_paste`), as in Windows Terminal, where they follow `copyOnSelect`. (d) **The risk:** a right-click on a selection with copy-on-select on now types that text at the prompt. It's text from the screen you selected, filtered and bracketed like any paste, and Windows Terminal users expect it; `copy_on_select = false` (or `right_click_paste = false`) brings the copy-only right-click back. (e) **Alternatives:** keep roster's copy-only right-click (lost: the owner chose Windows Terminal's behaviour, which their hands already know); paste the selected text directly instead of the clipboard after the copy (lost: one paste path through the clipboard keeps the read the same as every other paste, and the fail-closed rule already covers a refused write); paste the old clipboard when the write fails, as Windows Terminal (lost: it can type stale text, such as a password, into a remote shell). Tested by `clipboard.test.ts` and the e2e suite (`copy-paste.e2e.ts`, #302 cases); the owner's hand checks are in `docs/app/dev/copy-paste-checklist.md`. |
| Q167 | `:version` and the installers' licence files (T180, #343) | **Agent's calls within the issue, for the owner's review (2026-11-03).** (a) **`:version` is a help page, not a message.** The issue asks `:version` ("or the about/help it opens") to name roster's licence and say where the notices are; the app had no `:version`. It's now a command, like Neovim's, that opens the `version` help page (also `:help license`, `licence`, `notices`, `third-party-notices`, `about`): this build's version as its title, the licence (GPL-3.0-or-later), a table of where each download puts `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` on disk, and the notices' text, bundled from `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` when the app is built. A message line couldn't hold the notices, and a page is on the docs website too; it uses the help panel's existing styles, so no CSS (#340's budget). (b) **The file comes in as `virtual:third-party-notices`** (`app/scripts/notices-plugin.mjs`, in `vite.config.ts` and `vite-run.mjs`): Vite's `server.fs.allow` takes folders, and allowing the repo's root to reach one file would let the dev server read all of it. (c) **`bundle.resources` is a map** (`../../LICENSE` → `LICENSE`), so both files sit at the top of the app's resources (a list keeps the `../../` as `_up_/_up_/`); **`bundle.licenseFile` is `LICENSE`**, so the NSIS and MSI installers show the GPL before installing, and the `.dmg` shows it as a licence agreement when opened (Tauri passes it to the disk image as its licence; CI agrees to it to mount the image), as the issue asks (with the usual agree button; the GPL governs copying, not use, so the page informs more than it binds). The JS budget has 9 KB of gzip left, which the GPL's own text would mostly use: it stays on disk only. (d) **CI unpacks every installer and looks** (`check-release-notices.py FOLDER`: some folder holds both): the `-setup.exe` with 7-Zip, the `.msi` with `msiexec /a`, the `.deb` with `dpkg-deb -x`, the `.AppImage` with `--appimage-extract`, the `.dmg` mounted read-only, and `Roster.app`; the log names the folder each holds the files in (with `/` on every OS); an installer's own third-party files may hold a `LICENSE` of theirs, so the archives' "every LICENSE has the notices beside it" rule isn't used there. The Version job checks the bundle config first (`--config`), and a pull request touching `tauri.conf.json` now runs the release dry run. |
| Q168 | Switching named Inventories in the app (T115 part 2, #201 items 6-9) | **Owner (2026-11-01, Q157): the app switches only after you confirm, shows the active Inventory at all times, restarts serve and locks the old vault. Agent's calls marked (agent), for the owner's review (2026-11-03).** (a) **The command and key (agent):** `:inventory NAME`, completing the names `inventories.list` gave; alone (or `␣ I`, a free Leader key next to the Live Inventory's capitals) it starts the command line with `inventory ` typed, or says how to name one when there are none. Needs serve API 27. (b) **The confirm step:** the side-panel `ConfirmPanel` the Live Inventory's remove uses (no CSS added: the bundle had about 71 bytes of headroom), with the issue's sentence ("Switch from work to home? roster restarts on home's file and locks work's vault."), then a line each for the open Sessions (connecting or open; ended ones aren't counted) and the Pending changes. (agent) The button is the primary one ("Switch to home"), not the danger colour: a switch breaks nothing. Cancel is "Stay"; Esc stays. (c) **What blocks it (agent):** Pending changes (asked of serve again when the step opens and again on confirm, so one made while it asked still blocks): the step names them and how to publish or discard, with no Switch button. An earlier step of roster's lookup: when serve runs on another file than the active Inventory's (`inventories.list`'s `name` isn't its `active`), `$ROSTER_CONFIG`, a `./roster.yml` or a `-c` in `roster_command` won, and would win again after a restart; the step says so with no Switch button rather than pretending. When nothing is active yet the app can't tell beforehand, so after the restart it compares `name` with the target and says plainly when roster still runs on another file. A target whose file isn't there, an unknown name, or the one roster runs on already, are a message, not a step.
