(d) **The switch:** lock (the cover first, then waiting for `vault.lock`; one that fails stops the switch, "Couldn't lock the vault (…): nothing switched", since the old serve's secrets would otherwise outlive the switch until its process ends), `inventories.use`, "Restarting roster on home…" on the message line, then serve started again as for a new `backend` (`backend_connect` with `restart`): the core runs detection again, whose probe asks `roster inv which`, never the old serve's `-c PATH` (the security review of #335). Tested with the fake Backend, whose restart runs its own lookup: the new serve reports home's name and path, and the status bar shows them. While it runs (the review of #369) the step's ✕, Esc and Stay do nothing (`aria-busy`), and a second `:inventory` says "A switch to home is already running". Once `inventories.use` has answered, roster's active Inventory is the new one whatever happens next, so the app says so: a serve that doesn't start again gets "home is the active Inventory now, but roster didn't start again on it" on the error screen, and a new serve that doesn't answer `inventories.list` gets "… but roster couldn't say which Inventory it started on", never silence. The step's text is the alertdialog's description (`aria-describedby`), so a screen reader reads what will happen, not only the title and the button. (e) **Always in sight (agent):** a status bar segment `inventory` ("home Inventory", its file in the tooltip) after the Box, shown only when serve runs on a named Inventory, so nothing changes for anyone without one; it's a segment like the others, so `modes.*.statusbar` can move or drop it, and Focus hides it with the status bar (the user's choice there). A change is said on the message line, a status region, so screen readers hear it ("Now on the home Inventory …"). (f) **Sessions from the other Inventory (agent; made fail-closed after the review of #369):** each tab knows the Inventory, name **and** `roster.yml` path, that roster ran on when it opened (two unnamed files are two Inventories). Every serve start (a switch, a `backend` change, a Retry) makes every tab count as another Inventory's until the new serve's `inventories.list` (or, before API 27, `inventory.info`'s path) says it runs on theirs; if that call fails they stay another's for that serve's life, and a tab opened on a serve that never said is lost to the next one. While a tab counts as another's, its label ends " · work" (the tab strip, the Sidebar's Sessions and the session bar draw that tail as they draw " (2)"), its fill offer is hidden, every fill refuses (the fill key, a nested ssh's fill and so the core's Q129 fill, which only `Tabs.fill` starts), nothing starts its Session again (reconnect, the reconnect after an unlock, a host-key answer), and the shell hook line isn't sent to it, since serve would answer for a Host of the same name in the other Inventory (a password typed into the wrong machine). `:connect HOST here` in such a tab is an explicit new Session on this Inventory. Back on theirs, the mark goes. (g) **Plugins:** they can't reach `inventories.*`: neither is in `SERVE_METHODS` or under the `mesh.`/`discover.` prefixes; the shared permission table now refuses both, so a change there fails the core's and the fake's tests. (h) **No motion:** the step opens from a key or a command, as the other side panels do (taste: motion confirms; the animate gate says keyboard-initiated panels don't animate), and the restart shows the existing connecting state. |
| Q170 | A pointer press selects without moving the graph's view (T179, #341) | **Agent's call within Q99 (f) and Q160, a fix, for the owner's review (2026-11-03).** A press on a node or a collapsed cluster selected it through the same path as a key, which brings the selection on screen (`reveal`: within 56 px of an edge, or 168 px of the right one, where its label goes, the camera centres on it), so a drag that started there moved the whole map under the pointer before it began. The issue offered two fixes: reveal only for a selection by key, or also once a press ends without a drag. **(agent) The second:** a press selects in place, the view never moves during a press or a drag, and a click (pointer up with no drag) brings what it selected in as a key does, so a click near the edge still shows its label, as before. Considered: never revealing on a pointer selection (what was clicked is on screen already, but its label at the right edge can be cut off, and a click's reveal was what the app did). No motion (the animate gate: a pointer action), no API or State change. Tested end to end in headless Chromium (`app/e2e/graph-drag.e2e.ts`), which also pins #237's cluster drag (Q160). |
| Q171 | Stop in the `:check` panel (#221 item 1; follows Q122 and Q142) | **Owner authorised the feature (2026-11-03); agent's calls within it, for the owner's review.** (a) **The core hands the app the check's job as it starts:** `host_check` takes a channel that hears `{job, own}` once serve has the check under way (API 21; an older serve sends none, so there's no Stop), and `host_check_stop {job}` stops it through serve's existing `jobs.stop` (API 20). No serve or Plugin API change, so no `API_VERSION` bump. (b) **(agent) Stop is offered only for the app's own check**, as the issue asks: one the core started, or one a later `:check` of the same Host joined while the core's first call still waits for it (the core counts the calls waiting for each job it started). A check another caller started, a Plugin's `host.check` say, gets no Stop, and the core refuses to stop it without asking serve: stopping it would cut that caller's answer short, the same reason the core's 90 s limit stops only a check it started. (c) **What it shows:** pressing Stop takes the button away (one way to stop, no second press), and the summary line says "Stopping the check of HOST…" until serve answers; serve stops the check at its next step (a login under way is ended at once, any other step finishes first), and the answer is Q122 (f)'s: "Stopped at STEP; the rest were skipped.", the steps that ran ticked with their messages, the square on where it stopped. (d) **Nothing half-written:** a check writes nothing (it reads roster.yml, the ssh config and the recorded host keys, and its one login runs `true`), and a stopped one closes its connection and ends its login, so a cancelled check leaves nothing behind on this Box or the Host; the Host's rate limiter sees no more attempts than a check that ran on. (e) **(agent) The button:** a borderless 24 px icon button with a small filled square (the stop mark a stopped step gets, so cause and effect match), beside Full window in the panel's header, named "Stop the check" with that tooltip; the existing `.icon-button` style, no new CSS (the CSS budget waits for #340), and in the header it moves no row as it comes and goes (Q122 (b)). No motion: it's there or not. (f) **Keyboard and focus:** Tab reaches it; it takes no focus on a mouse press, as the panel's other header buttons; when it goes (pressed, or the check answered) while it has focus, focus moves to the panel's close button rather than drop to the page. No key of its own here (closing the panel stays Esc); #361 adds one, see (h). (g) **(agent) A stop that doesn't reach serve** puts "The check couldn't be stopped: …" on the message line (errors.md row 203; errors stay visible, Q162) and offers Stop again; the check still ends on its own or when the app stops waiting at 90 s. (h) **Stop's key (#361, owner authorised 2026-11-03; agent's calls):** **(agent) `x` anywhere in the panel**, as a view's heading button has its own key (#176): the tooltip says "Stop the check (x)" and the button has `aria-keyshortcuts`; a field keeps its letters, a held key stops nothing more, and in App mode the keymap has the keys first, as for a view's. `x` because it's free in the leader table (`s`, the obvious letter, is `␣ s` statusbar there) and it's already the views' key for cutting something off (the Mesh controls' Cut). The panel key isn't in the keymap (no new keymap context for one key); **(agent) `:check stop`** is the rebindable way: `roster.keymap.set("leader", KEY, "check stop")`. It does what the button does (same `jobs.stop`, same "Stopping…" line, row 203 on failure, focus on Stop moves to Close), only for the app's own check, and says why otherwise: no check running, "isn't the app's to stop: another caller started it" (the core now marks a check it says isn't its own), already stopping, a roster before API 21, or not started yet. **(agent) A Host named `stop` wins:** `:check stop` checks it, so every `check HOST` line the app builds (the row menu, an error's Run :check, the Topology's `c`) stays right; that user stops with `x` or the button. Completion offers `stop` after the Hosts. No CSS. |
| Q172 | Mesh state per Inventory and `roster revoke --all` (T182 #346, T183 #347; follow Q157 (i)) | **The owner authorised both tickets (2026-11-03, issue bodies as the spec); the calls below are the agent's, for the owner's review.** (a) **Where (agent):** a named Inventory's Mesh state is `~/.config/roster/mesh-NAME.json`, beside `mesh.json` in roster's own folder (as known_hosts is `roster_known_hosts-NAME` beside `roster_known_hosts`); a `roster.yml` that isn't named keeps `mesh.json`, written exactly as before. One function (`common.mesh_state_file`) names it, defaulting to this run's Inventory, which is set once at start: serve's Mesh jobs read the same one, and nothing switches it. (b) **The one-time move (agent; tightened by #367's review, F2):** `mesh.json` records nothing about the file it came from, and adding that would change its bytes. `inv add NAME PATH` moves it to `mesh-NAME.json` only when all of these hold: roster's unnamed ssh config (`config.d/roster`) was last written from PATH (the Mesh reaches its Hosts over it), every Host the state names (members, pending, cuts and their peers) is a Host of PATH, and its `applied` digest, when set, is PATH's Mesh as it is now (`mesh.digest`). The header alone only says what synced last, so it could hand another file's Mesh to this one, which would then leave that file's `roster-mesh` lines in place and visit a same-named Host of its own to take away what it never wrote. Otherwise it stays the unnamed Inventory's and `inv add` says so and why (the file can be moved by hand). A `mesh-NAME.json` already there is never overwritten, and another roster moving it first is fine. Rejected: copying it to every Inventory named, and moving it on the header or the Hosts alone. (c) **`inv remove` (agent, not in the issue):** sets `mesh-NAME.json` aside (`.removed-TIME`) and says so, as Q157 (d) does for known_hosts, so a new Inventory of that name for another file never inherits its Mesh. The state is bookkeeping only (docs/mesh.md): losing it means the next `roster mesh` doesn't revisit a Host that left `roster.yml` meanwhile. (d) **What `--all` covers (agent):** every named Inventory in the order they were named, then the unnamed one (labelled `(unnamed)`, which no Inventory's name can be: #367's review, F4), which is the `roster.yml` roster's unnamed ssh config was last written from (as `refresh_roster_md` decides it; the only unnamed file whose Hosts `ssh NAME` reaches), unless that is a named one's file. It takes no `--only`, `--inv` or `-c` (a usage error). (e) **Reaching Hosts (agent; (g) gives plain `revoke` the same rule):** each Inventory is loaded from its path and its Hosts reached by name, but only a name in that Inventory's section of this Box's ssh config (`inventories.reachable_hosts(NAME)`): a left-out Host is skipped with the usual words, never `ssh`ed by its name (the other Inventory's Host by that name is revoked with its own Inventory); an Inventory whose ssh config isn't on this Box or isn't included, and a Host not in its file yet, are skipped and said, since `ssh NAME` would reach something else or nothing. These and an Inventory that doesn't load (said without its error text, which could quote a line, as Q157 (e)) make the exit 1. (f) **Confirmation and summary (agent):** one question, as today's, naming the key tag(s), the total and each Inventory's count (`Remove key 'roster-managed:BOX' from 3 hosts in 2 Inventories (work 2, home 1)? This box loses access. [y/N]`), skipped by `-y`; one line per Host (`ok`/`FAIL  work: esx-02`) and one summary, `removed from N/M hosts in K Inventories`, listing every key file and every Inventory's ssh config (and the index) to delete, but only when every Host was revoked: with anything skipped or failed it says to revoke those first, as deleting the key would leave its line on them for good (#367's review, F3). (g) **The same guard for every command that `ssh`es by name (coordinator's review of #367, same class as #335's N1):** with named Inventories (`~/.ssh/config` includes the index), `run` (and `--sudo`), `check`, `setup`, plain `revoke` and the Mesh reach a Host only when its name is in this run's Inventory's part of the index (`sshops.not_reached`); a left-out one keeps its words, and one that isn't there (the Inventory not synced on this Box under `--inv`/`-c`, or a Host added since) is skipped or refused with "not in this Box's ssh config for this Inventory" and the `sync` that fixes it. `setup` writes the ssh config before it connects, so it is unaffected. serve's `session.prepare` and `:check HOST` already asked `in_ssh_config`. Without the index (no named Inventories) nothing changes, as Q157 (c) requires. (h) **Code 149 (catalog row 207):** an ssh roster won't hand revoke's script (a batch-file shim on Windows) is an error before `revoke` or `--all` asks or connects, so nothing is revoked half way. (i) **Two unnamed files (#367's review, F1; already so on dev):** every `roster.yml` that isn't named shares `config.d/roster`, whichever one last wrote it (its header). A run on an unnamed file (`-c`, `$ROSTER_CONFIG`, `./roster.yml`) now reaches a Host by name only when that header is this file (`inventories.unnamed_from_elsewhere`, from the path `find_config` records with the Inventory at start); otherwise `run`/`--sudo` refuse, `check`, `setup`'s and `revoke`'s logins skip, the Mesh leaves the Host pending, and serve's `session.prepare` and `:check HOST` answer 21, each saying "not in this Box's ssh config for this roster.yml" and `roster -c PATH sync`. This holds with or without named Inventories: before, `roster -c B.yml run --sudo pi` sent B's sudo password to A's `pi`. The unnamed file's own runs are unchanged (the byte-for-byte test still passes). |
| Q173 | The Mesh with roster installed or not, and revoke/check with Hosts skipped (T193 #365, T194 #371) | **The owner authorised both tickets (2026-11-03, issue bodies as the spec); the calls below are the agent's, for the owner's review.** (a) **Looking beyond the plan is opt-in (coordinator's call after #372's review, B1):** the first version swept every key, reviewed Host automatically when this Box had no Mesh record and removed whatever `roster-mesh:` entries roster.yml didn't ask for. The tag names only the source Host, not the Box or Inventory, so a new Box (a teammate's, a laptop listing the same NAS) tore down another Box's live Mesh; the reviewer reproduced it with three stand-in Boxes. Now `roster mesh`, `--dry-run`, `roster check` and `roster setup` behave exactly as on dev (the plan's Hosts and recorded members only; no extra connections, so no repeated scans either, F3). A reinstalled Box with the same roster.yml takes its earlier keys and lines as they are (the plan's own comparison). `roster mesh --find-leftovers` (agent's shape within that call) visits every key, reviewed Host once, one at a time, read-only, and lists per Host the `roster-mesh:` lines whose source this roster.yml doesn't give a key Link to that Host (with the source and its `from=`), and, on a Host that isn't a source here, the Mesh files roster wrote (`mine`), the mark's Include and a Mesh `ROSTER.md`; it says plainly they may be another Box's or Inventory's Mesh, still in use (catalog row 208). `--remove-leftovers` does the same, asks (the question says another Box's Mesh would stop working; `-y` skips it) and removes exactly what was found: those tags only, and a former source's files as when a Host stops being a source. (b) **The same-Box guard (agent, kept):** with another Inventory's Mesh state on this Box (`mesh*.json` other than this run's), `--remove-leftovers` refuses (row 211), since one of that Inventory's lines would look the same; `--find-leftovers` still reports. (c) **No tag change (agent):** putting the Box in new tags (`roster-mesh:SRC@BOX`) was optional; it touches every script's tag pattern and cut's, and old lines would stay ambiguous, so it's left as a follow-up. `roster mesh status` says `no Mesh record on this Box` (CLI text only; `mesh.status` unchanged) and names `--find-leftovers`. The plan's result shape and serve's API are unchanged. (d) **`roster mesh cut --all` (agent; the issue says "maybe"):** every `roster-mesh:` line and every source's Mesh files off every key, reviewed Host in roster.yml, in parallel, as a cut does on the cut Host itself (`cut_script(own=True)`), for uninstalling; like (a), it also takes off any other Box's or Inventory's Mesh entries on those Hosts, which its question and docs/mesh.md now say (#372's review, F2); it does not write `mesh: false` or touch roster.yml (the next `roster mesh` puts the Mesh back), exits 1 with a Host not confirmed (row 211), keeps that Host in the record and drops confirmed ones from pending and cuts pending. CLI only: not a serve method or Plugin action (a follow-up if wanted). docs/mesh.md has an "Uninstalling roster" section: `roster mesh cut --all`, then `roster revoke` (`--all`), then delete the files it names. (e) **A meshed Host that runs roster (agent):** its `~/.ssh/config` already kept both writers' lines apart (each looks for its own exact `Include` and prepends only when missing; the Mesh's awk only touches its mark and the line after it), now pinned by tests in both orders, named and unnamed; ssh takes a setting from the first `Include` that sets it. The one shared file is `ROSTER.md` when the Host's roster.yml is in its home folder: the Host's roster no longer overwrites a `ROSTER.md` whose first line is the Mesh's (`claude.written_by_the_mesh`, also in `refresh_roster_md`) and warns, and the Mesh now says a `ROSTER.md` with roster's own header is that Host's roster's (row 210) rather than "isn't roster's"; neither is ever overwritten or deleted. The Mesh's `ours` check on a Host's `ROSTER.md` now matches its exact header line (`<!-- roster-mesh: written by roster for `), not any first line containing `roster-mesh`, which removed a user's notes (#372's review, F1; on dev too). (f) **Leftovers of a removed roster on a Host (agent):** an `Include` of a deleted `config.d/roster`, an old `ROSTER.md` and stale known hosts are left alone (ssh skips a missing `Include`, the Mesh's ssh config uses its own `known_hosts`, its test is `nc -z`). "A dangling Include roster relies on" is read as the Mesh's own `Include` naming a missing Mesh config (row 209: said in a plan, written again by an apply); a dangling `Include` of the Host's old roster's file is not warned about, since it's harmless and roster never removes it, so a warning would repeat forever. (g) **T194: what counts as finished (agent):** `roster revoke` and `roster check` exit 1 when any selected Host was skipped (`not_reached`) or none was revoked/checked (including an empty selection); `revoke --all` exits 1 with a left-out Host skipped too (it was 0), so every revoke agrees. Plain `revoke` names its files to delete only when every key Host of the file was selected (no `--only`), none skipped and none failed, and names `config.d/roster` only when `unnamed_from_elsewhere()` is None (it was written from this file). |
| Q174 | Windows Hosts, part 1: `os:`, and setup, revoke and check on Windows (T195, #375; part 2 is T196, #376) | **Owner's decisions (2026-11-03):** (1) **a new `os:` key** in roster.yml marks a Host's OS, cascading like other settings (defaults → group → Host); `type:` stays a free label. `roster discover` writes `os: windows` for the Hosts it takes for Windows (and `type: windows` as before). A Host with `type: windows` and no `os:` gets a warning suggesting `os: windows`; roster never infers it. (2) **`roster run` on a Windows Host always runs PowerShell** (that's T196). **The agent's calls, for the owner's review:** (a) **(agent) values:** `windows` or `posix`, any case, stored lowercase; unset is `null` in the Host (as `mesh:`), which means posix; anything else (`linux`, `win`, `true`, `''`) stops roster.yml from loading, naming the Host. (b) **(agent) the warning is code 154 on stderr** from `roster hosts`, `check`, `setup` and `revoke` (where `warn_problems` already warns), not in `box.check` or serve: a Host with `os: posix` set by hand isn't warned (that's a choice). (c) **The login probe is `exit 0` for every Host** (setup's "already works?", `roster check`, `host.check`'s `auth` step): the same in sh, busybox, cmd.exe and PowerShell; `true` failed under PowerShell on a real Host, so a working key login looked refused. (d) **What runs on a Windows Host:** roster never relies on the Host's DefaultShell (field: one real Host's is PowerShell, another's is unset, so cmd.exe, where PowerShell syntax fails as "'Select-String' is not recognized"); it always launches `powershell -NoProfile -NonInteractive -EncodedCommand <B64>`, `<B64>` a fixed bootstrap that reads the real script's bytes from stdin and decodes them as UTF-8 whatever the Host's code page (`[Console]::OpenStandardInput()`), then runs it: base64 is the same under cmd.exe, PowerShell and a Box's ssh.cmd, so roster needn't know the Host's DefaultShell (the line is under 200 characters, far below cmd's 8191; the stand-in Windows Host runs every test under both, refusing a line either shell would read otherwise); nothing on the Host needs an OpenSSH newer than 8.1 (field: Win32-OpenSSH 8.1 and 9.5) or more than Windows PowerShell 5.1; the key line and tag are PowerShell single-quoted literals in the script, never in argv, and only printable ASCII is let in (a byte a code page reads as a Unicode quote could end a literal; the `.pub`'s own comment never goes to the Host); a password is only paramiko's login, never in the script. Key Hosts (revoke, check) use `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ControlPath=none`. (e) **(agent) Admin detection:** SID S-1-5-32-544 in `whoami /groups /fo csv /nh` in any state (deny-only too, as a filtered token is still an admin's to sshd), not `IsInRole` (false for a filtered token) or `Get-LocalGroupMember` (fails on a domain controller). (f) **(agent) Following sshd_config:** the stock top-level `AuthorizedKeysFile .ssh/authorized_keys` and one plain path under `Match Group administrators` (`__PROGRAMDATA__` expanded) are followed; any other `AuthorizedKeysFile` (a different top-level one, one under another `Match`, several paths, %-tokens) an `Include` (roster doesn't follow included files), a relative path under the `Match`, or a missing `sshd_config` is refused with error 157, never guessed. An admin with the `Match` block uses that file; everyone else (and an admin with the block commented out) `%USERPROFILE%\.ssh\authorized_keys`. (g) **(agent) The edit is byte-for-byte:** the file is read as bytes (Latin-1, one char per byte) so a UTF-8 comment or any other line is written back unchanged; only lines ending in this Box's tag (`tag_re`, case-sensitive, trailing whitespace allowed) are dropped, the new line added with the file's own line ends (CRLF for a new file, as Microsoft's `Add-Content` writes); unchanged content isn't rewritten. Written to a temp file beside it with a random name, made with `FileMode.CreateNew` (one already there is never opened), which gets its final ACL and owner (and is read back) while still empty; only then are the keys written into it, and it replaces the file (`File.Replace`, which keeps the replaced file's ACL) or is moved into place (`File.Move`). A UTF-16 (BOM or NUL bytes) or UTF-8-BOM file is refused (157), never re-encoded: re-encoding would turn on keys sshd ignores today. (h) **(agent) ACLs only narrow, and never re-trust a file sshd was ignoring (the security review's B1):** sshd ignores a key file others can write, so narrowing such a file's ACL would turn on any key someone planted in it, for every administrator. So before anything is written, an existing admin file is refused (157) and left exactly as it is when any SID other than SYSTEM, Administrators or this account has a write-class right (write, append, modify, full control, delete, change permissions, take ownership, or a generic-write number) or its owner isn't SYSTEM or Administrators; read-only entries (the folder's inherited Authenticated Users read) are fine to narrow. The key folder (`%ProgramData%\ssh`, or `%USERPROFILE%\.ssh`) is refused the same way when others can write in it, since they could swap or seed the temp file. Otherwise the admin file (its temp file first, then the final file every time setup or revoke writes it, and when setup finds it unchanged) gets `icacls /setowner *S-1-5-32-544`, `/inheritance:r /grant:r *S-1-5-32-544:F /grant:r *S-1-5-18:F`, then `/remove:g` for any other SID with an allow entry, then is read back with `Get-Acl` by SID (only those two, both FullControl, no deny for either, inheritance off, owned by Administrators or SYSTEM) and refused (157) if not. A user file roster creates gets this account, SYSTEM and Administrators, inheritance off, on its temp file before any key is in it, read back by SID (review F3). An existing user file's ACL is never changed: other SIDs that can write it, an owner other than this account, SYSTEM or Administrators, or a deny for this account or SYSTEM is a warning (157) on setup's `ok` line and from `check`, since sshd silently ignores such a file; others who can only read it aren't reported (sshd minds writers). A missing admin file is the usual case on a new Host (field), so creating it is the normal path. A new file's ACL is set on its temp file while it's empty, so the key file is never in place with any other ACL. (i) **(agent) Revoke** cleans the user file always and the admin file when the account is an administrator, so a key left after a group change goes too. An account no longer in Administrators can't open the admin file, where a key it added as an administrator may still log in as every administrator (review F4): revoke warns (157, on stderr) and says to have an administrator remove the line or run revoke as one. **A key in the admin file logs in as every administrator on that Host** (Microsoft's design, review F4): setup's `ok` line and `check` say so, and adding-hosts.md does. (j) **(agent) `roster check` on a Windows Host whose key works** runs the same script read-only and prints what it found (no roster key in the file, a wrong ACL) as `warning: [157]` on stderr, never a failure (the login works) and not in `--json` (its shape is unchanged); a failed key login runs nothing more, since check never reads the vault. (k) **ControlMaster:** `os: windows` Hosts get `ControlMaster no` with no ControlPath or ControlPersist in this Box's ssh config; `host.check` (and `roster check HOST`) maps "No existing session", "mux_client_request_session" and "Session open refused" to code 158 instead of a login failure. The table `roster check` already logs in with `ControlPath=none`. No app catalog row: the app's `other` row shows serve's message and fix (a follow-up). (l) **(agent) Wrong `os:` either way:** an sh script that fails with cmd's or PowerShell's "'sh' is not recognized" is error 155 (set `os: windows`); an `os: windows` Host whose shell says "powershell: not found", or whose `$env:OS` isn't `Windows_NT`, is 156, before anything is touched. (m) **(agent) Output:** setup's `ok` line is followed by the key file it wrote and any warning; revoke's `FAIL` lines now say why for every Host (they said nothing). Everything a Windows Host's script says is printed with its control characters replaced (review F2), so a Host can't send terminal escapes. (n) **(agent) No `API_VERSION` bump:** the Host gains `os` (`hosts.list`, `host.get`), an added field the app ignores. (o) **(agent) discover** still writes `enroll: false` for the Windows Hosts it finds (a person reviews them); the review hint is T196's. (p) **Tests:** a stand-in Windows Host (`tests/fakes/ssh`, a `windows` marker) runs the decoded PowerShell with pwsh (Linux CI's image has it) or powershell.exe (Windows CI), with stand-in `whoami.exe`, `icacls.exe` and `Get-Acl` defined as PowerShell functions (`tests/fakes/windows/acl.py` keeps the ACLs), and a login test that fails until roster's key is in the file sshd would read with the ACL it needs; they skip where there's no PowerShell. **Security review (2026-11-03), fixed in this PR:** B1 (above, (h)), F1 (UTF-8 stdin, ASCII-only values), F2 (control characters), F3 (deny entries, the new user file read back, read-only others not reported), F4 (every administrator; revoke's warning), F5 (`Include`, relative paths), and the trailing-whitespace nit. **For the owner to check on a real Windows Host before merge:** admin and plain accounts; DefaultShell cmd and PowerShell; the stdin bootstrap under sshd; that icacls takes `/setowner` and two `/grant:r` in one call; that an elevated admin can create and fill the temp file once its ACL is set; that ReplaceFile keeps the DACL; that `Get-Acl` reports `FullControl` for `*SID:F`; that the admin's ssh session can write `%ProgramData%\ssh`; CRLF accepted; what `check` says for a wrong ACL; and that a stock `%ProgramData%\ssh` and profile `.ssh` pass the folder check. |
| Q175 | Whose Mesh it is: the Box (and Inventory) in the Mesh's tags and headers (T199, #379; follows Q173 (c)) | **The owner authorised the ticket (2026-11-03, the issue body as the spec); the calls marked (agent) are the agent's, for the owner's review.** (a) **The owner:** `BOX` as the Box key's tag names it (`roster-managed:BOX`: `box_name:` or the hostname), and `BOX/NAME` for a named Inventory (agent: `/` can't be in a Box name, and `@`, `/` are outside the old tag's characters, so an older roster's patterns never match a new line and keep it). Every `authorized_keys` line is `roster-mesh:SOURCE@OWNER`, a new Mesh key's comment the same, the Mesh `config`'s first line `# Written by roster mesh for SOURCE (owner OWNER) from FILE: …` and `ROSTER.md`'s `<!-- roster-mesh: written by roster for SOURCE (owner OWNER); … -->`. A source directory's owner is its `config` header's, else its key's comment. A reinstalled Box with the same name is the same owner, as for its key. (b) **Only this owner's entries:** apply, `--dry-run`, `check`, a cut and `--rotate` add, replace and remove only lines tagged with this owner, and a Host that stops being a source loses its Mesh files, `Include`, `ROSTER.md` and import only when they're this owner's; another Box's or Inventory's stay (the L1/L3 repro of #372's review, and two Inventories on one Box). (c) **Two owners, one source Host (agent):** the source's directory is one per Host (one key, one `config`), so the second owner never writes or replaces another's: it uses the key as it is (its own tagged lines on the peers), and when the other's files say the same peers (same body below the header) nothing changes; when they say others, the Host is pending with `its Mesh files (…) are another Box's or Inventory's (OWNER), and say other peers (config, …): roster leaves them alone`, and `--rotate` of that key is refused there. Per-owner directories were the alternative: they change every path, `mesh_dir:` and ESXi's datastore pick, beyond the issue. (d) **Untagged entries (older roster) are unknown owner:** kept by apply, cut and rotate, listed by `--find-leftovers` (`…, with no Box in its tag`), removable by `--remove-leftovers` and `cut --all` behind their questions. (e) **The proof for the one-time re-tag (agent):** the Mesh record now keeps `owner`, the owner its entries carry; a record without it is from before this change. On a Host the record's `members` names, the next apply replaces an untagged line for a source it writes there with its own line (`tag key from SOURCE as this Box's`), and rewrites an untagged source directory it writes as a source. It never removes untagged Mesh files on a Host that isn't a source here: `members` doesn't say which Box made that Host a source, and an old L1's files with L3 upgraded first is exactly #379's case; those stay, for `--find-leftovers`. `owner` is written after a complete apply (no Host pending), so a partial one finishes the re-tag next time. The same proof covers a changed owner (`box_name:` changed, or the Inventory named, `inv add` moving `mesh.json`): lines and files under the recorded earlier owner, on recorded Hosts, are this Box's, so they're re-tagged or, when no longer asked for, removed. (f) **A cut (agent):** removes this owner's lines for the Host, and the proven ones of (e) (a security cut right after upgrading must still take the key off); an untagged line it can't prove is left and warned about (`… with no Box in its tag is still there …`, naming `--find-leftovers`). (g) **Leftovers:** `--find-leftovers` lists this Box's own entries roster.yml no longer asks for and untagged ones, never another Box's or Inventory's (their tag says whose); on a Host that is a source here only untagged files. The same-Box refusal of Q173 (b) now applies only when an untagged entry is among those found. `cut --all` takes this owner's and untagged entries, and its question says another Box's or Inventory's tagged entries stay: uninstalling with named Inventories is `roster --inv NAME mesh cut --all` for each. (h) **Upgrading:** every Box's first `roster mesh` after this release re-tags (each Host changes once); `roster check` reports the re-tag as drift until then. An older roster still running on another Box leaves tagged `authorized_keys` lines alone (they don't match its pattern) but still removes untagged lines, and any Mesh files on a Host it doesn't make a source (it can't tell whose they are), as before; so upgrade the Boxes that share Hosts together. No serve API change: the plan's result keeps its shape (new change texts only). |
| Q22 | Build order | A thin slice first, then test it, then the next iteration. See [roadmap.md](roadmap.md). |
